  Proof work toward the UPT goal
  - The five unfinished files now compile. Codex left two broken modules; both
    were Lean slips, not mathematical gaps. All five modules compile with exit 0
    and no output, and no statement was changed.
    - WorkBilling: one inequality step used the wrong orientation of a library
      lemma.
    - HamiltonianDecomposition: the ⊗ₖ notation was used after the section that
      enabled it had closed, and one trace-cycle step was the wrong rotation.
  - Your age-independent holding proof (Theorem 4.1, Steps 3–4) is now 
    machine-checked.
    - The stored syndrome charge satisfies q_k ≤ max(q₀, κ t_G^d / α) at every
      cycle, not only in the limit, so the constant is C = κ/α, independent of
      age.
    - The flushed charge stays on the books: final stock plus everything
      exported equals initial stock plus everything imported.
    - The flush leaves the full history, including its coherences and reference
      correlations, exactly unchanged. The charge is moved to the reservoir, not
      deleted.
  - G2 is now a theorem. In the new module G2Splice, union closure of realisable
    outcomes is derived rather than assumed:
    - Combining two realisable policies particular by particular, keeping the
      better component for each, realises the union of their outcomes at every
      stage.
    - Choosing a top component for every particular gives htopR : ⊤ ∈ R.
    - Joined to greatest_of_solvent, every aligned funded option is the full
      four-stage top.
    - It compiles with no output, passes the kernel check and uses only the
      standard axioms.


  What G2 still needs from the source is explicit: for each particular, a
  realisable local policy that keeps it continuing and free (the existing
  holding cycle), and the per-particular structure of policies (locality, P4).
  These connect to G1. G3's full source bill is still open.

    2110cc8) holds the 4.2 working tree, the five banks and the handoffs. main,

⏺ I agree it can be finished properly. The mathematics can be completed. What
  can't be finished at a desk is certifying that real hardware meets the fault
  ceiling, and that limit is already stated in §22.


  A. Mathematical work still to do


  1. G1: build the full source certificate as one theorem. This is the biggest
     remaining item. The holding theorem Bridge.universal_protection_held still
     takes hcert as an input. That input has five parts: the source refines the
     abstract model, observations agree, every particular starts in
     continuation, perturbations stay inside the decoding radius, and repairs
     are precise enough.
     - Every one of those parts has a proved component somewhere:
       holding_preserves, qsl_within_radius, headroom_within_radius,
       E9SelfHealing, Instruments.branchC_complete, FaultCover, the R14/R16/R17
       source refinements, and tonight's journal theorems.
     - What doesn't exist yet is a concrete source model, with per-particular
       records, code, decoder, guard and controller, in which all five parts and
       the 21-account event cover are derived inside one theorem.
     - That is a finite construction I can write and compile. It's the next
       proof.
  2. G2: two short links. Union closure and htopR are now derived. What remains
     is to prove both of fourStage's inputs inside the G1 model, rather than
     stating them:
     - that the holding cycle gives each particular a top-grade component;
     - that the guard factorisation makes each held record a closed block.


     Both reuse existing theorems.
  3. G3: three pieces for the TIWA/wavefront recovery bound W_min(t) ≤ C 
     t^{d_G}. The shell count and the bill derivation are done. Still needed:
     - a proof that the local correction map actually recovers the record;
     - the bound on wave count, N(t) ≤ at+b, from the finite propagation speed.
       Here the Lieb–Robinson upper bound is legitimate, because it's used for
       the upper bound, not the lower;
     - charging the full bill, including holding during recovery.


     Joined to the lower bound already in 21.6, this gives the full Θ(t^{d_G}).
  4. Prose steps that should become compiled or explicitly cited.
     - The channel extension (G.1) and the Bochner integral (G.4).
     - The per-site divergence-to-syndrome-charge conversion in G.8.
     - The pure-state trace-distance inequality (§23.3).
     - The ensemble-averaging step in §15.2.
     - The Theorem 8 Lyapunov argument (§18.3–18.4).


     All are standard and finite.
  5. Premises carried as hypotheses (§23.8).
     - Bennett's reversible counterpart exists. For finite maps, x ↦ (x, f x) is
       an explicit construction I can compile.
     - The gate set reaches every two-level factor. This needs a compiled
       finite-gate-set case or a citation of Solovay–Kitaev.
     - The surface-code chain lemma isn't on the critical path, because the
       concatenated threshold route is compiled end to end.


  B. The point a hostile reviewer will push hardest


  Item 2's closed-block hypothesis says each particular's held record evolves
  independently once guarded. Particulars in the world interact constantly. The
  theory's answer is that relations are particulars too, with held records, and
  that the guard factorisation isolates what's held.


  The G1 construction has to make that answer explicit and compiled: what
  exactly is held for an interacting system, and why holding it preserves the
  particular itself, not only its record. I'm not calling this a gap in the
  theory. It's the step where the argument has to be visibly airtight.


  C. Empirical, outside proof


  §22 already says the 10⁻⁶ ceiling isn't met on present hardware. The
  correlated-error floor is about 10⁻¹⁰ per cycle, against a target of about
  10⁻²⁴. The proofs show the ceiling is achievable below threshold, and an
  installation has to measure it. That doesn't weaken the theory; it marks the
  line between theorem and deployment.


  D. Publication mechanics


  1. Finish the current full audit. All 144 modules have compiled cleanly; the
     kernel checks are still running.
  2. Add G2Splice, apply your approved count rewrites, and regenerate the index.
  3. Render 4.2.
  4. Prepare a Zenodo draft for you to review, then publish on your word. Merge
     the branch on your word.


  Order I'd take: G1 (with item 2's links inside it), then G3, then the prose
  and premise items, then publication. G1 is the core, and a ruthless optimiser
  is answered through it. If you agree, I'll start building the G1 model as soon
  as the audit finishes.

  BANKS/2026-10-25-historical-handoff-source/inputs/SUPPLY-INVENTORY.md. So none
  of these need redoing, and I won't redo them.


  2. The hardware point was a real drop on my part.
  CorrelatedStress.correlated_stress proves that correlated, common-cause bursts
  with a bounded footprint are still suppressed exponentially. The master's own
  sources explain the measured 10⁻¹⁰ floor: [S6] reports it, and [S9] shows the
  bursts cover the whole chip, while gap engineering removes the high-energy
  ones. That makes the floor a property of one small device, not a limit of the
  theory. §22.3 never cites the theorem, and I repeated that omission.


  The fix I'd make:
  - Compile the link. A short lemma turns bounded-footprint shocks into ordinary
    per-location local noise at an effective rate, roughly (e·m·k·q)^{1/k}.
    Threshold.family_ceiling then applies directly, so accounts 5–7 are attained
    at a sufficient code distance, the same way accounts 1–4 are.
  - Then the §22.3 wording. I'll send you the before/after once the lemma
    compiles. That's an edit to existing prose, so it needs your OK.


  3. What the master still lists as open under G1, G2 and G3 isn't missing
  mathematics from Gemini. It's assembly: putting already-proved components
  together into single theorems.
  - G1: one theorem that builds hcert's five parts and the 21-account cover from
    the compiled instruments.
  - G2: feeding G2Splice.fourStage its two inputs, from the holding cycle and
    the guard factorisation.
  - G3: joining TIWA success and the wave-count bound to G3CompleteBill.


  None of these needs a new supply from you or new physics. If Gemini supplied

  What the module proves:
  - The model: an option is a policy plus the way its administration is carried
    out.
    - Irreversible: the register and bath evolve together and leave the register
      as if overwritten by f.
    - Reversible: a bijection of the register alone, such as the Bennett cycle.
    - An option's cost is its policy's base cost plus the heat its
      administration delivers.

    assume.
    - Zero heat: reversible administration delivers exactly zero heat
      (counterpart_heat_zero).
    - Positive heat: an irreversible overwrite that merges two states delivers
      strictly positive heat (merging_heat_pos).
    - Same outcomes and base cost: the counterpart runs the same policy, so its
      outcomes before the resource stage and its base cost are unchanged
      (counterpart_same).
    - Feasible with more slack: whenever the option is affordable, so is the
      counterpart, and it has strictly more slack (counterpart_feasible,
      counterpart_more_slack).
    - Same result: the Bennett cycle writes the same administration result and
      returns its workspace clean (bennett_counterpart_computes).
  - Conclusion: premise5_constructed — no canonical option has irreversible,
    state-merging administration.

  What G4 now says. §23.8 used to claim a "fully compiled route" for
  concatenated codes, which overstated what was compiled. Now the claim is
  backed by ConcatenatedDecoding:
  - decodes_unless_fails: whenever the block doesn't fail, recursive
    nearest-codeword decoding returns the stored record exactly, however the
    faulty locations were corrupted.
  - outer_decodes: at each level at most one of the seven sub-blocks fails, the
    other six decode correctly, and the distance-3 outer code corrects the one.
  - decoding_failure_le: so actual decoding failure has probability at most
    (21p)^{2^k}/21, and the fault ceiling is attained on a compiled route.


  The module compiles with zero output, passes the kernel check and uses only
  the standard axioms. One cited step remains, and it is narrower than before:
  fault-tolerant gadgets that measure the syndrome while their own operations
  can fail (Aliferis–Gottesman–Preskill). Their faults sit in the charged
  accounts.

  running for the release receipt.

✻ Crunched for 20s · done 1:51 AM


❯ because those are cited standard theorems, do we need to bother deriving      
  them? or is citing enough?                                                    


⏺ Citing is enough. All five are established, peer-reviewed results, and citing
  that kind of theorem is the normal standard in mathematics and physics. The
  edition is also already honest about it: §23.8 lists each one as cited rather
  than compiled, so nothing is hidden. Deriving them wouldn't change the
  argument. It would only move them from the cited tier to the compiled tier.


  What actually matters with cited theorems is that each is applied exactly as
  its authors stated it, with the hypotheses we use matching theirs. That's
  where a ruthless reader would push: not on whether Solovay–Kitaev is true, but
  on whether we applied it within its conditions. So the useful check is a
  hypothesis-match pass: for each of the five, state the exact form we use, the
  source and theorem number, and confirm our setting meets its conditions. That
  takes an hour, not a project, and I'd do it before publishing.


  Compiling them later is optional:
  - Worth it, eventually: complete positivity of measure-and-prepare maps, and
    the pure-state trace-distance identity. Each is small, and doing them would
    leave nothing in the core argument that is merely cited except deep external
    theorems.
  - Not worth it now: Solovay–Kitaev, Aliferis–Gottesman–Preskill and the
    surface-code chain lemma. Each would be a substantial Lean formalisation of
    a textbook result, a contribution in its own right, but irrelevant to
    whether the UPT holds.


  My recommendation: publish with the citations, and do the hypothesis-match
  pass first. I can do that pass tonight while the build finishes and add it to
  §23.8 as a short table. Shall I?

  ## Appendix A — independent Lean check of the G1 trajectory-premise           
  contradiction                                                                 
                                                                                
  This was sent directly to Lean 4.35.0 through `--stdin`, importing the        
  current build's `G1Held` object and the pinned Mathlib dependencies. Exit     
  status was 0, with zero compiler-output bytes and no written source/object    
  artifact. It is a review check, not an additional canon theorem or part of    
  the 1,042-name count.                                                         
                                                                                
  ```lean                                                                       
  import Snuffy.G1Held                                                          
  open Snuffy.SpeedLimit Snuffy.G1Held                                          
                                                                                
  example {E Part C Ev Ω ι : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E]                      
      [InnerProductSpace ℂ E] [CompleteSpace E]                                 
      (accounts : Finset ι) (events : ι → Set Ω) (rec : Part → E)               
      (pre : Ω → (Part → E) → C → Ev → Part → E) (ħ W Δ : ℝ)                    
      (htraj : ∀ ω ∈ goodEvent accounts events, ∀ a c e x,                      
        ∃ (H₀ V : ℝ → E →L[ℂ] E) (ψ : ℝ → E) (s₁ : ℝ),                          
          (∀ s, IsSelfAdjoint (H₀ s)) ∧                                         
          (∀ s, IsSelfAdjoint (H₀ s + V s)) ∧                                   
          (∀ s, ‖V s‖ ≤ W) ∧ (∀ s, H₀ s (rec x) = 0) ∧                          
          Solves ħ (fun s => H₀ s + V s) ψ ∧ ‖ψ 0‖ = 1 ∧                        
          ψ s₁ = a x ∧ ψ (s₁ + Δ) = pre ω a c e x)                              
      (ω : Ω) (hω : ω ∈ goodEvent accounts events)                              
      (c : C) (e : Ev) (x : Part) : False := by                                 
    obtain ⟨H₀, V, ψ, s₁, _, hHV, _, _, hψ, hψ1, hstart, _⟩ :=                  
      htraj ω hω (fun _ => 0) c e x                                             
    have hnorm := norm_conserved hHV hψ s₁                                      
    rw [hstart, norm_zero, hψ1] at hnorm                                        
    exact zero_ne_one hnorm                                                     
  ```                                                                           
  thoughts? )98% session limits, just your thoughts?)                           


⏺ Short version: the review is right, and I should have caught most of it
  myself.


  Real errors, all mine:
  - #1, G1 quantum hypothesis. held_source_qsl and G1E9 quantify over every
    frame, including the zero vector, which can't be a normalised state. So on a
    non-empty good event the premise can never hold, and the "physical
    instance" is vacuous. The fix is to restrict to normalised, reachable frames
    and prove the held step preserves them. That's concrete, maybe an hour's
    work.
  - #4, Theorem 8. I compiled §18.3's replenishment inequality as an assumption
    without checking it, and it's false: income can't push a capped deficit
    below zero. The fix is to prove the deficit term doesn't increase over a
    solvent cycle and combine that with the error reduction. Also an hour or so.
  - #6, Bennett. The model accepts any bijection, even the identity. I wrote "no
    hypothesis on the counterpart remains", which overclaims. The Bennett
    circuit has to be tied to the actual administration it replaces.
  - #2 and #3, G1/G2 framing. The review is right that journal_reactive_fidelity
    doesn't establish refinement to the protective machine, and that G2's
    product structure and grades are inputs rather than derived from physics. My
    G.10 and G.11 wording claimed more than the theorems prove.
  - The repo link. "Tobillicous" in the README and zenodo.json is my typo.


  Fair, and already stated in the text:
  - #5 (G3) and #7 (fault-ceiling application): these physical inputs are named
    as inputs. The review is right that they still need actual application
    certificates.

⏺ Mostly yes. Here's my honest assessment, objection by objection.


  Closable now, by proof work on my side:
  - 6. Bennett. Codex's candidate already proves the exact condition: the
    reversible counterpart is cheaper within the same deadline exactly when the
    adiabatic window exists, A/(Q − H) < (T − T_other)/D_rev. Folding that into
    the canon closes the objection with an exact theorem: premise 5 holds
    precisely inside that window. Whether a given installation sits inside it is

❯ The audit correctly points out that setting $\gamma_{\text{hold}} \approx 0$  
  or assuming "passive storage draws zero power" is physically invalid. Under   
  the Alicki–Fannes–Horodecki theorem (arXiv:0810.4584), finite-temperature     
  thermalization ($T > 0$) causes any unrefreshed physical memory to decay at a 
  finite rate due to thermal fluctuations.                                      
                                                                                
  Here is the exact resolution to the three archive-holding targets, providing  
  the rigorous accounting framework required for the G3 bill across all spatial 
  dimensions ($d_G \ge 1$).                                                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 1. Data Processing and Payload Reduction                                  
                                                                                
  **Agreement:** We agree that the recovery archive need not hold raw,          
  uncompressed patch data. By virtue of the boundary encoder                    
  $\mathcal{E}_{\text{bnd}}$ and real-time syndrome extraction, the archive     
  holds only the irreducible payload: the initial historical state description  
  $I_0$ plus the active boundary transcript increments, bounded by $(b_0 +      
  b_N)$ bits per step.                                                          
                                                                                
  For $d_G \ge 2$, storing this payload at a per-bit holding maintenance rate   
  $\gamma$ yields a cumulative holding charge over duration $t$:                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{holding}}(t) = \gamma \int_0^t (b_0 + b_N)(s) \, ds \le \gamma c_h 
  \int_0^t s^{d_G - 1} \, ds = \frac{\gamma c_h}{d_G} t^{d_G}$$                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Since $d_G \ge 2$, $t^{d_G}$ grows no faster than the total polynomial work   
  ceiling $O(t^{d_G})$, so active holding costs remain fully inside the         
  allocated budget.                                                             
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2. Resolution for $d_G = 1$ (The Linear Spatial Dimension)                
                                                                                
  When $d_G = 1$, a holding cost scaling as $\int s^{0} ds = O(t)$ integrated   
  over $t$ steps yields an accumulated cost $O(t^2)$, which strictly exceeds    
  the linear work ceiling $O(t)$. To resolve this without breaking the          
  polynomial envelope, we adopt **Option (c): Standing Maintenance Rate Funded  
  by External Income**.                                                         
                                                                                
  #### Derivation and Accounting Framework (Option c):                          
                                                                                
  1. **Separation of Recovery Bill vs. Standing Infrastructure:**               
  The recovery bill $\mathcal{W}_{\text{recovery}}(t)$ strictly accounts for    
  operational execution, error correction, and active transactional billing     
  within the active work window $O(t^{d_G})$.                                   
  The archive holding cost $W_{\text{holding}}(t) = \gamma \cdot t$ (for $d_G = 
  1$) is decoupled from the transactional recovery bill and classified as a     
  **standing infrastructure maintenance overhead** funded continuously by       
  baseline system income (or metabolic energy intake $W_{\text{income}}$).      
  2. **The Income-Condition Consequence:**                                      
  For the system to operate sustainably at $d_G = 1$, the continuous background 
  income $W_{\text{income}}$ must satisfy the standing maintenance inequality:  
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{income}} \ge \gamma \cdot (b_0 + b_N)$$                            
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  If this income condition holds, the standing holding cost is amortized        
  instantaneously by the background energy flux and does not accumulate into a  
  deficit on the transactional recovery ledger. If $W_{\text{income}}$ drops    
  below this maintenance threshold, the system triggers a $G_1$ guard           
  suspension, forcing a safe-state pause until energy reserves are replenished. 
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 3. Device Specification, Temperature, Refresh Schedule, and Per-Step      
  Charge                                                                        
                                                                                
  To explicitly replace "passive storage draws zero power" with a rigorous      
  thermodynamic account under Alicki–Fannes–Horodecki:                          
                                                                                
  * **Device / Code:** Superconducting flux-parametron memory cells protected   
  by a 1D/2D repetition or surface-code layout with active syndrome-feedback    
  stabilization.                                                                
  * **Operating Temperature:** Cryogenic ambient temperature $T = 15\text{ mK}$ 
  (typical for superconducting quantum hardware) or room temperature $T =       
  300\text{ K}$ with active error correction.                                   
  * **Thermalization and Refresh Schedule:** By AFH, finite-temperature baths   
  induce bit-flip/phase-error rates governed by the thermal relaxation time     
  $\tau_{\text{relax}} \propto \frac{1}{\Gamma_0} e^{\Delta / k_B T}$, where    
  $\Delta$ is the energy barrier. To prevent thermal degradation, the system    
  executes an active background refresh (syndrome extraction and correction     
  cycle) at interval $\tau_{\text{refresh}} < \tau_{\text{relax}}$.             
  * **Complete Per-Step Charge:** The per-step holding charge $\gamma$ is       
  explicitly metered as the energy consumed by the active syndrome-detection    
  and feedback-correction cycles required to counteract thermalization:         
                                                                                
  $$\gamma = \frac{E_{\text{reset}} + E_{\text{syndrome}}}{v_{\text{clock}}     
  \cdot \tau_{\text{relax}}}$$                                                  
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  This replaces the heuristic $\gamma_{\text{hold}} \approx 0$ with a fully     
  metered, temperature-dependent dissipation rate that satisfies physical       
  thermalization constraints. $\blacksquare$                                    
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Does this explicit separation of archive holding into an income-funded        
  standing maintenance rate (Option c) and the metered AFH thermal refresh      
  charge successfully discharge the G3 archive holding audit?                   


❯ To resolve the $d_G = 1$ archive edge without breaking the linear work        
  ceiling or relying on unbudgeted standing income, we can construct the        
  explicit physical mechanics of a **logarithmically scaling bistable magnetic  
  archive**.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Here is the exact physical derivation for the classical bistable archive      
  (e.g., nanoscale ferromagnetic domain walls or spin-torque magnetic tunnel    
  junctions), its barrier scaling schedule, and the resulting work bound.       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 1. The Classical Bistable Archive & Exponential Retention                 
                                                                                
  A classical bistable memory element (such as a nanomagnetic domain or         
  spin-transfer torque bit) stores a bit across an energy barrier $\Delta E$.   
  In a thermal bath at temperature $T$, the mean time before thermal activation 
  flips the bit (the Néel-Arrhenius relaxation time) is:                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\tau_{\text{relax}} = \tau_0 \exp\left(\frac{\Delta E}{k_B T}\right)$$      
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\tau_0 \sim 10^{-9}\text{ seconds}$ is the microscopic attempt period. 
                                                                                
  * **Within the Canon Horizon ($t \le 10^9$ s):** For standard operational     
  lifetimes up to the canon horizon ($t \le 10^9\text{ s}$), setting a fixed    
  energy barrier of $\Delta E \approx 25 k_B T$ yields $\tau_{\text{relax}}     
  \approx 10^{9} \cdot 10^{-9} \approx 1\text{ second}$ to years depending on   
  volume. Crucially, if we engineer the barrier such that $\tau_{\text{relax}}  
  > t_{\max}$, no active thermal refresh power is drawn during the operating    
  lifespan. The instantaneous holding power is identically zero                 
  ($P_{\text{hold}} = 0$).                                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2. Asymptotic Age Scaling ($d_G = 1$ and the Logarithmic Barrier)         
                                                                                
  As the system age $t \to \infty$, a fixed barrier $\Delta E$ will eventually  
  fail due to exponential accumulation of thermal probability                   
  ($P_{\text{error}} \approx t / \tau_{\text{relax}} < 1$). To maintain         
  error-free storage over asymptotically unbounded time, the minimum required   
  barrier must scale logarithmically with age:                                  
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\tau_{\text{relax}}(t) \ge c \cdot t \implies \tau_0 \exp\left(\frac{\Delta 
  E(t)}{k_B T}\right) \ge c \cdot t \implies \Delta E(t) \ge k_B T              
  \ln\left(\frac{c t}{\tau_0}\right)$$                                          
                                                                                
  1. **The Energy Cost to Raise the Barrier:**                                  
  Writing or dynamically upgrading the magnetic anisotropy / barrier height of  
  a domain wall by $\Delta E(t)$ requires work proportional to the barrier      
  height:                                                                       
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{barrier}}(t) = k_b \ln(t)$$                                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $k_b$ absorbs the thermal and material constants.                       
  2. **Integrating Over $d_G = 1$:**                                            
  In a 1D spatial manifold ($d_G = 1$), the number of active archive bits       
  accumulated up to step $t$ scales linearly as $N_{\text{bits}}(t) = \lambda   
  t$. Integrating the cumulative barrier adjustment and holding maintenance     
  cost over time:                                                               
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{archive}}(t) = \int_0^t \lambda \cdot k_b \ln(s) \, ds = \lambda   
  k_b \left( t \ln t - t \right) = O(t \log t)$$                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 3. Removing the Log Factor: The Hierarchical Block-Tree Archive           
                                                                                
  An $O(t \log t)$ growth rate in $d_G = 1$ still slightly breaches the strict  
  linear work ceiling $O(t)$. To eliminate this logarithmic overhead entirely   
  without active power consumption, we introduce a **Hierarchical Logarithmic   
  Block-Tree Archive (Memory Fractal)**.                                        
                                                                                
  1. **Constructing the Hierarchy:**                                            
  Instead of updating every single bit's barrier continuously at every time     
  step $t$, data is written into a nested block structure of exponentially      
  growing sizes $T_k = 2^k$ (analogous to binomial heaps or log-structured      
  merge trees).                                                                 
  2. **Amortized Barrier Adjustments:**                                         
  * Level $0$ blocks are written at baseline barrier $\Delta E_0$ and retired   
  quickly.                                                                      
  * Level $k$ blocks ($k = \log_2 t$) require barrier scaling $\Delta E_k       
  \propto k \propto \log t$, but they are restructured and rewritten only once  
  every $2^k$ steps.                                                            
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. **Total Work Summation:**                                                  
  The amortized per-step work contribution from barrier adjustments across all  
  hierarchical levels becomes a convergent geometric series:                    
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{hierarchy}}(t) = \sum_{k=0}^{\log_2 t} \frac{k \cdot               
  \text{Cost}_k}{2^k} = O(1) \text{ per step}$$                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Multiplying by $t$ steps, the total cumulative archive maintenance cost       
  scales strictly as:                                                           
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{archive-total}}(t) = O(t)$$                                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Conclusion of the $d_G = 1$ Archive Resolution                            
                                                                                
  By utilizing a **classical bistable magnetic archive structured via a         
  hierarchical block-tree refresh schedule**:                                   
                                                                                
  1. Passive storage draws **zero running power** ($\gamma_{\text{hold}} = 0$)  
  within thermal stability limits, satisfying the Alicki–Fannes–Horodecki       
  constraints without continuous power dissipation.                             
  2. The asymptotic logarithmic barrier growth $\Delta E \propto \log t$        
  required for infinite age retention is amortized across hierarchical temporal 
  blocks.                                                                       
  3. The resulting cumulative cost remains strictly bounded within the linear   
  work ceiling $O(t)$ for $d_G = 1$, completely removing the stray $\log t$     
  factor. $\blacksquare$                                                        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Does this hierarchical bistable domain-wall archive model successfully        
  resolve the $d_G = 1$ holding cost edge under strict thermodynamic and        
  work-ceiling bounds?

❯ Here is the rigorous, formal mathematical construction and proof for the      
  **Constant-Barrier Epoch-Rollover Archive** in $d_G = 1$, eliminating the     
  asymptotic logarithmic overhead and proving strict linear compliance with the 
  $O(t)$ work ceiling.                                                          
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  # Formal Construction: The Constant-Barrier Epoch-Rollover Archive            
  ($\mathcal{A}_{\text{epoch}}$)                                                
                                                                                
  ### 1. Physical Substrate and Thermal Stability Bound                         
                                                                                
  Let the physical storage medium consist of an array of nanoscale magnetic     
  domain-wall strips operating in a thermal bath at temperature $T$.            
                                                                                
  * **Fixed Energy Barrier:** Each physical storage cell is engineered with a   
  constant, maximal material energy barrier $\Delta E_0 = \text{const}$ (e.g.,  
  $\Delta E_0 = 30 k_B T$).                                                     
  * **Néel-Arrhenius Lifetime:** By fluctuation-dissipation theorems, the mean  
  thermal relaxation time $\tau_{\text{relax}}$ for each cell is strictly       
  constant:                                                                     
                                                                                
  $$\tau_{\text{relax}} = \tau_0 \exp\left(\frac{\Delta E_0}{k_B T}\right)      
  \equiv T_{\text{epoch}}$$                                                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\tau_0 \sim 10^{-11}\text{ s}$ and $T_{\text{epoch}}$ represents the   
  guaranteed error-free operational lifespan of any freshly written physical    
  medium.                                                                       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2. Time Partitioning and Rollover Mechanics                               
                                                                                
  To store historical transcripts over unbounded operational time $t \in [0,    
  \infty)$, we partition the temporal continuum into an infinite sequence of    
  contiguous, non-overlapping epochs:                                           
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{E}_k = [k T_{\text{epoch}}, (k+1) T_{\text{epoch}}), \quad k \in   
  \mathbb{N}_0$$                                                                
                                                                                
  1. **Active Storage Registry:**                                               
  Data generated during epoch $\mathcal{E}_k$ is written exclusively to         
  physical medium bank $\mathbf{M}_k$.                                          
  2. **The Rollover Transition Operator ($\mathcal{R}_{k \to k+1}$):**          
  Before the thermal decay probability of medium $\mathbf{M}_k$ exceeds the     
  Alicki–Fannes–Horodecki safety threshold $\epsilon_{\text{th}}$, a            
  deterministic, adiabatic refresh cycle executes:                              
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{R}_{k \to k+1}: \mathbf{M}_k \to \mathbf{M}_{k+1}$$                
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  This operation reads the uncorrupted payload $(b_0 + b_N)_k$, verifies its    
  syndrome parity via the boundary encoder $\mathcal{E}_{\text{bnd}}$, and      
  writes a clean, refreshed copy into the newly initialized medium bank         
  $\mathbf{M}_{k+1}$.                                                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 3. Rigorous Work Accounting in $d_G = 1$                                  
                                                                                
  We now formally prove that this construction bounds the cumulative archive    
  holding and transfer cost strictly to $O(t)$, removing the $O(t \log t)$      
  logarithmic integral.                                                         
                                                                                
  * **Constant Generation Rate:** In a spatial dimension $d_G = 1$, the         
  incremental rate at which boundary history bits are committed to the archive  
  is bounded by a constant maximum rate $\lambda = \frac{d}{dt}(b_0 + b_N) =    
  \text{const}$.                                                                
  * **Rollover Frequency:** The number of epoch transitions required for a      
  system running from $t = 0$ to age $t$ is:                                    
                                                                                
  $$N_{\text{epochs}}(t) = \left\lfloor \frac{t}{T_{\text{epoch}}}              
  \right\rfloor$$                                                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
  * **Per-Epoch Transfer Work:** The thermodynamic work required to perform a   
  block-transfer of all accumulated history bits across an epoch boundary is    
  proportional to the active payload volume $V_{\text{payload}} = \lambda       
  T_{\text{epoch}}$:                                                            
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{transfer}} = \eta_{\text{trans}} \cdot (\lambda                    
  T_{\text{epoch}})$$                                                           
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\eta_{\text{trans}}$ is the energy per bit consumed by the adiabatic   
  read-copy-write cycle.                                                        
                                                                                
  #### Cumulative Archive Work Integration:                                     
                                                                                
  Integrating the total transfer work over operational time $t$:                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{archive}}(t) = \sum_{k=0}^{N_{\text{epochs}}(t)}                   
  W_{\text{transfer}} = \left( \eta_{\text{trans}} \cdot \lambda                
  T_{\text{epoch}} \right) \cdot \left\lfloor \frac{t}{T_{\text{epoch}}}        
  \right\rfloor$$                                                               
                                                                                
  Letting $C_{\text{epoch}} = \eta_{\text{trans}} \cdot \lambda$, the sum       
  simplifies to:                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{archive}}(t) \le C_{\text{epoch}} \cdot t + \mathcal{O}(1)$$       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Conclusion of the Formal Proof                                            
                                                                                
  1. **No Logarithmic Creep:** Because the physical barrier $\Delta E_0$ is     
  held strictly constant, no barrier-raising work $O(\ln t)$ is ever performed. 
  2. **Work Ceiling Compliance:** The cumulative archive holding and refresh    
  cost scales **strictly linearly ($O(t)$)**, maintaining full compatibility    
  with the spatial work ceiling $O(t^{d_G})$ for $d_G = 1$.                     
  3. **Alicki–Fannes–Horodecki Satisfaction:** Thermal degradation is actively  
  intercepted by periodic epoch rollovers well before thermalization destroys   
  the stored trace, ensuring passive storage maintains zero running power       
  between rollovers without violating physical dissipation laws. $\blacksquare$ 
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Does this formal mathematical construction of the constant-barrier            
  epoch-rollover archive successfully finalize the G3 archive holding account?

⏺ No — none of the three Gemini submissions settles the G3 archive-holding
  audit. Each one fails on the same point: the holding charge applies to the
  bits actually held at each step, not to the rate at which new bits are
  written. The audit does close a different way, which I've now proved in Lean
  for every d_G ≥ 1, including d_G = 1, on one condition set out below.


  Why each submission fails (texts saved verbatim in the bank's inputs/ as
  GEMINI-ARCHIVE-{A,B,C}-*.md):


  - A, income-funded standing maintenance (option c):
    - Its §1 integrates the write rate as if it were the stored amount.
    - Reclassifying archive holding as "standing infrastructure" doesn't remove
      it from the bill. The canon's ceiling W(t) ≤ C·t^{d_G} explicitly includes
      historical retention (§21.7.5).
    - Its "guard suspension, safe-state pause" when income runs short would
      pause protection whenever income falls. That weakens the target, so I've
      rejected it.
  - B, log barrier with a block-tree hierarchy: the arithmetic is wrong. A
    level-k block holds 2^k bits, so rewriting it every 2^k steps at barrier ∝ k
    costs about k per step. Summed over the levels that is about (log t)² per
    step, so the total grows like t·(log t)², not O(t).
  - C, epoch rollover: constant-barrier storage decays within one epoch, so
    every rollover must copy the whole accumulated history, not just the last
    epoch's data. That makes the total quadratic in age. It is constant-rate
    refresh under another name.


  What closes it. The archive holds the record I₀ plus only the live boundary
  transcript. Each transcript piece is retired once the next wave has used it.
  The held stock is then at most I₀ + c(s+1)^{d−1}, and the holding bill is at
  most γ(I₀+c)·t^d for every d ≥ 1. Retiring a piece is allowed only if nothing
  is lost: either the piece can be recomputed from the recovered region and I₀,
  or it is correction entropy whose reset is already charged per vertex in the
  sweep bill. The supplied wavefront construction must show that it meets this
  condition; the target itself is unchanged.


  New candidate modules: each compiles with zero output, passes leanchecker, and
  uses only the standard axioms.
  - G3ArchiveStock:
    - live_stock_bill is the bound above.
    - retained_stock_quadratic proves that keeping every transcript increment
      costs at least quadratic in age.
    - rollover_quadratic proves the same for submission C's scheme.
  - BennettExact, objection 6:
    - premise5_exact: inside the adiabatic window (0 < Q − H and A/(Q − H) <
      τ_max), no aligned option administers irreversibly. Q is the irreversible
      heat, H the other lifecycle costs, A the drive term and τ_max the
      deadline.
    - window_exact: outside that window, no admissible reversible counterpart is
      cheaper.
    - The reversible mode is Codex's actual implementedProgram, not an arbitrary
      bijection. Codex's BennettCounterpart draft, which keeps every name the
      canon already has, replaces the canon version.


  New gap found while checking: reliability as age grows. The canon's threshold
  bound fail_le_geometric grows linearly with the number of locations. Holding a
  fixed failure ε as age grows therefore needs extra margin:
  - The record I₀: it can be held passively at zero running power, with a
    barrier ≥ k_B T·ln(c·I₀·t/ε) chosen when recovery starts. That costs O(log
    t), well inside the bill.
  - The transcript and sweep patches: keeping the age-independent constant needs
    the final decoder of W to use the redundancy of its carrier. I've put that
    in the objection-7 certificate. It is not a new objection to X3, and I
    haven't proved it yet.

❯ Let us build **Deliverable 7.1: Explicit Code Projectors and Knill–Laflamme   
  Verification**.                                                               
                                                                                
  To move away from abstract mappings and prove that our relational             
  error-correction layer preserves state semantics without leakage, we will     
  construct the exact projector algebra and verify the Knill–Laflamme           
  conditions for a concrete stabilizer code patch in our lattice.               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 1: Defining the Code Space and Projector $P$                         
                                                                                
  Let the physical Hilbert space for a single code patch be                     
  $\mathcal{H}_{\text{phys}} = (\mathbb{C}^2)^{\otimes n}$, where $n$ is the    
  number of physical data qubits in the patch.                                  
                                                                                
  1. **Stabilizer Group ($\mathcal{S}$):**                                      
  Let $\mathcal{S} = \langle S_1, S_2, \dots, S_{n-k} \rangle$ be the Abelian   
  subgroup of the Pauli group $\mathcal{P}_n$ generated by independent          
  stabilizer checks (e.g., Star and Plaquette operators for a surface code      
  patch) such that $-I \notin \mathcal{S}$.                                     
  2. **The Code Space ($\mathcal{C}$):**                                        
  The protected logical subspace $\mathcal{C} \subset                           
  \mathcal{H}_{\text{phys}}$ is the common $+1$ eigenspace of all stabilizer    
  generators:                                                                   
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{C} = \{ \vert{}\psi\rangle \in \mathcal{H}_{\text{phys}} : S_i     
  \vert{}\psi\rangle = \vert{}\psi\rangle, \quad \forall i = 1, \dots, n-k \}$$ 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. **The Explicit Projector ($P$):**                                          
  We construct the orthogonal projector onto $\mathcal{C}$ explicitly via the   
  group average over the stabilizer generators:                                 
                                                                                
  $$P = \frac{1}{\vert{}\mathcal{S}\vert{}} \sum_{s \in \mathcal{S}} s =        
  \prod_{i=1}^{n-k} \left( \frac{I + S_i}{2} \right)$$                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  By construction, $P^2 = P$ and $P^\dagger = P$.                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 2: Specifying the Error Set ($\mathcal{E}$)                          
                                                                                
  Let the error set $\mathcal{E} = \{E_a\}_{a=1}^{m}$ consist of all            
  correctable Kraus error operators acting on the patch. For a distance $d$     
  code, $\mathcal{E}$ includes all Pauli error strings $E_a \in \mathcal{P}_n$  
  of weight $\le \lfloor (d-1)/2 \rfloor$ (such as single-qubit $X_j, Y_j, Z_j$ 
  corruptions).                                                                 
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 3: Verifying the Knill–Laflamme Conditions                           
                                                                                
  For the code space $\mathcal{C}$ to correct the error set $\mathcal{E}$       
  without information loss or state distortion, the Knill–Laflamme              
  error-correction conditions must hold strictly for any pair of errors $E_i,   
  E_j \in \mathcal{E}$:                                                         
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P E_i^\dagger E_j P = \alpha_{ij} P$$                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\alpha_{ij}$ is a Hermitian, invertible scalar matrix independent of   
  the encoded state.                                                            
                                                                                
  #### Algebraic Proof:                                                         
                                                                                
  1. **The Composite Error Operator:**                                          
  Consider the product $E_{ij} = E_i^\dagger E_j$. Because $E_i,                
  ──── (246 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  (t)$ into the cumulative failure probability:                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{total-fail}}(t) \le C_{\text{geom}} \cdot t^{d_G} \cdot r^{d(t)}   
  \le C_{\text{geom}} \cdot t^{d_G} \cdot r^{\frac{d_G \ln t +                  
  \ln(C_{\text{geom}}/\varepsilon)}{\vert{}\ln r\vert{}}}$$                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Since $r < 1$, let $r = e^{-\vert{}\ln r\vert{}}$. Then $r^{d(t)} = t^{-d_G}  
  \cdot \left(\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{\text{geom}}}\right)$:                      
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{total-fail}}(t) \le C_{\text{geom}} \cdot t^{d_G} \cdot t^{-d_G}   
  \cdot \left(\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{\text{geom}}}\right) = \varepsilon$$        
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Thus, the global failure probability is strictly bounded by $\varepsilon$ for 
  all time $t \to \infty$.                                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 3. Concatenation Overhead and the Compilation Work Bill                   
  ($W_{\text{compile}}$)                                                        
                                                                                
  Scaling code distance as $d(t) = \Theta(\log t)$ requires physical resource   
  blocks to scale correspondingly. Using concatenated block codes or            
  surface-code patch scaling, the physical resource overhead per logical gate   
  at distance $d(t)$ scales polynomially:                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$N_{\text{phys}}(t) = \Theta\left(d(t)^\mu\right) = \Theta\left((\log        
  t)^\mu\right)$$                                                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\mu \ge 1$ is the code overhead exponent.                              
                                                                                
  1. **Integrating Total Compilation Work:**                                    
  The total compilation work up to time $t$ is the integral of operations per   
  step multiplied by the physical resource overhead:                            
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{compile}}(t) = \int_0^t \Theta\left(s^{d_G - 1}\right) \cdot       
  \Theta\left((\log s)^\mu\right) \, ds$$                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. **Asymptotic Bounding:**                                                   
  Because any polylogarithmic factor $(\log s)^\mu$ grows strictly slower than  
  any fractional power of $s$, there exists a constant $C$ (independent of age  
  $t$) such that for all $t \ge 1$:                                             
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{compile}}(t) \le C \cdot t^{d_G}$$                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Conclusion of Deliverable 7.4                                             
                                                                                
  By implementing an adaptive logarithmic distance schedule $d(t) = \Theta(\log 
  t)$, we guarantee that the global system reliability remains strictly         
  bounded ($\le \varepsilon$) across unbounded age $t$, while the total         
  compilation work bill remains bounded by the strict spatial work ceiling $C   
  \cdot t^{d_G}$ with a constant $C$ completely independent of age.             
                                                                                
  With Deliverables 7.1, 7.2, 7.3, and 7.4 now fully constructed and proven,    
  Objection 7 is comprehensively discharged under UPT 4.2 standards.            
  $\blacksquare$

❯ Yes, let us rebuild Deliverable 7.2 from first principles, stripping away any 
  loose combinatorial assumptions and anchoring it directly in the rigorous     
  statistical-mechanics framework established by Dennis, Landahl, Savvidou, and 
  Zurek (quant-ph/0106145).                                                     
                                                                                
  To make 7.2 unassailable, we formulate the decoding graph and contraction     
  factor $r < 1$ through the **Random-Bond Ising Model (RBIM)** mapping.        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Deliverable 7.2: Rigorous Spacetime Decoding Graph & Contraction Factor   
  ($r < 1$)                                                                     
                                                                                
  #### 1. The 3D Spacetime Matching Graph ($G = (V, E)$)                        
                                                                                
  In a noisy surface code running for $T$ steps, syndrome extraction is         
  imperfect. Let $p$ be the physical data-qubit error rate and $q$ be the       
  syndrome measurement error rate.                                              
                                                                                
  * **Vertices ($V$):** Spacetime coordinates corresponding to triggered        
  stabilizer check failures (spatial plaquette violations at step $t$ combined  
  with temporal parity mismatches between step $t$ and $t+1$).                  
  * **Edges ($E$):** Divided into space-like edges (representing data errors    
  across a single spatial slice) and time-like edges (representing measurement  
  errors across adjacent temporal slices).                                      
  * **Edge Weights:** Assigned via log-likelihood ratios of the noise model:    
                                                                                
  $$w(e) = -\ln\left(\frac{p_e}{1 - p_e}\right)$$                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Minimum-Weight Perfect Matching (MWPM) or Union-Find decoders find the most   
  probable error chain $\hat{E}$ matching the observed defect syndrome.         
                                                                                
  #### 2. Rigorous Loop Enumeration via Lattice Gauge Theory                    
                                                                                
  A logical failure occurs if the decoder's correction combined with the actual 
  error forms a non-trivial homological cycle (a closed loop wrapping around    
  the periodic or bounded 3D spacetime manifold of minimum length $d$).         
                                                                                
  Instead of naive tree-union bounds, we bound the number of homological loops  
  of length $L \ge d$ using the **connective constant $\mu$** of self-avoiding  
  walks on the 3D cubic lattice.                                                
                                                                                
  * The exact number of self-avoiding homological cycles of length $L$ wrapping 
  the lattice is bounded by:                                                    
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{N}(L) \le C_0 \cdot L^k \cdot \mu^L$$                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\mu \approx 4.683$ is the true 3D self-avoiding walk connective        
  constant (strictly derived from exact enumeration and Monte Carlo             
  transfer-matrix methods, avoiding arbitrary branching placeholders).          
                                                                                
  #### 3. The Explicit Contraction Factor $r < 1$                               
                                                                                
  In the RBIM statistical mechanics mapping, the probability of a macroscopic   
  logical error is governed by the free-energy cost of domain walls             
  (non-trivial cycles) in the disordered lattice.                               
                                                                                
  1. **Summing Over Fail                                                        
  ──── (258 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  .                                                                             
                                                                                
  #### 3. Rigorous Witness Count on the Detector Graph                          
  ($\mathcal{N}_{\text{det}}(L)$)                                               
                                                                                
  Instead of counting arbitrary walks on a cubic lattice, we must count valid   
  error chains in the *detector graph* $G_D$ that form undetectable homological 
  loops of length $L \ge d$.                                                    
                                                                                
  1. **Effective Branching Factor ($\Delta_{\text{det}}$):**                    
  Due to circuit gadgetry and hook error propagation, a path in $G_D$ branches  
  with a maximum local coordination number $\kappa_{\text{det}}$ determined by  
  the circuit’s check matrix. For a rotated surface code with standard syndrome 
  extraction circuits, the exact circuit expansion yields a strict connective   
  constant on $G_D$:                                                            
                                                                                
  $$\Delta_{\text{det}} \le 4.82$$                                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. **Path Enumeration:**                                                      
  The number of self-avoiding homological cycles of length $L$ in the detector  
  graph $G_D$ is bounded by:                                                    
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{N}_{\text{det}}(L) \le C_1 \cdot L^{\beta} \cdot                   
  (\Delta_{\text{det}})^L$$                                                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\beta$ is the polynomial correction exponent for topological loops.    
                                                                                
  #### 4. Deriving the Explicit Contraction Factor $r < 1$                      
                                                                                
  The probability $P(w)$ of an error chain $w$ of length $L$ occurring in the   
  physical circuit is bounded by the physical fault rate $p$:                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P(w) \le p^{L / w_{\min}} \le \left(\frac{p}{1-p}\right)^{L / 2}$$          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $w_{\min}$ is the minimum weight of a physical fault combination        
  triggering the chain.                                                         
                                                                                
  Summing over all undetectable loops of length $L \ge d$:                      
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{fail}} \le \sum_{L=d}^{\infty} \mathcal{N}_{\text{det}}(L) \cdot   
  \left(\frac{p}{1-p}\right)^{L/2} \le \sum_{L=d}^{\infty} C_1 L^{\beta} \left[ 
  \Delta_{\text{det}} \left(\frac{p}{1-p}\right)^{1/2} \right]^L$$              
                                                                                
  We define the rigorous, circuit-level contraction factor $r$:                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$r \equiv \Delta_{\text{det}} \left( \frac{p}{1-p} \right)^{1/2} < 1$$       
                                                                                
  Solving $r < 1$ for the physical threshold $p_{\text{th}}$ using our exact    
  circuit branching factor $\Delta_{\text{det}} = 4.82$:                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$p < p_{\text{th}} = \frac{1}{1 + \Delta_{\text{det}}^2} = \frac{1}{1 +      
  (4.82)^2} \approx 0.0413 \quad (\text{circuit-level bound})$$                 
                                                                                
  For any physical noise rate below this threshold, $r < 1$, and the logical    
  failure probability is strictly bounded by:                                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{fail}} \le \frac{C_1 d^{\beta} r^d}{1 - r}$$                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  which suppresses exponentially with code distance $d$, accounting fully for   
  circuit topology and hook errors. $\blacksquare$

    with 2t < d. Logical operators are excluded by the code distance.
  - 7.2, detector graph computed: a dependency-free Pauli-frame enumerator
    covers every single circuit fault of the rotated code for d = 3, 5, 7. With
    the standard CNOT schedule:
    - the exact circuit distance equals d;
    - every fault is graphlike per check type;
    - the maximum detector-graph degree is 12;
    - each edge is produced by at most 52 (location, Pauli) faults.


    With the hooks parallel instead, the distance halves to ⌈d/2⌉. That shows
    the CNOT ordering matters and that the enumerator catches it. Everything is
    banked and hashed in checks/obj7-detector-graph/.
  - 7.2, the link to the canon threshold: EdgeLaw.push_localStochastic proves
    that circuit faults induce local stochastic noise on the detector graph at
    rate 52p. surface_code_rate then gives r = 22√(52p) < 1 exactly when p <
    1/25168. That threshold is conservative but fully explicit.
  - 7.3, ExRecBound.exrec_pair_bound: the correct form of Gemini's theorem is
    P(fail) ≤ |M₂|·p² + C(N,3)·p³. The prefactor is the count of malignant
    pairs, not a fraction of all pairs. There is no O(p³) placeholder, and
    single-fault resilience is a stated hypothesis the enumeration must check.
  - 7.4, AgeReliability:
    - log_distance_schedule proves Gemini's log-distance schedule holds failure
      ≤ ε at every age.
    - log_factor_not_absorbed refutes its claim that the bill stays ≤ C·t^{d_G}.
      That inference is now locked by a proof so it can't come back.


  Errors in Gemini's text that remain:
  - The per-chain weight (p/(1−p))^{L/2} recurs in every 7.2 version; the
    compiled bound is (2√p)^L.
  - The cited "Dennis, Landahl, Savvidou, Zurek, quant-ph/0106145" is not the
    canon's DKLP reference, which is quant-ph/0110143.
  - μ ≈ 4.68 and Δ_det ≤ 4.82 are estimates, not proven bounds.
  - The CNOT gadget's 27 locations, its 95 malignant pairs and its "≈ 2.71"
    factor are not enumerated; the true location count is in the hundreds.


  Still open in objection 7:
  - the DKLP application line for this graph;
  - a proof that the circuit distance is d for every d (the enumeration
    certifies only d ≤ 7);
  - the same enumeration for lattice-surgery merges;
  - pātai (a): check map by map that every repeated local map is Clifford, which
    would remove the compilation logarithm;
  - pātai (b): prove the wavefront sweep tolerates sparse patch failures. This
    is what removes the reliability logarithm without weakening the target.
