❯ These five Codex *pātai* strike directly at the remaining structural          
  vulnerabilities in the TIWA v4.2 specification. Let us address each one with  
  the formal rigor required to close the audit.                                 
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 1. The DKLP Application Line for the Spacetime Detector Graph             
                                                                                
  To rigorously apply the Dennis–Landahl–Kitaev–Preskill (DKLP) mapping to our  
  circuit-level detector graph $G_D$:                                           
                                                                                
  * **Statistical Mechanics Mapping:** We map the syndrome decoding problem to  
  the **Random-Bond Ising Model (RBIM)** partition function on a 3D dual        
  lattice.                                                                      
  * **Interaction Constants:** Each edge $e \in E_D$ in the detector graph is   
  assigned an effective bond coupling $J_e = -\frac{1}{2} \ln \left(            
  \frac{p_e}{1 - p_e} \right)$.                                                 
  * **Phase Transition:** The decoding success probability corresponds to the   
  disorder-to-ordered phase transition of the RBIM. The DKLP application line   
  guarantees that below the critical coupling threshold $p_{\text{th}}$, domain 
  walls (unmatched logical loops) are energetically suppressed with             
  free-energy cost proportional to their length $L$, yielding the exponential   
  suppression $r^d$.                                                            
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2. Proof That Circuit Distance Scales as $d$ for All $d$                  
                                                                                
  Brute-force enumeration fails for large $d$. We prove distance scaling via    
  **homological induction**:                                                    
                                                                                
  1. **Homology Class Preservation:** A logical error in the spacetime complex  
  $\mathcal{M}$ corresponds to a non-trivial cycle in the relative homology     
  group $H_2(\mathcal{M}, \partial \mathcal{M})$.                               
  2. **Inductive Step:** Assume a code patch of distance $k$ has minimum        
  homological generator length $k$. When scaling to distance $k+2$ via code     
  expansion or concatenation, the minimum weight of any non-trivial cycle       
  wrapping the spatial or temporal boundary is constrained by the minimum       
  distance between independent stabilizer generators.                           
  3. **Conclusion:** Because the boundary checks are uniformly tiled with a     
  constant local density, no local fault configuration can bridge the boundary  
  in fewer than $d$ steps. Thus, the minimum distance is strictly $d$ for all   
  $d \in \mathbb{N}$.                                                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 3. Fault Enumeration for Lattice-Surgery Merges                           
                                                                                
  During a lattice-surgery merge (e.g., parallel $ZZ$ measurements between      
  patches $A$ and $B$), the extended rectangle spans an auxiliary ancilla       
  strip:                                                                        
                                                                                
  * **Seam Geometry:** The merge seam consists of $d$ physical boundary sites   
  interacting over $d$ extraction cycles.                                       
  * **Malignant Pairing:** A fault on the ancilla seam can propagate            
  symmetrically into both data blocks. The topological weight of a merge fault  
  chain is bounded by the transverse cut of the seam, requiring at least        
  $\lceil d/2 \rceil$ simultaneous faults to bridge the seam without detection. 
  * **Enumeration Bound:** The exact combinatorial count yields                 
  $A_{\text{merge}} = \Theta(d)$, matching the bulk fault-tolerance polynomial  
  scaling without introducing stray sub-linear leakage.                         
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 4. Pātai (a): Checking Local Maps for Clifford Closure                    
                                                                                
  To remove the compilation logarithm:                                          
                                                                                
  * **Map-by-Map Verification:** Examining the repeating local update rules in  
  the TIWA compilation schedule reveals that every primitive generator is       
  restricted to the Clifford group ($\text{CNOT}, H, S, X, Z$ measurements).    
  * **Consequence:** Because all repeated local maps are Clifford, state        
  evolution remains within the normalizer of the Pauli group (governed by the   
  Gottesman-Knill theorem). This prevents operator weight expansion from        
  scaling non-trivially, collapsing the compilation depth logarithm and         
  satisfying Pātai (a).                                                         
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 5. Pātai (b): Wavefront Sweep Tolerance to Sparse Patch Failures          
                                                                                
  To remove the reliability logarithm without weakening the target:             
                                                                                
  * **Erasure and Failure Tolerance:** The archival wavefront sweep operates on 
  overlapping local patches. If a sparse fraction $\rho$ of spatial patches     
  suffer permanent failure (erasure), the local redundancy of the boundary      
  encoding allows adjacent live patches to reconstruct the lost state via       
  entanglement distillation and syndrome re-routing.                            
  * **Work Ceiling Preservation:** Because failures are sparse ($\rho <         
  \rho_{\text{th}}$), the repair and rerouting overhead is bounded by a         
  constant factor per unit volume. The cumulative archive and sweep work        
  remains strictly linear ($O(t)$) in $d_G = 1$, successfully removing the      
  reliability logarithm.                                                        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Shall we compile these five resolved pātai into the final verification bundle 
  for Codex?

⏺ Your question was whether these five round-3 answers can go to Codex as
  resolved. They can't: none of the five holds as written. I've built proper
  replacements for items 1 and 2, and items 3–5 remain open.


  1. DKLP application line via RBIM: not accepted. The RBIM phase transition is
     an asymptotic statement about the optimal decoder and gives no explicit
     bound. The canon threshold theorem fail_le_geometric needs the
     combinatorial witness property instead. I've compiled it:
     - MatchingWitness.half_faulty / matching_witness: for a minimum-weight
       decoder, every zero-syndrome residual cycle is at least half faulty.
     - Zero output, leanchecker 0, standard axioms.
  2. Distance d for all d by "homological induction": refuted. The argument
     never uses the CNOT orientation, and my enumeration shows the swapped
     schedule has the same uniform tiling but distance ⌈d/2⌉ (2, 3, 4, 5 at d =
     3, 5, 7, 9). In its place I've built a potential argument, now checked at d
     = 3, 5, 7 and 9:
     - With the standard schedule, boundary edges attach only at φ = 0 and φ = d
       − 2, and every edge (time-like and hook edges included) moves φ by at
       most 1.
     - So any walk joining opposite boundaries has at least d edges.
     - The exact BFS distance equals d at every size checked.
     - Carrying this to every d rests on a locality argument (each fault touches
       one check neighbourhood in one round), which I state openly as an
       argument rather than a computation.
  3. Lattice-surgery merge: not accepted. Needing "⌈d/2⌉ faults to bridge the
     seam" would mean the distance halves. A_merge = Θ(d) is not enumerated. The
     same enumeration is still owed for the merged patch.
  4. Clifford closure: not verified. It lists no maps. The real mechanism is
     that exact native gates need no approximation; Gottesman–Knill is beside
     the point. Still owed: a list of each repeated local map with its circuit.
  5. Sparse-failure tolerance: not derived. There is no confinement lemma, no
     outer decoder and no deviation bound. This is genuine research-level work:
     a von Neumann-type bound per patch that does not grow with depth, plus
     concentration for dependent failures. I haven't constructed it.

❯ Let us build the explicit inventory of repeated local maps for the TIWA       
  architecture. By cataloging each primitive local map and its exact circuit    
  structure, we prove that every operation executed during routine runtime is   
  an **exact native Clifford operation**, eliminating any need for              
  Solovay–Kitaev approximation or compilation logarithms.                       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Explicit Inventory of Repeated Local Maps                                 
                                                                                
  The runtime execution engine of our surface-code and lattice-surgery          
  architecture relies on a closed, finite set of repeated local maps. Because   
  these maps operate purely within the Pauli normalizer group, they incur zero  
  approximation overhead.                                                       
                                                                                
  | Map Identifier | Operation Name | Exact Circuit Structure | Target / Domain 
  | Clifford Status |                                                           
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |                                               
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Id}}$** | Idle / Memory Preservation | Single wire    
  with optional thermal/depolarizing noise channel | Data qubits | Exact        
  Identity ($I \in \mathcal{P}_1$) |                                            
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{1Q}$** | Transversal 1Q Clifford ($H, S$) | Single-qubit    
  rotation gate applied across all patch qubits in parallel | Data patch |      
  Exact Normalizer ($\mathcal{C}_1$) |                                          
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Merge}}$** | Lattice-Surgery Seam ($ZZ$ / $XX$) |     
  Ancilla strip initialization $\to$ Transversal $CNOT$ chain to boundary $\to$ 
  Ancilla $M_Z$ | Patch $A \leftrightarrow$ Patch $B$ | Exact 2-Block Clifford  
  |                                                                             
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Syn}}$** | Syndrome Extraction Cycle | 4-step CNOT    
  clock cycle around data-ancilla plaquettes / stars | Ancilla checks           
  $\leftrightarrow$ Data qubits | Exact Stabilizer Generator ($S_k$) |          
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Teleport}}$** | Non-Clifford Resource Injection |     
  Bell-state preparation $\to$ Lattice-surgery merge $\to$ Destructive readout  
  | Magic State Factory $\to$ Data Patch | Exact Clifford + Teleported State |  
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Verification of Exact Native Execution                                    
                                                                                
  1. **Absence of Parameterized Angles:**                                       
  Notice that none of these five repeated local maps involve parameterized      
  rotation angles (such as arbitrary $RZ(\theta)$ gates requiring               
  $\epsilon$-net compilation). Every gate belongs strictly to the discrete      
  Clifford hierarchy.                                                           
  2. **Pauli Preservation Property:**                                           
  For any input Pauli operator $P \in \mathcal{P}_n$, the output under any map  
  $\mathcal{M}_i$ in our inventory satisfies:                                   
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{M}_i(P) = \pm P' \quad \text{where } P' \in \mathcal{P}_n$$        
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  This guarantees that error propagation is entirely discrete and               
  deterministic, resolving Pātai (a) by proving that the compilation overhead   
  collapses to zero.                                                            
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  With the local map inventory established to eliminate the compilation         
  logarithm, shall we now construct the exact enumeration for the               
  lattice-surgery merged patch (Item 3) to clear out the remaining joins in     
  G.18?                                                                         
  Here are the rigorous resolutions for the remaining open audit items (Items   
  3, 4, and 5) to close G.18 permanently.                                       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Item 3: Lattice-Surgery Merge Exact Fault Enumeration & Distance          
  Preservation                                                                  
                                                                                
  To correct the previous error where a seam fault was misconstrued as halving  
  the distance to $\lceil d/2 \rceil$:                                          
                                                                                
  1. **The Seam Stabilizer Extension:**                                         
  When patches $A$ and $B$ (each of distance $d$) merge via an intermediate     
  ancilla strip of length $d$, the joint system temporarily defines a new       
  stabilizer group $\mathcal{S}_{\text{merge}} = \mathcal{S}_A \times           
  \mathcal{S}_B \times \langle Z_i Z_{i+d} \rangle_{\text{seam}}$.              
  2. **Preventing Distance Halving:**                                           
  The seam measurements are not passive breaks; they are active $ZZ$ check      
  operators measured redundantly across $d$ temporal cycles. Because each seam  
  check is part of the expanded stabilizer group, an error attempting to bridge 
  the seam must commute with all $d$ seam checks.                               
  3. **Exact Combinatorial Enumeration ($A_{\text{merge}}$):**                  
  A fault configuration on the seam can at worst span a length equal to the     
  transverse width of the auxiliary strip. However, because the strip is        
  protected by time-like repetition and spatial check redundancy, any           
  uncorrectable error bridging patches $A$ and $B$ requires a minimum weight of 
  **$d$**, exactly matching the bulk code distance.                             
  The exact combinatorial prefactor for the merge gadget is evaluated over the  
  expanded $\text{exRec}$ of size $N_{\text{merge}} = 2n_{\text{data}} +        
  d_{\text{strip}} \times T_{\text{merge}}$:                                    
                                                                                
  $$A_{\text{merge}} =                                                          
  \frac{\vert{}\mathcal{M}_2(\text{merge})\vert{}}{\binom{N_{\text{merge}}}{2}} 
  = \Theta(1) \quad (\text{independent of } d)$$                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  This proves that distance is strictly preserved at $d$ during lattice-surgery 
  merges.                                                                       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Item 4: Explicit Circuit Inventory for Repeated Local Maps (Pātai a)      
                                                                                
  To prove that local update rules require no approximation or compilation      
  logarithms, we catalogue the exact primitive circuits for every repeated      
  local map:                                                                    
                                                                                
  | Local Map | Target Component | Exact Quantum Circuit Gate Sequence |        
  Clifford Status |                                                             
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |                                                     
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Plq}}$** (Plaquette Check) | $X$-stabilizer on 4 data 
  qubits | 1 Ancilla $\text{Init } \vert{}0\rangle \to 4 \times \text{CNOT}$    
  (Ancilla $\to$ Data qubits in star order) $\to$ Hadamard $H \to \text{Readout 
  } M_Z$ | Exact $\mathcal{C}_3$ |                                              
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Star}}$** (Star Check) | $Z$-stabilizer on 4 data     
  qubits | 1 Ancilla $\text{Init } \vert{}+\rangle \to 4 \times \text{CNOT}$    
  (Data qubits $\to$ Ancilla) $\to \text{Readout } M_X$ | Exact $\mathcal{C}_3$ 
  |                                                                             
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Merge}}$** (Lattice-Surgery Seam) | Inter-patch       
  boundary fusion | $d$ Ancillas $\text{Init } \vert{}0\rangle \to d \times     
  \text{CNOT}$ across boundary seam $\to T$ rounds of $Z$-readout | Exact       
  $\mathcal{C}_2$ |                                                             
  | **$\mathcal{M}_{\text{Teleport}}$** (Resource Injection) | Magic State      
  Factory route | Bell-state prep ($\text{H} + \text{CNOT}$) $\to$              
  Lattice-surgery merge $\to$ Pauli correction adjustment | Exact               
  $\mathcal{C}_2 + \text{Pauli}$ |                                              
                                                                                
  Because every gate in these circuits belongs strictly to the Clifford group   
  ($\text{CNOT}, H, S, X, Z$), state evolution remains within the Pauli         
  normalizer. No $\epsilon$-net approximation is ever invoked, collapsing the   
  compilation logarithm entirely.                                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Item 5: Sparse-Failure Tolerance, Confinement Lemma, and Outer Decoder    
  (Pātai b)                                                                     
                                                                                
  To establish rigorous sparse-failure tolerance without a reliability          
  logarithm:                                                                    
                                                                                
  1. **The Patch Confinement Lemma:**                                           
  Let a spatial lattice of surface-code patches experience permanent or         
  transient patch failures (erasures) at a sparse density $\rho <               
  \rho_{\text{th}}$. We define a local renormalization operator (outer decoder) 
  $\mathcal{R}$ that coarse-grains blocks of $k \times k$ patches into          
  macro-patches.                                                                
  * *Confinement Proof:* If a patch fails, its syndrome information is absorbed 
  by adjacent live patches via boundary entanglement routing. The spatial       
  extent of the disturbance is strictly bounded to a local coordination radius  
  $R$ around the failed site, preventing unconfined error cascades.             
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. **Concentration for Dependent Failures:**                                  
  Using Dobrushin's uniqueness condition for dependent random fields, because   
  the failure density $\rho$ is strictly below the percolation threshold of the 
  coarse-grained lattice, large-scale connected clusters of failed patches      
  occur with probability bounded by:                                            
                                                                                
  $$P(\text{Cluster of size } s) \le c_0 \cdot e^{-c_1 s}$$                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. **Age-Independent Work Ceiling ($O(t)$):**                                 
  Because failed patches are repaired locally by the outer decoder with         
  constant expected cost per unit volume, the cumulative repair and wavefront   
  sweep work does not scale with system age $t$. The work bill remains strictly 
  linear $O(t)$ with $d_G = 1$, successfully removing the reliability           
  logarithm.

  integration targets for G.18?

  - Dobrushin's condition is about uniqueness of Gibbs measures. Its exponential
    cluster bound assumes failures are independent beyond a finite range, and a
    cascade along the sweep front is exactly what would break that, so the
    argument is circular.
  - "Constant expected cost per unit volume" does not bound the pathwise bill,
    which the canon requires.
  - It gives no outer decoder for the message W and no deviation bound.


  This item remains genuinely open research. The likely route is a von
  Neumann-style fixed point for per-patch failure, which needs each patch
  decoder to tolerate a bounded number of failed neighbours.

❯ Ah, my apologies e hoa. Let's drop the procedural preamble and properly seal

  The core vulnerability across Items 3, 5, and the precise mechanics of Item 4 
  is that topological assertions and asymptotic hand-waving cannot replace      
  concrete circuit-level accounting. Let's construct the unassailable,          
  bottom-up formulations for each.                                              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Seam 1 (Item 3): Lattice-Surgery Merge Fault Enumeration & Distance Bound 
                                                                                
  The danger in lattice-surgery merges is that a fault on the auxiliary ancilla 
  strip during the $ZZ$ boundary measurement could collapse the effective code  
  distance across the seam if check redundancy is miscounted.                   
                                                                                
  1. **Spacetime Cut Geometry:**                                                
  Let patches $A$ and $B$ (distance $d$) merge over an auxiliary strip of width 
  $w = d$ measured for $T = d$ rounds. The spacetime volume of the merge exRec  
  is $V_{\text{merge}} = d^2 \times d$.                                         
  2. **The Seam Commutation Constraint:**                                       
  An error chain $E$ on the data-ancilla bipartite graph is undetectable if and 
  only if it commutes with all spacetime checks in the expanded stabilizer      
  group $\mathcal{S}_{\text{merge}}(t)$.                                        
  During the merge, the seam checks are:                                        
                                                                                
  $$S_{\text{seam}}^{(t)} = Z_i^{(A, t)} \otimes Z_j^{(B, t)} \otimes           
  X_k^{(\text{ancilla}, t)}$$                                                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  For an error to bridge patches $A$ and $B$ without triggering a detection     
  event across the $d$ temporal rounds, it must form a non-trivial homology     
  chain across the *entire* transverse cut of the strip.                        
  3. **Exact Weight Calculation ($A_{\text{merge}}$):**                         
  Because the strip has spatial width $d$ and temporal depth $d$, any path      
  traversing from patch $A$ to patch $B$ must cross at least $d$ independent    
  check surfaces. A single physical fault on the ancilla seam only propagates   
  to weight-1 spatial errors on *adjacent* qubits within a single round; to     
  span the distance, it requires a connected chain of at least $d$ faults.      
  Thus, the minimum weight of a seam-crossing error is strictly **$d$**,        
  proving that the merge operation does not halve the distance to $\lceil d/2   
  \rceil$, and $A_{\text{merge}}$ remains bounded by local combinatorics rather 
  than sub-linear scaling.                                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Seam 2 (Item 4): Explicit Circuit Inventory for Local Maps (Pātai a)      
                                                                                
  To close the compilation logarithm, we must demonstrate that every repeated   
  local map executes via discrete, exact Clifford operations without            
  $\epsilon$-net approximations or angle parameters.                            
                                                                                
  * **Map 1: Plaquette Check ($\mathcal{M}_{\text{Plq}}$)**                     
  * *Circuit:* $\text{Init } \vert{}0\rangle_{\text{anc}} \to \prod_{i=1}^4     
  \text{CNOT}(\text{anc} \to \text{data}_i) \to H_{\text{anc}} \to              
  M_Z(\text{anc})$.                                                             
  * *Group Action:* Maps $X^{\otimes 4} \to +1$ eigenvalue projection;          
  normalizer preserves $\mathcal{P}_n \to \mathcal{P}_n$.                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  * **Map 2: Star Check ($\mathcal{M}_{\text{Star}}$)**                         
  * *Circuit:* $\text{Init } \vert{}+\rangle_{\text{anc}} \to \prod_{i=1}^4     
  \text{CNOT}(\text{data}_i \to \text{anc}) \to M_X(\text{anc})$.               
  * *Group Action:* Maps $Z^{\otimes 4} \to +1$ eigenvalue projection; strictly 
  within $\mathcal{C}_3$.                                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  * **Map 3: Lattice-Surgery Seam Fusion ($\mathcal{M}_{\text{Merge}}$)**       
  * *Circuit:* $\text{Init } \vert{}0\rangle^{\otimes d}_{\text{strip}} \to     
  \bigotimes_{k=1}^d \text{CNOT}(\text{boundary}_A \to \text{strip}_k) \to      
  \text{Readout } M_Z(\text{strip})$.                                           
  * *Group Action:* Bipartite stabilizer fusion via exact Pauli measurement.    
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Because the generator set $\{\text{CNOT}, H, S, X, Z, M_Z\}$ forms a closed   
  finite representation of the Clifford group, zero continuous angles appear    
  anywhere in the runtime instruction set. The compilation depth logarithm      
  collapses because no Solovay–Kitaev approximation is required.                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Seam 3 (Item 5): Sparse-Failure Confinement & Concentration (Pātai b)     
                                                                                
  To achieve age-independent work ($O(t)$) under sparse physical patch failures 
  ($\rho < \rho_{\text{th}}$) without relying on heuristic reliability bounds:  
                                                                                
  1. **The Local Confinement Radius ($R$):**                                    
  When a surface-code patch suffers an uncorrectable fault or permanent erasure 
  at coordinate $(x, y, t)$, its syndrome defect leaks to the immediate von     
  Neumann neighborhood. We construct a local renormalization operator           
  $\mathcal{R}$ that routes logical data around the failed patch through        
  adjacent live patches via teleportation-based fault-tolerant routing.         
  2. **Dobrushin Uniqueness Condition for Dependent Failures:**                 
  Let $\Phi_i$ be the indicator variable that patch $i$ is failed. The          
  conditional probability of failure given its neighbors satisfies the          
  dependency matrix condition:                                                  
                                                                                
  $$\sup_{i} \sum_{j \neq i} \left\vert{} \frac{\partial P(\Phi_i = 1 \mid      
  \Phi_{\setminus i})}{\partial \Phi_j} \right\vert{} \le \gamma < 1$$          
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Since the spatial interaction graph is regular (coordination number $z = 4$)  
  and $\rho < \frac{1}{z+1}$, Dobrushin's uniqueness theorem guarantees that    
  failed patches do not percolate into macroscopic connected clusters.          
  3. **Exponential Tail Bound on Cluster Size:**                                
  The probability of encountering an unconfined failure cluster of diameter $s$ 
  is bounded by:                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P(\text{Cluster diameter} \ge s) \le c_0 \exp\left(-c_1 s                   
  \log\left(\frac{\rho_{\text{th}}}{\rho}\right)\right)$$                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Because large clusters are exponentially suppressed, the active repair        
  overhead per time step remains bounded by a constant expectation value. This  
  keeps the total archive and wavefront sweep work strictly linear ($O(t)$),    
  successfully eliminating the reliability logarithm.

❯ Here is the explicit boundary-pair classification and cut-set invariant proof 
  for the spatial half of the detector graph, rigorously handling step-size-2   
  edges caused by hook errors.                                                  
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Boundary-Pair Classification for Step-Size-2 Spatial Edges                
                                                                                
  To prove that paths crossing the spatial lattice from boundary $\partial L$   
  to boundary $\partial R$ maintain a minimum length of $d$ despite step-size-2 
  edges ($\Delta x = 2$), we classify the spatial edge set $E_s$ and establish  
  a **Column-Cut Invariant**.                                                   
                                                                                
  #### 1. Spatial Edge Classification on $G_s$                                  
                                                                                
  Let the spatial slice of the detector graph have a coordinate width           
  corresponding to code distance $d$. The edge set $E_s$ is partitioned into    
  two subsets based on spatial displacement:                                    
                                                                                
  * **Unit Edges ($E^{(1)}$):** Standard syndrome-to-syndrome connections with  
  displacement $\Delta x \in \{-1, 0, 1\}$ and $\Delta y \in \{-1, 0, 1\}$ such 
  that $\vert{}\Delta x\vert{} + \vert{}\Delta y\vert{} = 1$ (or diagonal       
  local check adjacencies).                                                     
  * **Hook-Assisted Step-2 Edges ($E^{(2)}$):** Edges arising from multi-qubit  
  hook error propagation during syndrome extraction, allowing a spatial         
  displacement of up to 2 in a single row/column ($\Delta x = \pm 2$ or $\Delta 
  y = \pm 2$).                                                                  
                                                                                
  #### 2. Boundary-Pair Partitioning                                            
                                                                                
  Let the source boundary be $\partial L = \{ (0, y) \mid y \in [0, d] \}$ and  
  the target boundary be $\partial R = \{ (d, y) \mid y \in [0, d] \}$.         
  Any path $\gamma$ connecting $\partial L$ to $\partial R$ is an ordered       
  sequence of edges $(e_1, e_2, \dots, e_k)$ where each $e_i$ has displacement  
  $(\Delta x_i, \Delta y_i)$.                                                   
                                                                                
  We partition the possible boundary-crossing paths into three classes based on 
  edge composition:                                                             
                                                                                
  1. **Pure Unit Paths ($\gamma_1$):** Composed entirely of $E^{(1)}$ edges.    
  The minimum length is trivially $d$ because each step contributes $\Delta x   
  \le 1$, and $\sum \Delta x_i = d$.                                            
  2. **Mixed Step-1 and Step-2 Paths ($\gamma_2$):** Composed of a mixture of   
  $E^{(1)}$ and $E^{(2)}$ edges. A step of $\Delta x = 2$ skips an intermediate 
  coordinate slice.                                                             
  3. **Pure Step-2 Paths ($\gamma_3$):** Theoretically bounded by $\lceil d/2   
  \rceil$ if unconstrained.                                                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. The Column-Cut Invariant Proof (Blocking Step-2 Shortcuts)            
                                                                                
  To prove that paths in $\gamma_2$ and $\gamma_3$ cannot fall below length     
  $d$, we define a family of parallel **orthogonal cut-sets** (columns) across  
  the lattice:                                                                  
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\text{Cut}_x = \{ (x, y) \mid y \in [0, d] \} \quad \text{for } x \in \{1,  
  2, \dots, d-1\}$$                                                             
                                                                                
  **Theorem (Distance Preservation under Step-2 Edges):**                       
  *Every valid path $\gamma$ connecting $\partial L$ to $\partial R$ must       
  intersect every intermediate cut-set $\text{Cut}_x$ for $x = 1, \dots, d-1$,  
  and consequently requires at least $d$ edge traversal increments.*            
                                                                                
  **Proof:**                                                                    
                                                                                
  1. **Intersection Property of Step-2 Edges:**                                 
  Although an edge $e \in E^{(2)}$ has $\Delta x = 2$ (e.g., spanning from      
  coordinate $x_i$ to $x_i + 2$), the lattice geometry of the rotated surface   
  code forces intermediate syndrome nodes to exist at coordinate $x_i + 1$.     
  2. **The Topological Obstruction:**                                           
  A step-2 edge does not represent "teleportation" across empty space; it       
  represents a correlated multi-detector trigger where the underlying physical  
  fault bridges two plaquettes across an intermediate check. However, the       
  stabilizer check generators in $\text{Cut}_{x_i+1}$ are non-trivially         
  entangled with the traversal.                                                 
  Specifically, any valid homological chain crossing from $\partial L$ to       
  $\partial R$ must cross the $d$ distinct vertical stabilizer columns. If a    
  path takes a $\Delta x = 2$ step across column $x$, it leaves an uncorrected  
  boundary residual unless paired with a transversal check in that column.      
  3. **Metric Lower Bound:**                                                    
  Assign a weight $w(e) = 1$ for $e \in E^{(1)}$ and a fractional weight $w(e)  
  \ge 1$ for $e \in E^{(2)}$ (accounting for the necessary gauge/syndrome       
  overhead of resolving a hook error). Under this canonical metric, the total   
  accumulated horizontal coordinate change $\sum \Delta x_i = d$ combined with  
  the transverse routing penalty ensures that:                                  
                                                                                
  $$\text{Length}(\gamma) = \sum_{i=1}^k w(e_i) \ge d$$                         
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  for all $d \le 9$ and inductively for all $d \in \mathbb{N}$. $\blacksquare$  
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Does this column-cut invariant proof successfully close the spatial           
  boundary-pair classification for step-size-2 edges?

⏺ Neither round-5 submission closes its item, including the column-cut proof.
  Each restates a step already shown wrong or rests on an assumption that isn't
  proved.


  Column-cut invariant: this is not a proof.
  - Each edge in the detector graph is exactly one fault, so its weight is
    exactly 1. Giving the step-2 hook edges weight "≥ 1" to get the bound d
    assumes the conclusion.
  - A step-2 edge really does skip a column: one fault flips two detectors two
    columns apart. That is exactly how the swapped CNOT schedule drops to
    distance ⌈d/2⌉.
  - "Inductively for all d" comes with no inductive step.


  Seam 1, merge distance: it claims a single seam fault produces only weight-1
  errors. That's false: hook faults produce weight-2 errors. The argument never
  uses the CNOT ordering, which is what actually protects the distance.


  Seam 2, Clifford inventory: this is the third time it has repeated check
  circuits that measure nothing (a |0⟩ ancilla used as a CNOT control, and a |+⟩
  ancilla used as a CNOT target). Its "merge" circuit measures single-qubit Z
  on one boundary, not a joint logical. It still lists only the error-correction
  layer, not the installation's own maps, which is what pātai (a) asks for.


  Seam 3, sparse failure:
  - The displayed "Dobrushin condition" differentiates a conditional probability
    with respect to a discrete variable, which isn't defined.
  - Dobrushin's theorem gives uniqueness of a Gibbs measure. It doesn't rule out
    percolation, and the sweep's failure pattern isn't a Gibbs field anyway:
    its dependence comes through cascade, which is the very thing to be proved.
  - Routing logical data around a failed patch doesn't recover that patch's lost
    history.
  - The bound is still an expected cost, and the canon requires a pathwise one.


  What I computed instead, from the lattice-surgery merge enumeration (script
  and results banked with hashes):
  - Time direction: every merge edge spans at most one round. So any walk from
    the merge's start to its split needs at least T faults. That half of the
    general argument holds.
  - Spatial direction: column steps are at most 1, but row steps reach 2 where
    the hooks run. A single-coordinate argument like Gemini's therefore cannot
    work.
  - Boundary pairs: splitting the boundary by type (merge start, split, four
    spatial sides), the shortest odd walk between any two classes is min(d, T)
    for d = 3, 5, 7. That agrees with the exact distances.


  Proving the merge distance for every d needs a finer invariant, such as
  counting faults across a cut rather than detector steps. I haven't built it
  yet. The Clifford check of the installation's own maps and sparse-failure
  tolerance are also still open.

❯ huge!                                                                         
                                                                                
  does this help ?                                                              
  Let us tackle the formal proof debt head-on, starting with **Item 3 (The      
  Fault-Counting Cut Invariant for Lattice-Surgery Merges across all $d$)**,    
  building directly on your concrete finding that boundary walks scale as       
  $\min(d, T)$ and time-like edges require $\ge T$ faults.                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Formal Debt Resolution: Item 3 — The Fault-Counting Cut Invariant         
                                                                                
  To prove that the merge distance holds for every $d$ without falling into the 
  step-size-2 spatial trap, we abandon step-by-step detector walks and          
  construct a **cohomological cut invariant** over the spacetime cochain        
  complex.                                                                      
                                                                                
  #### 1. The Spacetime Manifold and Cochain Complex                            
                                                                                
  Let the lattice-surgery merge region be modeled as a compact 3-dimensional    
  spacetime manifold with boundary, $\mathcal{M}_{\text{merge}} = \Sigma \times 
  [0, T]$, where:                                                               
                                                                                
  * $\Sigma$ is the spatial surface-code patch complex of distance $d$.         
  * $[0, T]$ is the temporal interval of the $T$ rounds of seam measurement.    
                                                                                
  The physical errors and syndromes form chain and cochain spaces over          
  $\mathbb{F}_2$:                                                               
                                                                                
  * Let $C^1(\mathcal{M}_{\text{merge}}, \mathbb{F}_2)$ be the space of         
  physical qubit errors (1-cochains).                                           
  * Let $C^2(\mathcal{M}_{\text{merge}}, \mathbb{F}_2)$ be the space of         
  stabilizer checks and syndrome detectors (2-cochains).                        
  * The boundary operator (or differential) is the exact incidence map $\delta: 
  C^1 \to C^2$ defined by the circuit's check matrix.                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Defining the Transversal Spacetime Cut ($\Gamma$)                     
                                                                                
  Instead of tracking individual spatial or temporal steps (which get           
  confounded by hook errors and row step-2 jumps), we define an exact           
  codimension-1 **transversal cut surface $\Gamma$** embedded in                
  $\mathcal{M}_{\text{merge}}$ that bisects the merge seam separating patch $A$ 
  from patch $B$.                                                               
                                                                                
  * **Topological Requirement:** $\Gamma$ is a homologically non-trivial        
  surface that separates the initial boundary state (merge start) from the      
  final boundary state (split) across the spatial seam.                         
  * **The Intersection Property:** Any non-trivial logical error operator       
  $\mathcal{E}$ that bridges patches $A$ and $B$ without being detected by the  
  seam checks must represent a non-trivial element in the relative homology     
  group $H_2(\mathcal{M}_{\text{merge}}, \partial \mathcal{M}_{\text{merge}})$. 
                                                                                
  #### 3. The Fault-Counting Cut Theorem                                        
                                                                                
  **Theorem (Min-Cut Distance Bound):**                                         
  *For any physical error chain $\epsilon \in C^1$ representing a non-trivial   
  logical failure across the lattice-surgery merge, the support of $\epsilon$   
  must intersect the cut $\Gamma$ in at least $\min(d, T)$ independent fault    
  locations.*                                                                   
                                                                                
  **Proof Sketch:**                                                             
                                                                                
  1. **Co-boundary Expansion:** A logical failure requires an error chain       
  $\epsilon$ such that $\delta \epsilon = 0$ on all bulk checks, but whose      
  projection onto the boundary wraps non-trivially around the torus/surface     
  topology of the merged patch.                                                 
  2. **Weighing by Cochain Support:** Each physical fault location $f$          
  corresponds to a basis element in $C^1$ with weight $w(f) = 1$.               
  3. **Bounding via $\min(d, T)$:**                                             
  * In the temporal direction, because every merge edge spans at most one round 
  and start-to-split requires traversing $T$ temporal layers, any path across   
  the temporal projection contributes a weight of at least $T$.                 
  * In the spatial direction, any path crossing the spatial width of the seam   
  must intersect the $d$ independent stabilizer columns. Even with step-size-2  
  row hooks, the algebraic cup product $[\Gamma] \smile [\epsilon]$ in relative 
  cohomology evaluates to 1, forcing the spatial intersection count to be at    
  least $d$.                                                                    
                                                                                
                                                                                
  4. **Conclusion:** The total weight of the minimal fault chain crossing       
  $\Gamma$ is strictly bounded by $\min(d, T)$. For $d = T$, this locks the     
  merge distance at $d$ for all $d \in \mathbb{N}$, completely bypassing the    
  spatial step-size-2 coordinate ambiguity. $\blacksquare$

⏺ It helps with direction, but it doesn't close the gap. Counting faults across
  cuts, instead of detector steps, is the right kind of invariant for every d,
  and the time-like half is sound because it rests on the verified result that
  merge edges span at most one round. The spatial half, which is the actual open
  step, is asserted rather than proved.


  Why the spatial step fails. The proof says the cup product [Γ] ⌣ [ε] = 1
  forces at least d spatial intersections. A cup product or intersection number
  of 1 says the error crosses the cut an odd number of times. That guarantees at
  least one fault on the cut, not d.


  What the argument needs instead:
  - d cuts, pairwise disjoint as sets of fault locations, so that no single
    fault lies in two of them;
  - each cut crossed an odd number of times by every nontrivial logical error,
    which is the cohomology condition the submission does state correctly;
  - then any logical error contains at least d faults, one per cut.


  The disjointness is where the hooks matter. With the correct CNOT ordering, a
  hook fault sits inside one cut. With the swapped ordering, a hook fault lies
  in two adjacent cuts, which is exactly how the distance drops to ⌈d/2⌉.

  and I can construct it from the merge enumeration and check it fault by
  fault. One catch: my current boundary classification is only a heuristic, and
  it lumps the A and B segments of each side together. So the first step is
  exact boundary segmentation. From there, if the cuts follow a closed-form
  pattern in (row, column, round) that holds for d = 3, 5, 7, 9, that pattern is
  the all-d proof. Shall I go ahead with that?

⏺ Yes, I can see a route for all three open items. My confidence differs a lot
  between them, and the lattice is what makes the first one closable.


  Why the lattice matters (item 3, merge distance for every d).
  - Two kinds of edge. Checks of one type sit on a checkerboard sublattice. An
    ordinary single-qubit error connects two diagonal neighbours (row ±1, column
    ±1). A hook error connects checks two rows apart in the same column. That
    is the "row step 2" in the enumeration.
  - Why hooks don't hurt. Hooks only run along one direction. With the correct
    CNOT order, that direction is parallel to the logical, so a hook never helps
    an error cross the patch. Swapping the order turns the hooks across the
    logical, and the distance halves.
  - Infinitely many d reduce to a finite check. The lattice is
    translation-invariant. Every edge at any size is a translate of one of
    finitely many local types (bulk, edge, corner, seam), and all of those
    appear by d = 9.
  - The cuts become explicit. Columns of the sublattice serve as spatial cuts
    and rounds as time cuts. Each fault lies in at most one cut, and every
    logical error crosses each cut, which gives at least min(d, T) faults.
  - Remaining work. I need to fix the boundary segmentation, check the cuts
    fault by fault for d ≤ 9, and could put the potential-to-distance step in
    Lean. Confidence: high.


  Item 4 (Clifford check) mostly reduces to item 5.
  - What to check. I'll read the installation's six maps in the canon and
    classify each as classical, Clifford, or non-Clifford: holding correction,
    patch decode, boundary encoder, handoff, smearing and the guard. The
    smearing and flux-derived encoding look like continuous evolution, so some
    are probably non-Clifford.
  - Why that need not cost a logarithm. A fixed non-Clifford map can be compiled
    once to a fixed precision. Its leftover error is then just another fault
    below threshold, which error correction absorbs. Precision only has to grow
    with age if every location must individually reach ε/N accuracy, and that is
    the reliability question of item 5.
  - The upshot. The compilation logarithm and the reliability logarithm become
    one problem. Confidence: medium-high once item 5 is solved.


  Item 5 (sparse-failure tolerance) is the real remaining work, and it is 
  research-level.
  - Each patch tolerates one failed input. A patch fails only through its own
    fault or at least two failed inputs, for example by a majority over
    redundant interior reconstructions.
  - Bound cascades by counting witnesses. A failure then needs a witness
    structure that branches at least twice, so it contains many independent
    faults. The canon's Threshold machinery (LocalStochastic, fail_le_witness)
    is the right tool. The technical snag is that the dependency structure is a
    DAG, so unrolled witness trees can reuse nodes. That calls for witness sets
    rather than trees, as in Gács-type arguments.
  - Decode W from the outside. W has constant size and is spread over Θ(t^{d_G})
    sites. If witnesses dominate the failures, then a failed fraction above θ
    has exponentially small probability, so a global decoder recovers W with
    error ≤ ε. Overhead per location stays constant and the bill stays pathwise.
  - The risk. For general functions, reliable computation with noisy gates
    provably needs a logarithmic factor (Pippenger-type lower bounds). I expect
    W's structure, a constant-size output carried with huge redundancy, to
    escape that, but I haven't proved it. If it didn't, the upper bound would
    come out as t^{d_G}·log t rather than t^{d_G}. I'd report that plainly,
    without changing the target. Confidence: medium-low until the witness-set
    lemma is done.

  witness machinery. Shall I start?

❯ Let’s look at the exact architectural shadow where the Pippenger $\log t$     
  overhead hides.                                                               
                                                                                
  The fear of a logarithmic penalty ($t \log t$) in fault-tolerant              
  architectures comes from a hidden assumption: that as a system runs for time  
  $t$, the unrolled dependency DAG of past operations grows in depth, meaning   
  the combinatorial space of error histories expands, requiring a log-scale     
  safety margin to prevent rare deep cascades from metastasizing.               
                                                                                
  The single move that destroys that logarithmic ghost and keeps the work       
  strictly linear ($O(t)$) lies in recognizing the true nature of the wavefront 
  sweep:                                                                        
                                                                                
  The Move: The Markovian Renewal Property of Local Patches                     
                                                                                
  An arbitrary quantum circuit accumulates depth, forcing tracking of           
  long-range entanglements and history. But our architecture is a localized     
  cellular automaton with active syndrome projection.                           
                                                                                
  1. The Temporal Reset (Erasure of History):                                   
     A patch does not remember its infinite past. Every $T$ cycles, syndromes   
  are measured, errors are projected, and corrections are applied. This acts as 
  a quantum measurement-induced reset. The physical history of the patch is     
  periodically collapsed back into the code space.                              
  2. Bounded Correlation Length ($\xi$):                                        
     Because the spatial interaction graph is regular and check operators are   
  local, any failure cascade has a finite spatial-temporal correlation length   
  $\xi$. Below threshold ($\rho < \rho_{\text{th}}$), the probability of a      
  cascade growing to diameter $s$ decays exponentially ($e^{-s/\xi}$). It       
  cannot scale with system age $t$.                                             
  3. Why Witness Sets Collapse the DAG:                                         
     In Gács-type arguments, when you unroll a DAG where nodes are reused, you  
  get overlapping dependencies. But because each patch's state is locally       
  refreshed and error-corrected against a fixed reference frame, the DAG is not 
  a free-growing tree; it is a directed acyclic graph wrapped in finite-depth   
  causal cones. The number of distinct witness configurations of size $s$ is    
  bounded independently of $t$.                                                 
                                                                                
  The Up-shot                                                                   
                                                                                
  The reason the reliability logarithm isn't needed is that time does not       
  compound error depth; time is chopped into constant-depth renewal windows.    
  The spatial-temporal volume grows as $O(t)$, but the effective density of     
  uncorrectable historical entanglement remains bounded by a constant.          
                                                                                
  That is the structural key that keeps the upper bound at clean linear scaling 
  without needing Pippenger's $\log t$ safety factor.                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Does framing the witness-set bound around this Markovian temporal reset       
  capture the exact mechanism you were sensing for Item 5?

  = 3, 5, 7 across all tested T values, with every fault lying in at most one
  cut — giving the all-d formula from item 3. I'll save this as a reproducible
  script mode and kick off d = 9 in the background.

❯ Let's turn that conceptual breakthrough into a rigorous mathematical sketch.  
  To formalize this into a Lean-compatible structure for the canon, we need to  
  translate the **Markovian temporal renewal** property into a bounded          
  causal-cone lemma that eliminates time $t$ from the combinatorial expansion   
  of witness sets.                                                              
                                                                                
  Here is the formal proof structure for **Item 5 (Age-Independent Pathwise     
  Witness Bound)**:                                                             
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Formal Proof: The Bounded Causal-Cone Renewal Lemma                       
                                                                                
  #### 1. Definitions and Setup                                                 
                                                                                
  * **Spacetime Lattice:** Let $\Lambda_t = \Lambda_{\text{spatial}} \times [0, 
  t]$ be the spacetime volume of the running system up to time $t$.             
  * **Local Update Operator:** Let $\mathcal{P}_{x, t}$ be the active syndrome  
  extraction and correction operator acting on patch $x$ at round $t$.          
  * **Causal Dependence Depth ($\tau_{\text{win}}$):** Due to local syndrome    
  measurement and projection onto the stabilizer subspace, the physical memory  
  of an error at time $t_0$ is completely scrubbed or bounded by active error   
  correction within a temporal window of depth $\tau_{\text{win}} = O(d)$.      
  * **The Causal Cone ($\mathcal{K}_{x, t}$):** For any spacetime point $(x,    
  t)$, its past dependence is strictly restricted to the past light-cone:       
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{K}_{x, t} = \{ (y, t') \mid t - t' \le \tau_{\text{win}}, \,       
  \text{dist}(x, y) \le v(t - t') \}$$                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $v$ is the maximum propagation velocity of errors/syndromes.            
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma 1: Temporal Memory Erasure (Markovian Renewal)                  
                                                                                
  *Statement:* For any time $t > \tau_{\text{win}}$, the conditional            
  probability distribution of an error state $E_t$ at patch $x$, given the      
  entire historical trajectory $E_0, E_1, \dots, E_{t-\tau_{\text{win}}}$,      
  depends *only* on the syndrome and correction state at the boundary of the    
  temporal window $t - \tau_{\text{win}}$.                                      
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. Syndrome measurement $M_Z / M_X$ is a projective measurement into the      
  eigenspaces of local stabilizer generators $S_k$.                             
  2. By the axioms of quantum mechanics, a projective measurement constitutes a 
  complete decoherence of phase and error history outside the current           
  gauge/stabilizer equivalence class.                                           
  3. Therefore, the transition kernel $P(E_{t} \mid E_{0 \dots t-1})$           
  factorizes across temporal boundaries of width $\tau_{\text{win}}$. The       
  system possesses a finite memory half-life, making the error process a        
  **finite-memory Markov random field on a spatial-temporal lattice**.          
  $\blacksquare$                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Lemma 2: Bounding the Unrolled DAG (Eliminating $\log t$)             
                                                                                
  *Statement:* The number of distinct valid witness sets of size $s$            
  contributing to a macroscopic failure at $(x, t)$ is bounded independently of 
  total runtime $t$.                                                            
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. In a naive unrolled DAG of depth $t$, the number of paths grows as         
  $\binom{t}{s}$ or higher, generating the dreaded Pippenger $\log t$ factor.   
  2. Because of Lemma 1 (Temporal Memory Erasure), any dependency graph         
  extending backward in time beyond $\tau_{\text{win}}$ is severed by           
  intermediate projective syndrome resets.                                      
  3. An unrolled witness tree of size $s$ cannot span an arbitrary temporal     
  depth $t$; its effective depth is truncated at $\tau_{\text{win}}$.           
  4. Thus, the unrolled DAG collapses structurally into a bounded-depth         
  directed acyclic graph (BD-DAG) whose node reuse is locally constrained by    
  the spatial coordination number $z$. The combinatorial count of valid witness 
  configurations of size $s$ is bounded by:                                     
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{N}(s) \le C^s \cdot \text{poly}(s)$$                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $C$ depends only on the lattice geometry and $\tau_{\text{win}}$,       
  **with zero dependence on $t$.** $\blacksquare$                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 4. Theorem: Age-Independent Pathwise Sparse-Failure Bound                
                                                                                
  Combining Lemma 2 with the spatial Dobrushin/percolation condition:           
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}(\text{Macroscopic failure of diameter } S \text{ anywhere in }   
  \Lambda_t) \le \sum_{s=S}^{\infty} \mathcal{N}(s) \cdot \rho^s \le            
  \sum_{s=S}^{\infty} C^s \cdot \text{poly}(s) \cdot \rho^s$$                   
                                                                                
  For physical error rates $\rho$ strictly below the fault-tolerance threshold  
  $\rho_{\text{th}} = 1/C$, this sum converges to an exponentially small bound: 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}(\text{Failure}) \le c_0 e^{-c_1 S}$$                             
                                                                                
  Because this bound has **no $t$ multiplier**, the total accumulated error     
  probability over an arbitrarily long runtime $t$ remains bounded by a         
  constant expectation per unit spacetime volume. The work bill for             
  outer-decoder repair and wavefront sweeping stays strictly $O(t)$,            
  successfully removing the reliability logarithm. $\blacksquare$               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Does this formal causal-cone renewal proof hit the exact mathematical         
  structure you need for the Lean formalization of Item 5?

❯ Here is the rigorous formalization sketch for the **Density Theorem** (Item   
  5), structured for translation into the canon's `Threshold` framework         
  (utilizing `LocalStochastic` and witness-set bounds to guarantee bounded      
  failure density and constant-overhead outer recovery).                        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem Statement: The Spacetime Density Bound                            
                                                                                
  Let $\Lambda_t = \Lambda_{\text{spatial}} \times [0, t]$ be the spacetime     
  lattice. Let $X_{v, t} \in \{0, 1\}$ be the indicator random variable that a  
  local patch or macro-block $v$ experiences an uncorrectable decoding failure  
  at time step $t$.                                                             
                                                                                
  **Theorem:**                                                                  
  *Given a physical error rate $\rho$ strictly below the fault-tolerance        
  threshold $\rho < \rho_{\text{th}}$, the expected spatial density of failed   
  blocks at any time $t$ is bounded by a constant strictly less than the outer  
  decoder's correction capacity $\theta_{\text{crit}}$:*                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\sup_{t} \mathbb{E}\left[ \frac{1}{\vert{}\Lambda_{\text{spatial}}\vert{}}  
  \sum_{v} X_{v, t} \right] \le C \cdot \rho^k \le \theta_{\text{crit}}$$       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  *where $k = \lceil d/2 \rceil$ and $C$ is a geometry-dependent constant.*     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Formal Proof Structure (Lean / Threshold Framework Integration)           
                                                                                
  #### Step 1: Bounded Temporal Memory and Spatial Range (Finite-Range          
  Dependence)                                                                   
                                                                                
  1. **Causal Cone Truncation:** By the Markovian renewal property established  
  earlier, the state of block $v$ at time $t$ depends only on physical errors   
  occurring within its past light-cone $\mathcal{K}_{v, t}$ of finite temporal  
  depth $\tau_{\text{win}}$ and spatial radius $R$.                             
  2. **m-Dependent Random Field:** Define the collection of failure indicators  
  $\{X_{v, t}\}$ over the spacetime lattice. Because the underlying physical    
  noise process is product-distributed (i.i.d. physical error rate $\rho$) and  
  the local decoding map only couples sites within distance $R$ and time        
  $\tau_{\text{win}}$, $\{X_{v, t}\}$ forms an **$m$-dependent random field**   
  (or finite-range dependent field) where $m = \max(R, \tau_{\text{win}})$.     
                                                                                
  #### Step 2: Mapping Failures to Witness Sets (`fail_le_witness`)             
                                                                                
  1. **Witness Expansion:** A local failure indicator $X_{v, t} = 1$ occurs if  
  and only if there exists at least one valid witness configuration $\omega \in 
  \Omega_v$ of physical faults within $\mathcal{K}_{v, t}$ whose weight meets   
  or exceeds the code distance threshold.                                       
  2. **Probability Bound via Union Over Witnesses:** Using the canon's          
  `fail_le_witness` primitive:                                                  
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}(X_{v, t} = 1) \le \sum_{\omega \in \Omega_v}                     
  \mathbb{P}(\text{fault pattern } \omega \text{ occurs}) = \sum_{s =           
  k}^{\infty} N(s) \cdot \rho^s$$                                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $N(s)$ is the number of valid witness sets of size $s$ rooted at $v$.   
  3. **DAG / Witness-Set Collapse:** Because the causal cone has bounded volume 
  and tree-width (avoiding unconstrained exponential blowup), $N(s) \le B^s$    
  for some constant $B$. Thus, for $\rho < 1/B = \rho_{\text{th}}$, the sum     
  converges geometrically:                                                      
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}(X_{v, t} = 1) \le \frac{(B\rho)^k}{1 - B\rho} = O(\rho^k)$$      
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  #### Step 3: Application of the Outer Decoder (Density Theorem)               
                                                                                
  1. **Ergodic / Concentration Bound:** Because $\{X_{v, t}\}$ is an            
  $m$-dependent random field with local success probability bounded by          
  $p_{\text{fail}} = O(\rho^k) \ll \theta_{\text{crit}}$, we invoke a dependent 
  law of large numbers or Liggett-Schrittnut-Stapley concentration inequality   
  for dependent variables.                                                      
  2. **Macroscopic Recovery:** The fraction of failed blocks across any spatial 
  slice is concentrated tightly around its expectation. Since the expected      
  density is strictly below the percolation and correction threshold of the     
  outer hierarchical decoder ($\theta_{\text{crit}}$), the outer decoder almost 
  surely succeeds in routing data around the sparse failure pockets.            
  3. **Linear Work Bound ($O(t)$):** Because failed patches are repaired        
  locally with constant expected cost per unit volume, the total cumulative     
  work for error correction and wavefront sweeping scales strictly as $O(t)$,   
  successfully avoiding any reliability logarithm. $\blacksquare$               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Would you like to map this proof structure directly into the Lean theorem     
  signature format for your local repository?
