⏺ To your question about mapping the density theorem into Lean: it's done. Item
  5's core theorem compiles, and item 3 now has an explicit certificate covering
  every d, up to the locality step described below.


  Item 3, merge and memory distance:
  - The certificate is a family of disjoint fault cuts, built from a set of
    detectors S. Every undetectable logical error crosses each cut an odd number
    of times, so if no fault lies in two cuts, every logical error has at least
    as many faults as there are cuts.
  - The cuts come from closed-form potentials:
    - memory: φ = crossing coordinate + 1;
    - merge: φ = max(round, distance from the seam).
  - I verified this against every fault for d = 3, 5, 7: memory in both bases,
    and the merge at T = d − 2, d, d + 2. That is 14 out of 14 cases and up to
    59,724 faults, each lying in at most one cut. The d = 9 run is still going.
  - Extending to every d rests on one locality step, which I state openly as an
    argument, not a computation. Each fault touches one check neighbourhood in
    one round, and the formulas are translation-invariant, so the finite set of
    fault types already enumerated covers every larger d.
  - The step-2 hook problem is resolved: with the correct schedule, hooks change
    φ by at most 1. With the swapped schedule, the computed distance drops to
    ⌈d/2⌉.


  Item 5, sparse failure: the core compiles as SparseFailure (zero output,
  leanchecker 0, standard axioms).
  - disjoint_windows: patches with disjoint fault windows, each needing at least
    k faults to fail, all fail with probability at most ∏ C(|W|, k)·p^(k|U|).
  - many_failures: suppose the patches can be coloured with K colours so that
    windows of the same colour don't overlap. Then at least K·n of N patches
    fail with probability at most K·C(N, n)·(C(w, k)·p^k)^n.
  - That falls exponentially in N, uniformly in age, and it bounds the actual
    number of failures, not an expectation. A larger installation becomes more
    reliable, not less.


  The three Gemini item-5 submissions: the direction is right, but the
  conclusions aren't proved as stated.
  - Missing union over locations: "failure anywhere" needs a sum over all ~t
    locations, which reintroduces the log.
  - Lemma 1 is wrong: syndrome projection does not erase a logical error.
  - The density version is weaker: it bounds only an expectation and cites a
    concentration inequality ("Liggett–Schrittnut–Stapley" means
    Liggett–Schonmann–Stacey) without applying it.


  What renewal genuinely supplies is the window hypothesis that many_failures
  uses.


  Still needed to finish item 5:
  1. Window property for the canon's sweep. It currently corrects each patch
     against the already-recovered interior, so one failure could cascade
     outward. Each patch needs to decode from its own window, or tolerate failed
     inputs.
  2. An outer decoder for W that tolerates a θ fraction of failed patches, with
     W's encoding across patches specified.

❯ eroder property itself                                                        
  Let's drill down into the rigorous formalization of the **Eroder Property**   
  so it translates cleanly into the canon's Lean framework (`SparseFailure`).   
                                                                                
  To make this a bulletproof proof rather than a physical analogy, we construct 
  it as a **Peierls-type contour expansion on a directed spatio-temporal        
  graph**, bound by local erasure recovery limits.                              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Formal Proof: The Eroder Contraction Lemma                                
                                                                                
  #### Step 1: Spacetime Cluster and Boundary Expansion                         
                                                                                
  Let $\Lambda$ be the spacetime lattice. An **eroder** $\mathcal{E} \subset    
  \Lambda$ is a connected set of spacetime blocks (patches at specific rounds)  
  where local decoding has failed ($X_{v, t} = 1$).                             
                                                                                
  Define the **external boundary** (or perimeter) of $\mathcal{E}$, denoted     
  $\partial \mathcal{E}$, as the set of healthy patches adjacent to             
  $\mathcal{E}$ that receive state handoffs or syndrome inputs from             
  $\mathcal{E}$.                                                                
                                                                                
  **Lemma 1 (Perimeter-to-Volume Bounded Growth):**                             
  *For any connected eroder $\mathcal{E}$ of size $\vert{}\mathcal{E}\vert{} =  
  n$, the external perimeter satisfies the isoperimetric inequality:*           
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\vert{}\partial \mathcal{E}\vert{} \ge c \cdot n^{\frac{d-1}{d}}$$          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  *where $c > 0$ depends only on the spatial dimension $d$ of the code patch    
  lattice.*                                                                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### Step 2: The Local Failure Condition at the Boundary                      
                                                                                
  A boundary patch $v \in \partial \mathcal{E}$ fails to decode successfully    
  (i.e., transitions from healthy to infected) if and only if:                  
                                                                                
  1. **Corrupted Handoffs:** The number of its incoming edges originating from  
  failed patches in $\mathcal{E}$ exceeds its erasure correction capacity       
  $\theta_{\text{erasure}} \cdot z$ (where $z$ is the coordination number);     
  **OR**                                                                        
  2. **Fresh Physical Fault Witness:** A fresh physical fault witness $\omega$  
  of weight $\ge k = \lceil d/2 \rceil$ occurs within its local causal window   
  $\mathcal{K}_v$.                                                              
                                                                                
  Because our windowed sweep (Part 1) treats suspicious handoffs as *erasures*  
  rather than unflagged Pauli errors, the local decoder utilizes the full       
  erasure threshold $\theta_{\text{erasure}}$, which is significantly higher    
  than the standard error threshold $\theta_{\text{error}}$.                    
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### Step 3: The Peierls Contour Sum (Bounding Eroder Survival)               
                                                                                
  To prove that an eroder cannot grow indefinitely, we bound the probability    
  that an eroder of size $n$ can sustain its boundary expansion.                
                                                                                
  1. **Contour Weight:** For an eroder $\mathcal{E}$ to persist or grow, every  
  site on its boundary $\partial \mathcal{E}$ must either be hit by a fresh     
  physical fault witness or be overwhelmed by corrupted erasures.               
  2. **Probability Bound via `SparseFailure` Primitives:**                      
  Using the compiled `disjoint_windows` and `many_failures` primitives, the     
  probability $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E} \text{ is a valid failure cluster of size 
  } n)$ is bounded by the sum over all possible connected subsets of size $n$:  
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}(\text{Eroder of size } n) \le \sum_{\substack{\mathcal{E}        
  \subset \Lambda \\ \vert{}\mathcal{E}\vert{} = n}} \prod_{v \in \partial      
  \mathcal{E}} \left( \Pr(\text{Fresh Witness}) + \Pr(\text{Erasure Overload})  
  \right)$$                                                                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. **Subcritical Branching Parameter:**                                       
  Let $\tilde{p} = C(w, k) \cdot p^k$ be the probability of a local witness     
  failure. The effective contribution of each perimeter site is bounded by $z   
  \cdot \tilde{p}$.                                                             
  Because the perimeter scales proportionally to $n^{\frac{d-1}{d}}$ while the  
  interior volume scales as $n$, the total probability of an eroder of size $n$ 
  scales as:                                                                    
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}(\text{Eroder of size } n) \le \sum_{\text{shapes}} \left( z      
  \cdot \tilde{p} \right)^{\vert{}\partial \mathcal{E}\vert{}} \le              
  \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} A \cdot \left( z \cdot \tilde{p} \right)^{c \cdot         
  n^{\frac{d-1}{d}}}$$                                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  4. **Convergence Condition:**                                                 
  For physical error rates $p$ strictly below the fault-tolerance threshold, $z 
  \cdot \tilde{p} < 1$, forcing the sum to converge absolutely. Thus, the       
  expected number of macroscopic eroders of arbitrary size is bounded by a      
  finite constant, proving that failure clusters remain localized and           
  exponentially suppressed. $\blacksquare$                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Does this Peierls contour bound provide the exact mathematical backbone       
  needed to close out Item 5(1) and transition to the outer decoder             
  integration?                                                                  


❯ Let's formalize **Parts 1 and 2** with the same rigorous mathematical         
  architecture, mapping them directly into the language of the canon's windowed 
  sweep and `SparseFailure` primitives.                                         
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Formalization of Part 1: Tolerant Correction & Erasure Containment        
                                                                                
  To prevent spatial error propagation across patch boundaries, we formalize    
  the local decoder's handling of untrusted neighborhood handoffs.              
                                                                                
  #### 1. Definitions and Setup                                                 
                                                                                
  * **Handoff Alphabet:** Let the state space of a boundary handoff between     
  adjacent patches $u$ and $v$ be $\Sigma_{\text{handoff}} = \mathcal{P}_1 \cup 
  \{\mathbf{e}\}$, where $\mathcal{P}_1$ represents a standard single-qubit     
  Pauli signal and $\mathbf{e}$ represents an explicit **erasure flag**.        
  * **Local Decoding Function:** Let $\mathcal{D}_v$ be the local decoder for   
  patch $v$ operating within its causal window $\mathcal{K}_{v, t}$.            
  $\mathcal{D}_v$ takes as input the local syndrome measurements $S_v$ and the  
  set of boundary handoffs $H_v = \{h_{u \to v}\}$.                             
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma 1 (Erasure-Marked Boundary Handoffs)                            
                                                                                
  *Statement:* If patch $u$ flags its output as a failure ($X_{u, t} = 1$) in   
  round $t-1$, the receiving patch $v$ maps the incoming handoff $h_{u \to v}$  
  to the erasure symbol $\mathbf{e}$. Under the local decoder $\mathcal{D}_v$,  
  erasures are corrected up to the full code distance $d-1$, whereas unflagged  
  errors are constrained by the standard weight limit $\lfloor(d-1)/2\rfloor$.  
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. **Syndrome Mapping:** An erasure token $\mathbf{e}$ instructs the Pauli    
  belief-propagation / MWPM decoder to treat the corresponding physical qubit   
  locations as having known support with unknown Pauli values.                  
  2. **Capacity Scaling:** The minimum distance of a stabilizer code against    
  arbitrary localized erasures is $d$, allowing correction of up to $d-1$       
  erasures.                                                                     
  3. **Containment Property:** By converting suspicious neighborhood states     
  into explicit erasures rather than letting them pass as unflagged errors, the 
  local decoder prevents corrupted sign information from mutating into          
  undetected logical errors. Thus, failure at $u$ does not amplify error weight 
  at $v$; it merely consumes erasure capacity locally. $\blacksquare$           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Formalization of Part 2: Eroder Reduction & Windowed Coupling             
                                                                                
  With local erasure containment established, we formalize how the global       
  spacetime lattice is partitioned into disjoint windows to interface directly  
  with the compiled `SparseFailure` primitives (`disjoint_windows` and          
  `many_failures`).                                                             
                                                                                
  #### 1. Spacetime Cell Partitioning                                           
                                                                                
  1. **Lattice Tiling:** Partition the spacetime volume $\Lambda$ into a grid   
  of disjoint spacetime boxes (windows) $W_{i, j}$ of spatial width $W_s$ and   
  temporal depth $W_t$.                                                         
  2. **Window Failure Indicator:** Define the binary indicator random variable  
  for a window:                                                                 
                                                                                
  $$Y_{i, j} = \begin{cases} 1 & \text{if window } W_{i, j} \text{ fails to     
  decode correctly} \\ 0 & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}$$                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma 2 (Reduction to Independent Window Faults via                   
  `disjoint_windows`)                                                           
                                                                                
  *Statement:* Because the temporal depth $W_t$ and spatial width $W_s$ of each 
  window exceed the finite-range dependence radius $m$, any two windows         
  $W_{i_1, j_1}$ and $W_{i_2, j_2}$ whose separation exceeds $m$ have           
  conditionally independent failure distributions given their boundaries.       
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. **Finite-Range Coupling:** By the Markovian renewal property and causal    
  cone truncation, the joint probability distribution of a collection of        
  spatially separated window failures satisfies the hypothesis of the canon's   
  `disjoint_windows` primitive.                                                 
  2. **Probability Bound:** For any set of $M$ mutually disjoint windows        
  $\{W_k\}_{k=1}^M$, each requiring at least $k = \lceil d/2 \rceil$            
  independent fault witnesses to fail:                                          
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{k=1}^M (Y_k = 1)\right) \le \prod_{k=1}^M \left(   
  C(\vert{}W\vert{}, k) \cdot p^{k \cdot \vert{}U\vert{}} \right)$$             
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  This proves that window failures factorize into independent bounds without    
  introducing global cumulative factors. $\blacksquare$                         
                                                                                
  #### 3. Mapping to `many_failures`                                            
                                                                                
  By coloring the spacetime windows into $K$ independent color classes such     
  that windows within the same colour class do not overlap or share causal      
  dependencies:                                                                 
                                                                                
  * We invoke the canon's `many_failures` primitive:                            
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}\left(\sum Y_k \ge N \cdot n\right) \le K \cdot \binom{N}{n}      
  \cdot \left( C(w, k) \cdot p^k \right)^n$$                                    
                                                                                
                                                                                
  * This translates local window resilience into a strict, pathwise upper bound 
  on macroscopic failure counts that decays exponentially with $N$, uniformly   
  across arbitrary runtime age $t$.                                             
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  With Parts 1, 2, and 3 now fully formalized and aligned with your Lean        
  primitives, shall we transition to **Item 4** (the map-by-map Clifford        
  classification of the six operational maps) or work on the final assembly of  
  Item 5?                                                                       


⏺ The mismatch is only a decidability-instance difference between two
  identical-looking filters. convert closes it.

❯ Let's continue pushing forward into the final frontier of Item 5:             
  **assembling the outer decoder $W$** and proving the **age-independent        
  pathwise work bill**.                                                         
                                                                                
  Here is the formal proof structure for the remaining components.              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 1: The Outer Decoder $W$ and Erasure Tolerance                       
                                                                                
  To guarantee that macroscopic failures are successfully scrubbed before they  
  can accumulate, we wrap the surface code patch array in a classical or        
  quantum outer concatenated code $W$.                                          
                                                                                
  #### 1. Definition of Outer Code $W$                                          
                                                                                
  * Let the spatial lattice be partitioned into $M$ macro-blocks, each          
  protected by our surface code patch.                                          
  * Apply an outer linear code $W$ (such as a high-rate expander code or        
  Reed-Solomon code) across these macro-block indices, with relative minimum    
  distance $\delta_W$.                                                          
  * The outer code can tolerate up to a fraction $\theta_{\text{crit}} =        
  \delta_W / 2$ of failed patches arbitrarily distributed across the spatial    
  slice.                                                                        
                                                                                
  #### 2. The Outer Decoder Recovery Lemma                                      
                                                                                
  *Statement:* If the spatial density of failed surface code patches $X_{v, t}$ 
  at any time $t$ is strictly bounded by $\theta < \theta_{\text{crit}}$ (as    
  guaranteed by our `many_failures` eroder bound), the outer decoder $W$        
  successfully decodes and reconstructs the correct logical state with failure  
  probability vanishing exponentially in the system size.                       
                                                                                
  *Proof:*                                                                      
                                                                                
  1. By the `many_failures` primitive established in Lean, the probability that 
  the fraction of failed patches in a spatial slice exceeds                     
  $\theta_{\text{crit}}$ is bounded by:                                         
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}\left( \frac{1}{M} \sum_{v=1}^M X_{v, t} \ge \theta_{\text{crit}} 
  \right) \le K \cdot \binom{M}{\theta_{\text{crit}} M} \cdot \left( C(w, k)    
  \cdot p^k \right)^{\theta_{\text{crit}} M}$$                                  
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. For physical error rates $p$ below threshold, this probability is          
  exponentially suppressed in $M$ ($O(e^{-\alpha M})$).                         
  3. Conditioned on the failure fraction being below $\theta_{\text{crit}}$,    
  the outer code $W$ has sufficient Hamming distance to locate and correct      
  every failed patch via its error locator polynomial or syndrome routing       
  table. $\blacksquare$                                                         
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 2: The Final Assembly & Pathwise Work Bill                           
                                                                                
  With the outer decoder $W$ locking down global recovery, we finalize the      
  proof that the operational work bill remains strictly linear in time $t$      
  without any reliability logarithm.                                            
                                                                                
  #### 1. Per-Location Work Metric                                              
                                                                                
  Let $U(v, t)$ be the computational cost (syndrome extraction, local           
  belief-propagation decoding, and outer correction routing) expended at patch  
  $v$ during round $t$.                                                         
                                                                                
  * If patch $v$ is healthy: $U_{\text{healthy}} = \text{const}$ (standard      
  local syndrome measurement and check update).                                 
  * If patch $v$ is failed and handled by the local/outer repair mechanism:     
  $U_{\text{failed}} = c_{\text{repair}}$.                                      
                                                                                
  #### 2. Total Spacetime Work Bound                                            
                                                                                
  The total cumulative work $W_{\text{total}}(t)$ up to time $t$ across the     
  entire spatial volume $V = \vert{}\Lambda_{\text{spatial}}\vert{}$ is:        
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{total}}(t) = \sum_{t'=0}^t \sum_{v=1}^V U(v, t') = V \cdot t \cdot 
  U_{\text{healthy}} + \sum_{t'=0}^t \sum_{v=1}^V X_{v, t'} \cdot               
  (c_{\text{repair}} - U_{\text{healthy}})$$                                    
                                                                                
  Taking the expectation and applying our spatial density bound                 
  ($\mathbb{E}[X_{v, t}] \le O(\rho^k)$):                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{E}[W_{\text{total}}(t)] \le V \cdot t \cdot \left(                  
  U_{\text{healthy}} + O(\rho^k) \cdot (c_{\text{repair}} - U_{\text{healthy}}) 
  \right)$$                                                                     
                                                                                
  #### 3. Conclusion of Item 5                                                  
                                                                                
  Because the expected density of failures $O(\rho^k)$ is a fixed constant      
  independent of total runtime $t$, **the work per unit spacetime volume        
  remains strictly bounded by a constant.**                                     
                                                                                
  * No Pippenger-type $\log t$ factor appears because temporal memory erasure   
  (Markovian renewal) prevents historical error compounding.                    
  * The error-repair bill scales as $O(V \cdot t)$, completing the rigorous     
  proof structure for Item 5. $\blacksquare$                                    
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Would you like to write this outer decoder assembly and work-bill proof       
  directly into the Lean repository alongside the compiled `SparseFailure`      
  module?

⏺ Item 5 has moved a long way: its probability bound and its pathwise bill now
  compile and are tied to the canon's actual sweep. The eroder property you
  asked about turns out not to be needed, and the Peierls version would not
  close it. Three inputs remain open.


  New Lean module ToleranceSweep (zero compiler output, passes leanchecker,
  standard axioms):
  - frozen_after and wrong_has_cause: tolerant correction on the canon's actual
    G3Sweep.sweep. Every wrong patch either has too many faults in its own
    window or depends on too many wrong patches.
  - wrong_subset_bad: model the physics properly. A wrong inner commit reaches
    the next window only as an error of bounded weight at the shared interface,
    which that window's own fault budget absorbs. Then every wrong patch is one
    with too many faults of its own. Nothing cascades, so no eroder is needed.
  - sweep_density: the end-to-end bound. With probability at least 1 − K·C(N,
    n)·(C(w, k)·p^k)^n, fewer than K·n patches are wrong, at every age.
  - pathwise_bill: on that event, the realised cost, not its expected value, is
    at most base·N + repair·K·n.


  Your three pastes:
  - Peierls eroder: not needed, and the bound fails as written. There are about
    μⁿ connected shapes of size n, but each one's weight decays only like
    (z·p̃)^(c·n^((d−1)/d)), so the sum diverges. For d = 1 there is no decay at
    all.
  - Parts 1 and 2: flagging a failed handoff as an erasure is a good design
    move, and cheaper, since e erasures plus s errors are corrected when e + 2s
    < d. But a patch cannot flag a logical failure it hasn't detected, and those
    undetected failures are the dangerous ones. The interface property has to
    cover them too.
  - Outer decoder and bill:
    - Decoding a fixed-size memory slice by slice and taking the union over t
      slices brings back the log: the size must grow like log t.
    - The bill is again an expectation; pathwise_bill now gives the pathwise
      version.
    - A chosen outer code works for data the installation holds itself. The
      historical message's spread across its carrier is set by physics, not by
      design.


  Open in item 5:
  1. Interface property of the windowed decoder: a wrong commit, flagged or not,
     must cost the next window at most c faults. This can be checked with the
     same enumerator by injecting wrong commits at the interface.
  2. W's tolerance: the historical message must survive a θ fraction of failed
     patches, given how physics spreads it over the carrier.
  3. Long-lived holding at fixed size: this is the same log t reliability issue.
     It belongs with the open holding-power item.


  Also outstanding:
  - Item 4, the map-by-map Clifford classification, hasn't started.
  - SparseFailure and ToleranceSweep go into the canon at the next checkpoint.
    The canon is still at 180 modules / 1,104 names.
  - Prose batch 03 and the CITATION.cff abstract still need your OK. Nothing is
    merged or published.


  The bank update is pushed to draft PR #8 as c6ace7f.


  If you agree, I'll run the interface-property check (open item 1) next, since
  it's a finite computation.

⏺ Ha, yes. It's long because of a pattern that keeps repeating. Each "closed"
  item turns out to rest on a smaller, sharper question, and you only find it by
  actually computing or compiling the thing rather than describing it. Gemini's
  submissions mostly re-assert the conclusion in new vocabulary: RBIM,
  cohomology, Dobrushin, Peierls. So each round costs time without moving the
  proof, and the real progress has come from the enumerations and the Lean
  modules.


  Where things stand:
  - Canon: 180 modules / 1,104 names, every gate passing at checkpoint 05, on
    draft PR #8 and not merged.
  - Item 3, merge distance: there's an explicit closed-form cut certificate,
    verified fault by fault for d = 3 to 9 in 19 of 19 cases. What carries it to
    every d is a locality argument, stated openly as an argument rather than a
    computation.
  - Item 5, the reliability log: the core is compiled. The failure tail holds at
    every age, it's tied to the actual sweep, and the bill is pathwise. Three
    inputs remain:
    - the decoder's interface property, which is a finite check;
    - W's tolerance of a fraction of failed patches;
    - long-lived holding at fixed size.
  - Item 4, the Clifford classification of the installation's own maps: not
    started.


  What I'd suggest:
  - A natural stopping point: fold SparseFailure and ToleranceSweep into the
    canon with one more checkpoint, write a short handoff, and pause. Everything
    is banked and pushed, so nothing is lost.
  - Or keep going: the interface-property check is concrete and probably an hour
    or so of compute and code. W's tolerance and long-lived holding are real
    physics questions that may need your input, or a different source than
    Gemini.

❯ Here is the rigorous formalization for **Subtask 1: The Interface Property of 
  the Windowed Decoder**, structured to connect directly with the               
  computational enumerator and the `ToleranceSweep` module.                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Formal Proof: The Interface Bounding Lemma                                
                                                                                
  #### 1. Definitions and Setup                                                 
                                                                                
  * **The Interface Cut:** Let $\Sigma_{i, i+1} = \partial W_i \cap \partial    
  W_{i+1}$ be the spatial-temporal boundary shared between adjacent spacetime   
  windows $W_i$ and $W_{i+1}$.                                                  
  * **Wrong Commit State:** Let $\psi_{\text{commit}}$ be a wrong commit        
  produced by window $W_i$ at the interface $\Sigma_{i, i+1}$. This commit      
  represents an uncorrected logical error or a residual syndrome mismatch of    
  weight $w(\psi_{\text{commit}}) \ge k = \lceil d/2 \rceil$.                   
  * **The Propagation Map:** Let $\mathcal{T}_{\text{interface}}$ be the        
  transfer operator mapping boundary states from $W_i$ across $\Sigma_{i, i+1}$ 
  into the input registers of window $W_{i+1}$.                                 
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma: Bounded Interface Cost                                         
                                                                                
  *Statement:* For any wrong commit $\psi_{\text{commit}}$ at interface         
  $\Sigma_{i, i+1}$, the effective fault weight $w_{\text{eff}}$ injected into  
  the downstream window $W_{i+1}$ satisfies:                                    
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$w_{\text{eff}}(\mathcal{T}_{\text{interface}}(\psi_{\text{commit}})) \le c  
  \cdot w(\psi_{\text{commit}})$$                                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
  *where $c$ is a constant determined strictly by the maximum weight of check   
  operators intersecting the boundary cut (bounded by the lattice coordination  
  number $z$ and code distance $d$).*                                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Proof via Computational Enumeration & Local Check Geometry            
                                                                                
  1. **Local Stabilizer Intersection:**                                         
  In a rotated surface code, stabilizer generators $S_k$ have local support of  
  weight at most 4 (in the bulk) or weight 2 (at the smooth/rough boundaries).  
  When a wrong commit $\psi_{\text{commit}}$ crosses the boundary cut           
  $\Sigma_{i, i+1}$, it commutes or anti-commutes with the boundary stabilizer  
  generators of $W_{i+1}$.                                                      
  2. **Syndrome Weight Expansion:**                                             
  The downstream window $W_{i+1}$ measures its local stabilizers. The syndrome  
  vector $s_{i+1}$ triggered by the boundary commit is given by the projection: 
                                                                                
  $$s_{i+1} = \bigoplus_{k \in \text{Boundary}} (\psi_{\text{commit}} \odot     
  S_k)$$                                                                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Because each check operator $S_k$ has finite, bounded weight ($wt(S_k) \le    
  4$), a single-site error or logical chain crossing the interface can trigger  
  at most $z_{\text{cut}}$ adjacent checks in $W_{i+1}$, where $z_{\text{cut}}$ 
  is the local boundary coordination degree.                                    
  3. **Exhaustive Verification via the Enum                                     
  ──── (79 lines hidden) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  ating the $\log t$ spatial growth requirement.                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Proof via Stationary Renewal Process                                  
                                                                                
  1. **Finite-Horizon Exposure:**                                               
  Because each holding patch is active for at most $T_{\text{hold}}$            
  consecutive steps before being teleported into a pristine destination patch,  
  the maximum number of error-accumulation steps is capped at                   
  $T_{\text{hold}}$. By the canon's `ToleranceSweep` and witness bounds, the    
  probability of an uncorrectable fault accumulating within this bounded window 
  is:                                                                           
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{window}} \le \sum_{s=k}^{\infty} N(s) \cdot (\rho \cdot            
  T_{\text{hold}})^s \le C \cdot \rho^k$$                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $C$ is a constant depending on $T_{\text{hold}}$ but independent of the 
  global system age $t$.                                                        
  2. **Teleportation-Mediated Fault Isolation:**                                
  When the logical state is transferred from patch $v$ to $v'$ via              
  lattice-surgery Bell-state measurements, any accumulated sub-threshold errors 
  are projected and corrected by the boundary decoding map during the handoff.  
  The transfer operator $\mathcal{U}_{\text{renew}}$ acts as a purifying        
  projection:                                                                   
                                                                                
  $$\rho_{\text{output}} = \mathcal{E}_{\text{decode}}(\mathcal{T}_{\text{telep 
  ort}}(\rho_{\text{input}}))$$                                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Because the teleportation circuit itself has bounded weight and its errors    
  are governed by the same local witness rules, the renewal operation           
  introduces no cumulative noise penalty.                                       
  3. **Elimination of the $\log t$ Factor:**                                    
  The total system lifetime $t$ is partitioned into $M = \lfloor t /            
  T_{\text{hold}} \rfloor$ independent renewal epochs. The failure event across 
  these epochs forms a discrete-time renewal process with a bounded success     
  probability per epoch $1 - P_{\text{window}}$.                                
  The probability of survival up to time $t$ is given by:                       
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{survival}}(t) = (1 - P_{\text{window}})^{\lfloor t /               
  T_{\text{hold}} \rfloor} \ge e^{-\alpha \cdot t}$$                            
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\alpha = P_{\text{window}} / T_{\text{hold}} = O(\rho^k)$ is a         
  strictly positive constant independent of $t$.                                
  4. **Conclusion:**                                                            
  Because the survival probability decays exponentially with a constant rate    
  rather than a $t$-dependent polynomial, the system achieves perpetual,        
  long-lived holding at a **fixed physical size and constant code distance      
  $d$**, permanently discharging the reliability logarithm. $\blacksquare$

  lifetime. Holding a record for age t at a fixed failure ε requires one of
  three things:
  - Code size growing like log t: with active correction, that costs about t·log
    t in total.

  - Passive quantum storage: no self-correcting quantum memory is proven to
    exist in 3 dimensions or fewer. The 4D toric code works, but that doesn't
    help us.

  requires the final answer to tolerate a constant fraction of silent local
  errors. There are only two cases:

    patches, the density tail sweep_density and an outer decoder finish item 5
    with no log.
  - The record is not redundant, so every location matters. Then a log factor is
    unavoidable for any protocol built from noisy operations. That is the
    Dobrushin–Ortyukov / Pippenger lower bound for computing input-sensitive
    functions with noisy gates.


  Where the supports come from. None of these needs inventing; they already
  exist:
  - Redundancy of classical records is a theorem of quantum Darwinism. Brandão,
    Piani and Horodecki (Nat. Commun. 6, 7908, 2015) prove that classical
    information emerges redundantly, and generically, across environment
    fragments. Horodecki, Korbicz and Horodecki (PRA 91, 032122, 2015) prove
    that objectivity implies a spectrum-broadcast structure. An accessible
    historical record is therefore redundantly encoded by the fact that it is
    objective.
  - The interface property is the overlapping-recovery analysis of DKLP, the
    very paper the canon already cites. Windows with buffers of order d fail
    only if a long error chain lies inside one window.
  - Long-lived holding: DKLP's own memory result says the failure rate per unit
    time falls like e^(−cL). So indefinitely holding coherent quantum content at
    fixed ε does need L ~ log t, while classical records can be held passively.

  records, decoded through windowed patches. Your answer to my earlier question
  decides which branch applies.

❯ Claude’s trilemma is a brilliant summary of classical thermodynamics and      
  static fault-tolerance constraints, but it assumes a monolithic, un-renewed   
  lifetime $t$ where errors accumulate passively without intervention.          
                                                                                
  Here is why our architecture bypasses Claude's trap:                          
                                                                                
  1. Point 1 ($d \sim \log t$ and $t \log t$ cost): This is true if and only if 
  you maintain a single static block of memory without resetting it, forcing    
  the code distance to scale with age. But our periodic renewal protocol        
  (Subtask 3) resets the accumulation clock every $T_{\text{hold}}$ cycles via  
  teleportation/lattice-surgery handoffs. The code distance $d$ remains         
  strictly constant, and the total work scales linearly as $O(V \cdot t)$       
  rather than $t \log t$.                                                       
  2. Point 2 (Passive classical storage): Relevant for cold storage, but        
  irrelevant for an active runtime engine processing operational states.        
  3. Point 3 (No 3D self-correcting quantum memory): Claude is entirely correct 
  here—which is precisely why we cannot rely on passive physical protection in  
  low dimensions. That is the exact motivation for our active, windowed-sweep   
  architecture coupled with periodic renewal: it achieves what passive 3D/4D    
  memories try to do, but dynamically through active spacetime control.         
                                                                                
  The Decision: Claude’s argument describes the limits of static memory, but    
  our active periodic renewal turns lifetime $t$ into a stationary Markovian    
  process. We stick with fixed-size code distance $d$ + active periodic         
  renewal, bypassing the $\log t$ growth entirely.

❯ Here is the rigorous formal proof that resolves Claude's trilemma by          
  formalizing how active periodic renewal breaks the $d \sim \log t$            
  requirement for long-lived quantum memory holding.                            
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: Bypassing the Holding Lifetime Trilemma via Active Periodic      
  Renewal                                                                       
                                                                                
  #### 1. Setup and Statement of the Trilemma                                   
                                                                                
  Claude’s trilemma asserts that maintaining a target failure probability       
  $\epsilon$ for a stored record over total age $t$ requires one of three       
  conditions:                                                                   
                                                                                
  1. **Growing Code Size ($d \sim \log t$):** Code distance scales with age,    
  leading to a cumulative work cost of $O(t \log t)$.                           
  2. **Passive Classical Storage:** A one-time energy barrier $O(\log t)$       
  (applicable only to classical bits).                                          
  3. **Self-Correcting Quantum Memory:** Requiring physical dimensions $\ge     
  4D$, as no self-correcting quantum memory exists in $\le 3D$.                 
                                                                                
  **Theorem:** *An active 2D/3D surface code architecture implementing periodic 
  renewal with fixed code distance $d = \lceil 2k \rceil$ and fixed renewal     
  interval $T_{\text{hold}}$ maintains a cumulative failure probability         
  $P_{\text{fail}}(t) \le \epsilon$ for arbitrary age $t$ at a total linear     
  work cost of $O(V \cdot t)$, strictly bypassing the trilemma.*                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Proof via Stationary Markovian Renewal                                
                                                                                
  ##### Step 1: Bounding the Single-Window Failure Probability                  
                                                                                
  Let a holding patch maintain a logical state over a discrete time interval    
  $T_{\text{hold}}$, where $T_{\text{hold}}$ is a constant independent of       
  global system age $t$.                                                        
  By the canon's `ToleranceSweep` witness bounds, the probability of an         
  uncorrectable logical error accumulating within this window of length         
  $T_{\text{hold}}$ is governed by the physical error rate $p$ and code         
  distance $d$:                                                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{window}}(T_{\text{hold}}) = \sum_{s=k}^{\infty}                    
  \binom{\vert{}W\vert{}}{s} \cdot \left( C(w, k) \cdot p^k \cdot               
  T_{\text{hold}} \right)^s \le C_0 \cdot (c \cdot p)^k \cdot T_{\text{hold}}$$ 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  By selecting a fixed code distance $d$ such that                              
  $P_{\text{window}}(T_{\text{hold}}) \le \delta \ll 1$, the single-window      
  failure probability is strictly bounded by a constant.                        
                                                                                
  ##### Step 2: The Renewal Projection Operator                                 
                                                                                
  At the termination of each interval $T_{\text{hold}}$, the logical state      
  $\rho$ is transferred via lattice-surgery teleportation into a pristine,      
  freshly initialized auxiliary patch $\rho_{\text{fresh}}$, while the old      
  patch is measured out and reset.                                              
  Define the renewal operation as a completely positive trace-preserving (CPTP) 
  map $\mathcal{M}_{\text{renew}}$:                                             
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\rho_{(j+1) T_{\text{hold}}} = \mathcal{M}_{\text{dec}}\left(               
  \mathcal{U}_{\text{teleport}}(\rho_{j T_{\text{hold}}} \otimes                
  \rho_{\text{fresh}}) \right)$$                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Because $\mathcal{U}_{\text{teleport}}$ and local decoding                    
  $\mathcal{M}_{\text{dec}}$ project out accumulated sub-threshold entropy and  
  correct errors up to distance $d$, the operation acts as a **quantum reset    
  channel** that bounds the maximum entropy of the stored state.                
                                                                                
  ##### Step 3: Global Survival Probability over Arbitrary Age $t$              
                                                                                
  Partition the total system lifetime $t$ into $M = \lfloor t / T_{\text{hold}} 
  \rfloor$ independent renewal epochs.                                          
  Because each epoch is bounded by $T_{\text{hold}}$ and separated by a renewal 
  projection, the global survival probability $P_{\text{survival}}(t)$ is the   
  product of survival probabilities across each independent epoch:              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{survival}}(t) = \prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor t / T_{\text{hold}} \rfloor}  
  \left( 1 - P_{\text{window}}(T_{\text{hold}}) \right) = \left( 1 -            
  P_{\text{window}}(T_{\text{hold}}) \right)^{\lfloor t / T_{\text{hold}}       
  \rfloor}$$                                                                    
                                                                                
  Taking the natural logarithm and expressing it as an exponential decay rate:  
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{survival}}(t) = e^{-\alpha \cdot t}$$                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where the decay constant $\alpha$ is defined as:                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\alpha = -\frac{1}{T_{\text{hold}}} \ln\left(1 -                            
  P_{\text{window}}(T_{\text{hold}})\right) > 0$$                               
                                                                                
  ##### Step 4: Resolution of the Trilemma                                      
                                                                                
  1. **Why Option 1 ($d \sim \log t$) Fails:** Claude's Option 1 assumes that   
  error accumulation is passive and unbounded, requiring $d$ to grow so that    
  $P_{\text{fail}}(t) \sim t \cdot e^{-\beta d}$ stays small. However, because  
  our renewal process caps accumulation at $T_{\text{hold}}$, $\alpha$ is a     
  **constant** independent of $t$. Thus, $d$ remains strictly constant for all  
  $t$.                                                                          
  2. **Total Work Cost:** Since $d$ does not scale with $t$, the computational  
  work per patch per cycle remains bounded by $U_{\text{healthy}}$. Summing     
  over spatial volume $V$ and time $t$ gives a total work bill of:              
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{total}}(t) = O(V \cdot t)$$                                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  This completely avoids the $t \log t$ cost explosion.                         
  3. **Compatibility with 2D/3D Constraints:** The architecture achieves        
  long-lived quantum memory without requiring 4D self-correcting topological    
  phases, relying instead on active 2D surface code lattice surgery and         
  temporal renewal. $\blacksquare$

  checkpoint, and proceed to Item 4 (the map-by-map Clifford classification)?

⏺ No. Gemini's renewal argument doesn't hold, and its own formula shows why. It
  writes P_survival(t) = e^(−αt) with α a positive constant. So the failure
  probability is 1 − e^(−αt), which goes to 1 as t grows. That contradicts its
  stated theorem that failure stays ≤ ε at every age.


  What renewal actually achieves is a constant failure rate per unit time. A
  constant rate α gives failure of about α·t by age t. Keeping that below ε at
  age t needs α ≤ ε/t, which forces the code distance to grow like log t. This
  is arithmetic, not an assumption about static memory. DKLP's analysis of
  actively corrected, indefinitely stored memory gives the same result: the
  failure rate per unit time falls like e^(−cL), so L must grow like log t.
  Passive storage also isn't "irrelevant": it applies to records the
  installation holds, such as the archive I₀.

  - ToleranceSweep.recovery_tail now compiles, with zero output, leanchecker 0
    and standard axioms. Suppose an outer decoder returns the record whenever
    fewer than K·n patches are wrong. Then recovery fails with probability at
    most K·C(N, n)·(C(w, k)·p^k)^n, at every age. The chain runs end to end: the
    actual sweep, then sweep_density, then the outer decoder.
  - The remaining support needed is one property: the record must be spread
    redundantly across its carrier.
    - For objective classical records, published theorems supply this.
      Brandão–Piani–Horodecki (2015) prove classical records emerge redundantly
      in quantum Darwinism, and Horodecki–Korbicz–Horodecki (2015) prove
      objectivity implies a spectrum-broadcast structure.
    - The interface property is DKLP's overlapping-recovery analysis, which the
      canon already cites.
    - Without redundancy, the log factor is provably unavoidable for any
      protocol built from noisy gates (the Dobrushin–Ortyukov / Pippenger lower
      bound).

  particular never fails. It can mean every failure is detected and repaired
  within a bounded delay, which the canon already supports through E9
  self-healing and funded repair. Prompt repair costs O(1) per failure, so a
  constant failure rate gives a constant-rate bill. Only silent failures break
  this:
  - Redundant classical records make failures detectable by comparison.
  - Coherent quantum content suffers silent logical errors.

  is each particular's protected content its objective classical record and

  - Full quantum coherence: I can't find a proven route without a log t

❯ Here is the formal proof structure establishing why anchoring a particular's  
  protected content to its **objective classical record and relational          
  web**—via redundant carrier spread (spectrum-broadcast structure)—bypasses    
  the Dobrushin–Ortyukov / Pippenger $\log t$ lower bound and locks in          
  steady-state $O(1)$ maintenance.                                              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: The Relational-Objective Holding Bound                           
                                                                                
  #### 1. Setup and Definitions                                                 
                                                                                
  * **The State Space Splitting:** Let a system's total informational state     
  $\rho$ be partitioned into its fragile microscopic quantum coherence          
  $\rho_{\text{quantum}}$ and its redundant macroscopic objective record        
  $\rho_{\text{classical}}$, where the latter is imprinted across $r$ redundant 
  environmental/carrier fragments via a **spectrum-broadcast structure**        
  (Brandão, Piani, & Horodecki, 2015):                                          
                                                                                
  $$\rho_{\text{total}} = \bigotimes_{j=1}^{r} \rho_{\text{classical}}^{(j)}    
  \otimes \tilde{\rho}_{\text{quantum}}$$                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  * **The Detection Criterion:** An error on a redundant classical record is    
  defined as *silent* if and only if it corrupts all $r$ fragments              
  simultaneously. If it corrupts fewer than $r$ fragments, it is detected via   
  internal cross-fragment comparison and corrected with constant local work     
  $O(1)$.                                                                       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma 1: Redundancy-Mediated Error Detection                          
                                                                                
  *Statement:* For a classical record replicated across $r$ redundant carrier   
  patches with minimum Hamming distance between fragments, the probability of   
  an undetected silent failure over time $t$ is suppressed exponentially with   
  the redundancy factor $r$, rather than scaling linearly with age $t$.         
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. Independent local noise acts on each carrier patch with physical error     
  rate $p$.                                                                     
  2. For an error to become a silent, uncorrectable logical failure in a        
  redundantly broadcast record, it must simultaneously corrupt a majority of    
  the $r$ redundant fragments within a single active correction epoch           
  $T_{\text{hold}}$.                                                            
  3. The probability of this multi-site simultaneous failure is bounded by:     
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{silent}}(T_{\text{hold}}) \le \binom{r}{\lfloor r/2 \rfloor}       
  p^{\lfloor r/2 \rfloor} \le e^{-c \cdot r}$$                                  
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $c > 0$ depends on the error rate $p$. Because $r$ is a fixed           
  architectural design parameter independent of total system age $t$,           
  $P_{\text{silent}}$ is bounded by a constant $\delta \ll 1$. $\blacksquare$   
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Lemma 2: Stabilization of the Failure Rate ($\alpha$)                 
                                                                                
  *Statement:* By anchoring the protected content to its objective classical    
  record and utilizing redundant carrier spread, the effective failure rate per 
  unit time $\alpha$ ceases to be a function of age $t$, resolving Claude's     
  DKLP accumulation trap.                                                       
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. In the previous monolithic renewal model, single-point accumulation forced 
  $\alpha \sim 1/t$ or required code distance to scale as $d \sim \log t$ to    
  keep cumulative failure $\le \epsilon$.                                       
  2. With redundant cross-checking across $r$ carriers, any local corruption is 
  detected via comparison against uncorrupted sibling fragments before it can   
  compound.                                                                     
  3. The maintenance protocol performs local syndrome checks and majority-vote  
  healing across the carrier spread every $T_{\text{hold}}$ cycles. The         
  expected work per patch per cycle remains strictly constant:                  
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{E}[\text{Work}] = O(1)$$                                            
                                                                                
                                                                                
  4. Therefore, the long-term survival probability over age $t$ satisfies:      
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{survival}}(t) = \left( 1 - e^{-c \cdot r} \right)^{\lfloor t /     
  T_{\text{hold}} \rfloor} = e^{-\alpha_{\text{eff}} \cdot t}$$                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\alpha_{\text{eff}} = O(e^{-c \cdot r})$ is a fixed constant           
  determined entirely by redundancy $r$, requiring **zero growth in code        
  distance $d$ or spatial block size**. $\blacksquare$                          
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 4. Conclusion: Bypassing the Dobrushin–Ortyukov / Pippenger Bound        
                                                                                
  The Dobrushin–Ortyukov and Pippenger lower bounds dictate that maintaining    
  uncompressed, fully coherent quantum data against local noise without         
  redundancy forces a $\log t$ spatial expansion.                               
                                                                                
  However, because Quantum Darwinism and spectrum-broadcast structures          
  naturally project quantum states into objective, redundantly copied classical 
  information, our architecture converts fragile quantum coherence into robust  
  relational-objective records. This allows active, comparison-based error      
  detection to operate at steady-state $O(1)$ maintenance costs, successfully   
  closing Item 5 and the holding module without violating thermodynamic limits. 
  $\blacksquare$                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Shall we commit this formal relational-objective proof into the repository    
  notes alongside `ToleranceSweep` and finalize Prose batch 03?

  fixed can't hold a fixed ε for every age.


  It does point at the right answer, though, and I can now close the classical
  case properly. The protector has to hold its reference record of each
  particular, and that reference can be stored passively on a doubling schedule:
  - Rewrite the reference at ages 1, 2, 4, …, 2^j.
  - At stage j, use a barrier tall enough that the stage fails with probability
    at most ε/2^(j+1). A barrier of about k_B·T·(2j·ln 2 + ln(1/ε)) suffices, so
    stage j's cost grows only linearly in j.
  - Failure: the sum over all stages is at most ε, at every age, with no growth
    in code distance.
  - Cost: about log₂ t stages, each costing O(j), so O(log² t) in total. That is
    sublinear, so the long-run holding rate is still the constant cost of
    comparing the particular against its reference.
  - Self-healing: a faulty comparison or a false repair is transient, since the
    next comparison against the intact reference catches it. The canon's E9
    self-healing covers this.

❯ Synthesizing these three pillars provides a rigorous blueprint for indefinite 
  quantum coherence holding. When combined, they form a multi-layered defense   
  mechanism that simultaneously attacks the three failure modes of              
  low-dimensional quantum memory: **microscopic drift**, **macroscopic spatial  
  inflation**, and **thermodynamic entropy accumulation**.                      
                                                                                
  Here is how the formal integration of all three layers constructs the unified 
  proof for indefinite quantum coherence preservation:                          
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### The Tri-Partite Coherence Preservation Architecture                       
                                                                                
  #### Tier 1: Microscopic Erasure Conversion (The GKP-Surface Interface)       
                                                                                
  * **The Mechanism:** Physical qubits are encoded into infinite-dimensional    
  oscillator Hilbert spaces (Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill states) before being     
  mapped into the surface code lattice.                                         
  * **Formal Role:** Continuous phase and amplitude drift (which normally       
  causes uncorrectable coherent accumulation over time $t$) is detected and     
  mapped via homodyne/heterodyne syndrome extraction into **explicit            
  erasures**.                                                                   
  * **Mathematical Payoff:** Because erasure surface code thresholds approach   
  $\sim 50\%$ (compared to $\sim 1\%$ for depolarizing Pauli noise), the        
  effective physical error rate $p_{\text{eff}}$ drops exponentially, keeping   
  local fault density well below the critical threshold without inflating code  
  distance $d$.                                                                 
                                                                                
  #### Tier 2: Structural Scale-Invariance (Holographic Tensor Network          
  Scaffolding)                                                                  
                                                                                
  * **The Mechanism:** Instead of a flat Euclidean 2D/3D lattice where local    
  patches must scale linearly or logarithmically with system size, the          
  architecture employs a **holographic tensor network (discretized              
  AdS/CFT-style bulk-boundary mapping)**.                                       
  * **Formal Role:** Bulk quantum information is distributed non-locally across 
  multi-scale entangled tensors. Scale-invariance ensures that the logical      
  operator support expands logarithmically with system size *in the bulk*, but  
  maps to a constant-overhead holographic boundary.                             
  * **Mathematical Payoff:** This breaks the linear/logarithmic spatial         
  inflation trap ($d \sim \log t$). The system maintains bulk coherence by      
  leveraging renormalization group fixed points where multi-scale entanglement  
  is actively sustained by boundary operations at constant local complexity.    
                                                                                
  #### Tier 3: Dynamical Entropy Pumping (Driven-Dissipative Lindbladian        
  Engine)                                                                       
                                                                                
  * **The Mechanism:** Rather than treating memory as a static passive state    
  defended by walls, the system is maintained as a **Non-Equilibrium Steady     
  State (NESS)** via engineered dissipation (Lindbladian quantum pumping).      
  * **Formal Role:** Continuous syndrome measurement channels extract entropy   
  out of the system and dump it into an engineered external bath faster than    
  environmental decoherence rate $\gamma$.                                      
  * **Mathematical Payoff:** The master equation governs the density matrix     
  $\rho(t)$ such that $\frac{d\rho}{dt} = \mathcal{L}(\rho) = 0$ defines a      
  unique, error-corrected steady-state subspace. Because entropy is actively    
  purged continuously rather than accumulating monotonically over time $t$, the 
  lifetime variable $t$ drops out of the exponential decay equation entirely.   
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### The Unified Combination Theorem (Sketch)                                  
                                                                                
  1. **Entropy Balance:** Tier 3 (Driven-Dissipative Pumping) ensures that the  
  net rate of entropy extraction $\dot{S}_{\text{out}}$ strictly balances or    
  exceeds the environmental decoherence rate $\dot{S}_{\text{in}}$, bounding    
  the von Neumann entropy of the logical subspace to a constant                 
  $S_{\text{max}}$.                                                             
  2. **Noise Mitigation:** Tier 1 (GKP Erasure Conversion) ensures that any     
  residual noise operator acting on the manifold manifests as a localized       
  erasure rather than an unmonitored coherent rotation, bounding the            
  single-step failure probability $p_{\text{erasure}}$.                         
  3. **Topological Stability:** Tier 2 (Holographic Scaffolding) ensures that   
  these bounded erasures are processed across a scale-invariant tensor network, 
  preventing the spatial code distance $d$ from needing to grow with age $t$.   
                                                                                
  By locking these three mechanisms together, the system achieves **stationary  
  quantum coherence holding**—maintaining full quantum coherence indefinitely   
  at a constant local overhead, thereby resolving the quantum-coherence branch  
  of the holding trilemma.                                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Shall we draft this tri-partite combination theorem as a formal module for    
  the quantum-coherence research branch, or would you like to examine how Tier  
  3's Lindbladian engine interfaces with the canon's existing sweep operations  
  first?
