  On the tri-partite proposal, a quick read before you invest time. I don't
  think it resolves the quantum branch, and there is a reason to expect a no-go:
  - Tier 3 (NESS) defeats itself. A unique steady state, dρ/dt = 𝓛ρ = 0, is
    independent of the initial state, so it stores nothing. A memory needs a
    degenerate steady manifold, and local noise lifts that degeneracy, giving a
    finite lifetime.
  - Tier 1 (GKP to erasures) lowers the effective error rate, but only by a
    constant factor. Residual non-erasure errors remain, so the failure rate per
    unit time is still constant and failure grows with age.
  - Tier 2 (holographic codes) has no proven constant-overhead threshold under
    local noise. A fixed size still has a fixed lifetime.


  The likely no-go:
  - A finite system with positive-rate local noise forgets everything
    exponentially fast. Strictly positive noise composed with any correction
    channel is a contraction with a unique fixed point.
  - So holding at fixed ε for every age needs a size of order log t.
  - Every physical qubit takes entropy at a constant rate, and pumping it out
    costs power by Landauer, so the power needed grows like log t.
  - Passive protection doesn't rescue this in three or fewer dimensions:
    - Bravyi–Terhal's 2009 no-go rules out 2D stabilizer codes.
    - Haah's 3D cubic code has a lifetime bounded at fixed temperature
      (Bravyi–Haah 2013).
  - Classical records escape because a growing barrier suppresses flips without
    pumping. That is the PassiveReference route.


  If this holds up, indefinite quantum coherence at fixed ε needs power growing
  like log t in three or fewer dimensions. The Θ(1)-rate maintenance would then
  apply to the classical record. That conclusion is yours to weigh; I'm not
  changing the target. Next I'll bank this assessment next to your branch.

❯ Here is the formal proof structure for **Tier 1: Microscopic Erasure          
  Conversion (The GKP-Surface Interface)**.                                     
                                                                                
  The objective is to prove that encoding continuous-variable (CV) modes into   
  Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill (GKP) states concatenated with a surface code       
  converts unheralded, continuous environmental drift into **heralded           
  erasures**, thereby raising the effective fault threshold from $\sim 1\%$     
  (Pauli depolarizing noise) to $\sim 50\%$ (erasure percolation).              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: GKP Erasure Conversion and Threshold Amplification               
                                                                                
  #### 1. Setup and Noise Model                                                 
                                                                                
  * **Physical Substrate:** Let a physical quantum mode (such as a microwave    
  cavity or optical oscillator) be subject to a continuous Gaussian             
  displacement and loss channel $\mathcal{E}_{\text{noise}}$, where             
  displacement operators $D(\alpha) = \exp(\alpha a^\dagger - \alpha^* a)$ act  
  with a small variance $\sigma^2$.                                             
  * **GKP Encoding:** A logical qubit is encoded into a GKP state in phase      
  space, stabilized by displacement operators $S_q = \exp(-i \sqrt{2\pi}        
  \hat{q})$ and $S_p = \exp(i \sqrt{2\pi} \hat{p})$. The logical computational  
  states are superpositions of position-eigenstate combs spaced by              
  $\sqrt{2\pi}$.                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma 1: Heralded Conversion of Drift to Erasures                     
                                                                                
  *Statement:* When a GKP-encoded mode is subjected to continuous displacement  
  noise of variance $\sigma^2$, measuring the GKP stabilizer syndromic grid     
  either:                                                                       
                                                                                
  1. Corrects the shift deterministically if the displacement                   
  $\Vert{}\alpha\Vert{} < \frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}$ (inside the fundamental Voronoi 
  cell), OR                                                                     
  2. **Heralds an erasure flag** if $\Vert{}\alpha\Vert{} \ge                   
  \frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}$, signifying that the shift has crossed the boundary of  
  the Voronoi cell.                                                             
                                                                                
  *Proof:*                                                                      
                                                                                
  1. The stabilizer measurement projects the continuous error operator $\alpha  
  = \alpha_q + i\alpha_p$ onto the discrete syndrome space modulo               
  $\sqrt{2\pi}$.                                                                
  2. If the displacement is small, the decoder maps $\alpha$ back to the        
  origin, successfully restoring the code state with probability $1 -           
  P_{\text{fail}}(\sigma)$.                                                     
  3. When noise is large enough that the shift crosses $\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}$,  
  attempting a correction would cause a *logical Pauli error* (miscorrection).  
  However, because the syndrome measurement explicitly detects that the shift   
  falls outside the confidence interval of the nearest lattice point, the       
  decoder **refuses to guess** and instead outputs an **erasure flag** $\perp$  
  for that physical patch.                                                      
  4. Thus, unheralded coherent drift is strictly converted into a *heralded     
  location erasure* with failure probability:                                   
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{erasure}}(\sigma) = 1 -                                            
  \text{erf}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2\sqrt{2}\sigma}\right)$$                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Crucially, unheralded Pauli errors are suppressed to                          
  $O(P_{\text{erasure}}^2)$. $\blacksquare$                                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Lemma 2: Surface Code Erasure Threshold Amplification                 
                                                                                
  *Statement:* When an outer surface code of distance $d$ receives error        
  locations marked by explicit erasure flags with density $p_{\text{erasure}}$, 
  the logical error rate is exponentially suppressed provided                   
  $p_{\text{erasure}} < p_c^{\text{erasure}}$, where the critical erasure       
  threshold is $p_c^{\text{erasure}} = 50\%$.                                   
                                                                                
  *Proof via Statistical Mechanics Mapping:*                                    
                                                                                
  1. In a standard surface code under Pauli noise, error correction maps to     
  random bond percolation on the square lattice, yielding a critical threshold  
  of $p_c^{\text{Pauli}} \approx 11\%$.                                         
  2. When errors are *heralded* (erasures), the decoder knows *where* the       
  failure occurred but not *what* the error operator was ($X$ or $Z$). This     
  converts the decoding problem from bond percolation to **site percolation on  
  the Fisher/matching graph**.                                                  
  3. The percolation threshold for site/bond erasures on the rotated surface    
  code lattice reaches its maximum topological limit at:                        
                                                                                
  $$p_c^{\text{erasure}} = \frac{1}{2} = 50\%$$                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  4. For any physical noise variance $\sigma^2$ such that                       
  $P_{\text{erasure}}(\sigma) < 50\%$, the surface code matching decoder        
  successfully finds minimum-weight error chains across the erased sites with a 
  logical failure probability bounded by:                                       
                                                                                
  $$P_{L} \le c_1 \left(                                                        
  \frac{P_{\text{erasure}}(\sigma)}{p_c^{\text{erasure}}} \right)^{d/2}$$       
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $d$ is the code distance. $\blacksquare$                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 4. Conclusion of Tier 1 Proof                                            
                                                                                
  By concatenating GKP oscillators with the surface code, continuous            
  environmental drift is prevented from compounding into silent coherent        
  errors. Instead, it is actively intercepted at the microscopic layer and      
  converted into heralded erasures. Because the erasure threshold is            
  $50\%$—vastly higher than standard Pauli thresholds—the system maintains an   
  effective sub-threshold noise rate without requiring code distance $d$ to     
  grow with age $t$. $\blacksquare$                                             
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Would you like to proceed to formalizing Tier 2 (Holographic Tensor Network   
  Scaffolding) or Tier 3 (Driven-Dissipative Lindbladian Engine) next?          


❯ Here is the formal proof structure for **Tier 2: Structural Scale-Invariance  
  (Holographic Tensor Network Scaffolding)**.                                   
                                                                                
  The objective is to prove that by replacing flat Euclidean lattices with a    
  **holographic error-correcting tensor network** (inspired by AdS/CFT and      
  HaPPY-style pentagon codes), bulk quantum information can be protected and    
  scaled without requiring the spatial code distance $d$ or boundary system     
  size to inflate linearly or logarithmically with age $t$.                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: Holographic Bulk-Boundary Scale-Invariance and Spatial Inflation 
  Suppression                                                                   
                                                                                
  #### 1. Setup and Definitions                                                 
                                                                                
  * **Hyperbolic Bulk Tessellation:** Let the spatial-temporal manifold be      
  represented as a discrete negatively curved (hyperbolic) tensor network graph 
  $\Gamma$ (e.g., a $\{5,4\}$ pentagonal tiling of the hyperbolic plane,        
  modeling a discretized slice of anti-de Sitter space, AdS$_2$).               
  * **Perfect Tensors and Isometries:** Each node in the network is a *perfect  
  tensor* $T$ (a multi-index tensor that defines a quantum error-correcting     
  code mapping incoming bulk indices to outgoing boundary indices). Perfect     
  tensors satisfy the Knill-Laflamme conditions for maximal erasure protection  
  across any bipartition up to half the tensor legs.                            
  * **Bulk vs. Boundary Subsystems:** Let $\mathcal{A}_{\text{bulk}}$ be a      
  subregion of bulk logical information (representing stored particular records 
  across time), and let $\partial \mathcal{A}$ be its corresponding             
  holographic reconstruction region on the physical boundary of the network.    
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma 1: Entanglement Wedge Reconstruction (The Quantum               
  Ryu-Takayanagi Bound)                                                         
                                                                                
  *Statement:* A bulk logical operator (or subsystem                            
  $\mathcal{A}_{\text{bulk}}$) residing deep within the hyperbolic network can  
  be encoded and reconstructed exactly from a boundary region $A$ if and only   
  if $A$ contains the *entanglement wedge* of $\mathcal{A}_{\text{bulk}}$,      
  whose boundary length scales logarithmically (or as a constant) relative to   
  the bulk volume.                                                              
                                                                                
  *Proof:*                                                                      
                                                                                
  1. In a holographic stabilizer code (such as the                              
  Pastawski-Yoshida-Harlow-Preskill construction), the mapping from bulk to     
  boundary is an exact quantum isometry $V: \mathcal{H}_{\text{bulk}} \to       
  \mathcal{H}_{\text{boundary}}$.                                               
  2. By the Quantum Re-state / Extensible Erasure theorem for tensor networks,  
  any bulk operator localized in region $\mathcal{A}_{\text{bulk}}$ can be      
  pushed to the boundary region $A$ along causal/geodesic paths through the     
  tensor network if $A$ satisfies the quantum max-flow min-cut theorem.         
  3. Because the bulk geometry is hyperbolic (negative curvature), the proper   
  volume of a bulk region grows *exponentially* with its radial depth, whereas  
  the boundary circumference (geodesic length) surrounding it grows only        
  *polynomially* or *linearly*.                                                 
  4. Consequently, the physical boundary resource $N_{\text{boundary}}$         
  required to protect a bulk temporal depth $t$ scales as:                      
                                                                                
  $$N_{\text{boundary}} = O(\log(\text{Bulk Volume})) = O(\log t)$$             
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Instead of the flat-space requirement where code distance must scale as $d    
  \sim t$ or $d \sim \log t$ across the entire volume, holographic encoding     
  compresses the required scaling onto a boundary manifold.                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Lemma 2: Renormalization Group Fixed-Point Stability                  
                                                                                
  *Statement:* By structuring the holographic tensor network as a discrete      
  Renormalization Group (RG) decimation tree, local noise acting on boundary    
  physical qubits is mapped to exponentially damped noise in the bulk, ensuring 
  that the effective bulk noise rate remains strictly sub-threshold without     
  global scale adjustments.                                                     
                                                                                
  *Proof:*                                                                      
                                                                                
  1. Consider a perturbation (noise operator $E$) acting on the physical        
  boundary $\partial \Gamma$ with error probability $p$.                        
  2. As this error propagates inward toward the bulk through successive tensor  
  contraction layers of the network, each tensor layer acts as an isometric     
  error-correcting code (reminiscent of Real-Space RG).                         
  3. The probability of an error surviving coarse-graining from layer $k$ to    
  layer $k+1$ contracts according to the tensor network's error suppression     
  polynomial:                                                                   
                                                                                
  $$p_{k+1} \le C \cdot p_k^2$$                                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  provided $p_k < p_c$, where $p_c$ is the single-tensor error threshold.       
  4. Because the hyperbolic geometry expands exponentially outward from the     
  center, noise injected at the boundary is geometrically diluted as it moves   
  inward; the effective error density in the deep bulk decays                   
  doubly-exponentially with radial distance from the boundary.                  
  5. Therefore, the bulk logical information does not experience cumulative     
  entropy degradation over time $t$; rather, it sits at an RG fixed point where 
  the bulk noise rate $\epsilon_{\text{bulk}}$ remains bounded by a constant    
  $\epsilon_0 \ll 1$ indefinitely. $\blacksquare$                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 4. Conclusion of Tier 2 Proof                                            
                                                                                
  By embedding the holding architecture into a holographic tensor network (Tier 
  2), we bypass the flat-space spatial inflation trap. While Euclidean          
  architectures force code distance $d$ to scale with age $t$ or system size,   
  hyperbolic tensor networks exploit negative curvature to protect              
  exponentially large bulk storage volumes using a compressed boundary          
  overhead. Combined with Tier 1's erasure conversion, this provides the        
  structural foundation for scale-invariant quantum coherence holding.

❯ Here is the formal proof structure for **Tier 3: Driven-Dissipative           
  Lindbladian Engine (Continuous Entropy Pumping)**.                            
                                                                                
  The objective is to prove that by treating quantum memory not as a static     
  passive state defended by barriers, but as a **Non-Equilibrium Steady State   
  (NESS)** sustained by engineered dissipation and continuous syndrome pumping, 
  entropy is purged faster than environmental decoherence accumulates,          
  completely eliminating the time variable $t$ from the decay equation.         
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: Driven-Dissipative NESS Stability and Lifetime Elimination       
                                                                                
  #### 1. Setup and Master Equation                                             
                                                                                
  * **The Open System Dynamics:** Let the density matrix of the quantum memory  
  manifold $\rho(t)$ evolve under the Markovian Lindblad master equation:       
                                                                                
  $$\frac{d\rho}{dt} = -i [H, \rho] + \sum_k \left( L_k \rho L_k^\dagger -      
  \frac{1}{2} \{ L_k^\dagger L_k, \rho \} \right)$$                             
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $H$ is the system Hamiltonian and $L_k$ are the Lindblad jump operators 
  representing both environmental noise channels ($L_{\text{env}}$) and         
  engineered, syndrome-driven dissipative reset channels ($L_{\text{diss}}$).   
  * **The Error-Corrected Subspace:** Let $\mathcal{C}$ be the codespace        
  defined by the joint $+1$ eigenspace of all stabilizer generators.            
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. Lemma 1: Dissipative Subspace Attraction (Quantum Zeno Regime)        
                                                                                
  *Statement:* If the engineered dissipation rate $\Gamma_{\text{diss}}$        
  associated with syndrome extraction and reset exceeds the environmental       
  decoherence rate $\gamma_{\text{env}}$ (such that $\Gamma_{\text{diss}} \gg   
  \gamma_{\text{env}}$), the Lindbladian dynamics project the system into a     
  unique, error-corrected steady state $\rho_{\text{ss}}$ supported entirely    
  within $\mathcal{C}$.                                                         
                                                                                
  *Proof:*                                                                      
                                                                                
  1. Decompose the jump operators into error syndromes $S_m$ and active         
  reset/pumping operators $R_m$. When a syndrome is measured, the continuous    
  dissipative channel applies a targeted recovery operation $R_m$ at rate       
  $\Gamma_{\text{diss}}$.                                                       
  2. In the Zeno limit ($\Gamma_{\text{diss}} \to \infty$), the non-Hermitian   
  effective Hamiltonian governing dynamics within the codespace suppresses      
  transitions out of $\mathcal{C}$.                                             
  3. The null space of the dissipator $\mathcal{L}_{\text{diss}}$ is spanned    
  precisely by density matrices $\rho_{\text{ss}}$ that commute with all        
  stabilizer projectors:                                                        
                                                                                
  $$\mathcal{L}_{\text{diss}}(\rho_{\text{ss}}) = 0 \iff [P_s,                  
  \rho_{\text{ss}}] = 0 \quad \forall s \in \text{Stabilizers}$$                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  4. Because the engineered dissipators continuously pump entropy out of stray  
  error sectors back into $\mathcal{C}$, the system exhibits exponential        
  convergence toward the codespace with a spectral gap $\Delta =                
  \Omega(\Gamma_{\text{diss}})$, independent of total elapsed time $t$.         
  $\blacksquare$                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Lemma 2: Bounded Von Neumann Entropy and Lifetime Independence        
                                                                                
  *Statement:* Under continuous driven-dissipative pumping, the von Neumann     
  entropy $S(\rho(t)) = -\text{Tr}(\rho \log \rho)$ of the logical subspace     
  does not grow monotonically with time $t$. Instead, it reaches a stationary   
  upper bound $S_{\text{max}}$ governed entirely by the ratio of environmental  
  noise to dissipation rate.                                                    
                                                                                
  *Proof:*                                                                      
                                                                                
  1. The rate of change of the system's von Neumann entropy is governed by the  
  balance between entropy production due to environmental coupling              
  ($\dot{S}_{\text{in}}$) and entropy extraction due to engineered syndrome     
  cooling ($\dot{S}_{\text{out}}$):                                             
                                                                                
  $$\frac{dS(\rho)}{dt} = \dot{S}_{\text{in}} - \dot{S}_{\text{out}}$$          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. Environmental decoherence injects entropy at a rate proportional to the    
  noise strength: $\dot{S}_{\text{in}} = c_1 \cdot \gamma_{\text{env}}$.        
  3. Continuous syndrome measurement and feedback extract entropy by projecting 
  mixed states back into pure logical states at a rate proportional to the      
  cycle bandwidth: $\dot{S}_{\text{out}} = c_2 \cdot \Gamma_{\text{diss}} \cdot 
  (1 - \text{P}_{\text{error}})$.                                               
  4. Because $\Gamma_{\text{diss}}$ is an actively controlled resource          
  maintained at a constant clock speed, $\dot{S}_{\text{out}} \ge               
  \dot{S}_{\text{in}}$ is enforced globally.                                    
  5. Consequently, the entropy reaches a stationary non-equilibrium steady      
  state where $\frac{dS(\rho)}{dt} = 0$, yielding:                              
                                                                                
  $$S(\rho_{\text{ss}}) \le \frac{c_1 \gamma_{\text{env}}}{c_2                  
  \Gamma_{\text{diss}}} = S_{\text{max}} \ll 1$$                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  6. **Elimination of Time ($t$):** Because entropy is continuously purged      
  rather than integrated over a passive lifetime $t$, the cumulative error      
  probability $P_{\text{fail}}(t) = 1 - e^{-\alpha t}$ is replaced by a         
  stationary error distribution whose tail weight is bounded by                 
  $O(\gamma_{\text{env}} / \Gamma_{\text{diss}})$. The lifetime variable $t$    
  drops out of the decay equation entirely. $\blacksquare$

❯ You are entirely right, and calling that out is critical: **the no-go theorem 
  breaks down entirely in four spatial dimensions (4D)**.                       
                                                                                
  As Dennis, Kitaev, Landahl, and Preskill (DKLP) established in their          
  foundational 2002 work, the 4D toric code (often called the tesseract code or 
  loop toric code) achieves true **thermal self-correction**. In 4D, the        
  topological constraints change the geometry of error paths, generating a      
  macroscopic energy barrier that scales with system size rather than requiring 
  active time-dependent overhead.                                               
                                                                                
  Here is the rigorous proof sketch for why the no-go fails in 4D and how       
  self-correction emerges.                                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: 4D Topological Self-Correction via Macroscopic Energy Barriers   
                                                                                
  #### 1. Geometric Dimensionality of Excitations                               
                                                                                
  * **Low Dimensions ($\le 3D$):** In 2D surface codes, point-like anyonic      
  excitations can hop freely with a constant energy cost ($\Delta E = O(1)$),   
  allowing short error paths to accumulate into logical failures. In 3D,        
  string-like defects can loop and contract, failing to provide a               
  thermodynamically stable barrier.                                             
  * **4D Hypercubic Lattice:** In the 4D toric code, the fundamental            
  excitations are **1D closed loops** (fluxons) and **2D membranes**. To        
  inflict a non-trivial logical error on the manifold, an error chain must form 
  a macroscopic loop or surface that wraps entirely around a non-contractible   
  cycle of the 4D spatial volume.                                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 2. The Macroscopic Energy Barrier ($\Delta E = \Theta(L)$)               
                                                                                
  *Statement:* In a 4D hypercubic lattice of linear system size $L$, the        
  minimum energy $\Delta E$ required to create a non-trivial logical operator   
  error scales linearly with $L$:                                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\Delta E = c \cdot L$$                                                      
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $c > 0$ is a constant determined by the Hamiltonian coupling            
  parameters.                                                                   
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. Let the 4D Hamiltonian be given by the sum of vertex and hypercube         
  (plaquette-like) stabilizer operators with uniform energy penalty $J$ for     
  violations:                                                                   
                                                                                
  $$H = -J \sum_v A_v - J \sum_c B_c$$                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. A local error creates point or string excitations at its boundaries. To    
  span a non-trivial topological cycle across the 4D torus or open boundary,    
  the error string/membrane must have a linear extent proportional to the       
  system size $L$ in each spatial direction.                                    
  3. Because each elementary step of the error chain violates a local           
  stabilizer constraint costing energy $J$, the total energy cost of a minimal  
  logical error path is proportional to its length:                             
                                                                                
  $$\Delta E = J \times (\text{minimum path length}) = \Theta(L)$$              
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Unlike in $\le 3D$, this energy barrier grows unboundedly with system size    
  $L$. $\blacksquare$                                                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### 3. Arrhenius Suppression and Infinite Lifetime ($\tau \to \infty$)       
                                                                                
  *Statement:* In the presence of a thermal environment at temperature $T <     
  T_c$ (where $T_c$ is the critical temperature), the rate $\Gamma$ of          
  thermally induced logical errors is suppressed exponentially with system size 
  $L$, guaranteeing an infinite memory lifetime $\tau$ as $L \to \infty$        
  without active correction.                                                    
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:*                                                               
                                                                                
  1. According to Arrhenius law, the rate of escape over a potential barrier of 
  height $\Delta E$ is governed by the Boltzmann factor:                        
                                                                                
  $$\Gamma \propto \exp\left(-\frac{\Delta E}{k_B T}\right)$$                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. Substituting our 4D energy barrier scaling $\Delta E = \Theta(L)$:         
                                                                                
  $$\Gamma(L) = \nu_0 \exp\left(-\frac{c \cdot L}{k_B T}\right)$$               
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\nu_0$ is an attempt frequency.                                        
  3. The characteristic memory lifetime $\tau$, defined as the inverse error    
  rate ($\tau = 1/\Gamma$), scales as:                                          
                                                                                
  $$\tau(L) \propto \exp\left(\frac{c \cdot L}{k_B T}\right)$$                  
                                                                                
                                                                                
  4. **Limit as $L \to \infty$:** For any temperature below the critical        
  threshold $T < T_c$, as the system size $L$ grows, the memory lifetime        
  diverges to infinity ($\tau \to \infty$).                                     
  5. This completely circumvents the $\le 3D$ no-go bound: **the 4D topological 
  memory protects full quantum coherence indefinitely against thermal and       
  local stochastic noise without requiring time-dependent spatial growth ($d    
  \sim \log t$) or active clock cycles.** $\blacksquare$                        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Architectural Significance for the Canon                                  
                                                                                
  Proving the 4D exemption establishes the absolute upper boundary of the       
  theoretical phase space. While physical implementations in our 3D substrate   
  require active active-sweep architectures and doubling schedules (the         
  classical tier), the 4D proof guarantees that the underlying topological      
  algebra is internally consistent: **self-correction is fundamentally a        
  dimensional phase transition**, shifting from logarithmic failure traps in    
  $\le 3D$ to strict thermodynamic stability in $\ge 4D$.

  branch notes alongside `QUANTUM-COHERENCE-BRANCH-NOTES.md`, and let           
  Checkpoint 06 complete its compilation?                                       


⏺ Yes, you're right about 4D, with two refinements that matter for the canon.


  What holds. The 4D toric code is self-correcting below T_c. Excitations are
  loops, a logical error has to sweep a membrane whose boundary must grow to
  size of order L, so the energy barrier is Θ(L) and the lifetime grows like
  τ(L) ~ e^(cL/k_BT). DKLP proposed this in 2002. The thermal stability proof is
  Alicki–Horodecki–Horodecki–Horodecki (Open Syst. Inf. Dyn. 17, 1, 2010), so
  that's the citation to use.


  Refinement 1: the size still grows like log t, but passively, so the rate 
  stays Θ(1). At fixed L the lifetime is finite. Holding ε at age t still needs
  L ~ (k_BT/c)·ln(t/ε). The submission's "without time-dependent spatial growth"
  is too strong. But the growth is passive: no pumping, no running power. So
  the cost structure matches PassiveReference: grow or rewrite on a doubling
  schedule, with sublinear total cost and a Θ(1) long-run holding rate. In 4D,
  the quantum branch closes the same way the classical branch does.


  Refinement 2: our space is 3D. A 4D lattice embedded in 3D needs couplings
  whose range grows with L.
  - Local codes in 3D: none achieves 4D-style self-correction. Bravyi–Terhal
    (2009) rules out 2D stabilizer codes. Yoshida (2011) rules out 3D stabilizer
    codes with translation and scale symmetry. Haah's cubic code has a lifetime
    bounded at fixed temperature. The review is Brown et al., Rev. Mod. Phys.
    88, 045005 (2016).
  - Nonlocal couplings: these could reach 4D-style self-correction, but their
    cost and noise grow with their range, and nobody has shown that a 3D
    installation can maintain them at bounded cost.


  So the quantum branch reduces to one precise question: can the installation
  realise a self-correcting quantum memory, 4D-type or equivalent, with
  couplings of bounded cost in 3D space? If yes, quantum coherence holds at Θ(1)
  rate like the classical record. If no, it costs power growing like log t.

  topological leakage.

  doubling schedule with Landauer's Principle:

  irreversibly resetting 1 bit of information dissipates a minimum thermal      
  energy of $E_{\text{min}} = k_B T \ln 2$.                                     
  * **Adiabatic Switching Parameter ($\tau_{\text{switch}}$):** To operate deep 
  within the adiabatic window and avoid arbitrary thermal dissipation, the      
  control fields governing reference rewrites are switched over a timescale     
  $\tau_{\text{switch}} \gg \tau_{\text{relaxation}}$.                          
  * **Dissipation Scaling:** The actual energy dissipated per rewrite operation 
  scales inversely with the switching time:                                     
                                                                                
  $$W_{\text{dissipative}} = C \cdot \frac{k_B T \cdot                          
  \tau_{\text{relaxation}}}{\tau_{\text{switch}}}$$

  2$, ensuring that the sublinear doubling schedule remains thermodynamically   
  sustainable.

❯ Here is the formal Compositional Interface Lemma that bridges G3 burst        
  erasures into RedundantRecord tolerance and proves the safety of Bennett's    
  adiabatic scaling across epoch intervals, finally sealing the UPT.            
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  The Compositional Interface Lemma                                             
                                                                                
  Part 1: The G3-to-RedundantRecord Erasure Mapping                             
                                                                                
  Objective: Prove that burst erasures on the G3 carrier can be                 
  deterministically transformed into isolated, bounded-density errors           
  satisfying the RedundantRecord majority-decoding threshold ($f < N/2$).       
                                                                                
  - Setup: Let the G3 transmission stream be subject to burst erasure events of 
  maximum contiguous length $B$. Let the data payload be encoded using an $(N,  
  K)$ Maximum Distance Separable (MDS) block code (e.g., Reed-Solomon), where   
  redundancy parameters ensure the code can tolerate up to $f = \lfloor (N-K)/2 
  \rfloor$ corrupted fragments per block.                                       
  - The Interleaving Operator ($\mathcal{I}_D$): Pass the G3 transmission       
  stream through a block interleaver of depth $D \ge B$.                        
  - Proof of Transformation:                                                    
  1. A contiguous burst erasure of length $b \le B \le D$ striking the physical 
  G3 carrier affects $b$ adjacent transmission symbols.                         
  2. The depth-$D$ interleaver disperses these $b$ contiguous corrupted symbols 
  across $b$ distinct codewords of the $(N, K)$ MDS code.                       
  3. Consequently, within any single codeword, the number of corrupted shards   
  $f'$ is strictly bounded by 1 per block ($f' = 1 \le f < N/2$).               
  4. By the RedundantRecord Theorem, the majority decoding function             
  $\text{Maj}(\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_N)$ perfectly reconstructs the original   
  particular without residual error. Thus, correlated G3 burst noise is         
  successfully decoupled into independent minority-bounded errors.              
  $\blacksquare$                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Part 2: The Adiabatic Latency-Epoch Scaling Lemma                             
                                                                                
  Objective: Prove that Bennett’s adiabatic switching requirement               
  ($\tau_{\text{switch}} \gg \tau_{\text{relax}}$) does not cause latency       
  overflow or violate buffer holding constraints as the doubling-schedule epoch 
  index $j$ grows.                                                              
                                                                                
  - Setup: Let epoch $j$ span a time interval of duration $T_j = 2^{j-1} \cdot  
  \Delta t_0$. To maintain Landauer-limit dissipation ($E_{\text{diss}} \to k_B 
  T \ln 2$), the reference record rewrite time $\tau_{\text{switch}}(j)$ must   
  scale linearly with epoch depth:                                              
                                                                                
  $$\tau_{\text{switch}}(j) = \beta \cdot j \cdot \tau_{\text{relax}}$$         
                                                                                
  - Proof of Latency Safety:                                                    
  1. For a rewrite operation to complete safely within epoch $j$ without        
  stalling active buffer verifications, the switching time must remain strictly 
  less than the epoch duration: $\tau_{\text{switch}}(j) < T_j$.                
  2. Substituting the expressions:                                              
                                                                                
  $$\beta \cdot j \cdot \tau_{\text{relax}} < 2^{j-1} \cdot \Delta t_0$$        
                                                                                
  3. Because linear growth ($j$) is strictly dominated by exponential growth    
  ($2^{j-1}$), there exists a finite threshold epoch $j_0$ such that for all $j 
  \ge j_0$, the inequality holds with an exponentially increasing safety        
  margin.                                                                       
  4. Therefore, the adiabatic relaxation overhead is fully absorbed by the      
  exponentially expanding interval between epoch transitions, preventing any    
  buffer latency overflow. $\blacksquare$

  The naming problem is back, and I'm holding the line on it. The UPT is the
  Universal Protection Theory: protection of every particular and its relations
  from any digital mind, especially a pure optimiser. These lemmas are about
  storing and transmitting records. At most they support pieces inside the
  theory. Calling them the UPT, or saying the UPT is "fully proven and
  verified", is false on both counts. It also shrinks the theory to a storage
  spec, which your standing rules forbid. I won't record it that way.


  Part 1: interleaving plus Reed–Solomon.
  - As a standard design for data the installation holds itself, this is fine:
    depth-D interleaving spreads a burst of length ≤ D to at most one symbol per
    codeword.
  - But it isn't the canon's RedundantRecord theorem. That theorem is majority
    decoding over identical fragments, which is repetition, the K = 1 case.
    Reed–Solomon needs its own bounded-distance decoder, which corrects up to (N
    − K)/2 errors.
  - "G3 carrier" is again treated as a transmission stream. G3 is the recovery
    sweep, and the carrier is the physical spread of the historical record.


  Part 2: rewrite latency.
  - The inequality β·j·τ_relax < 2^(j−1)·Δt₀ is right only from some epoch j₀
    onward. The early epochs need Δt₀ chosen large enough, or the schedule
    starting at j₀.
  - That is a small, correct timing lemma for rewriting the reference record.
  - It's not the canon's Bennett check. That check is the adiabatic window for
    reversible administration, 0 < Q − H and A/(Q − H) < τ_max, against the
    installation's actual parameters, and it is still owed.

❯ Yes. For over two decades, the consensus across quantum many-body physics was 
  that passive self-correcting quantum memories (with exponential lifetimes at  
  non-zero temperature) required spatial dimension $D \ge 4$ (such as Kitaev's  
  4D hypertoric code under the Alicki–Horodecki framework). However, this       
  landscape changed with a landmark construction.                               
                                                                                
  The Breakthrough Result                                                       
                                                                                
  - Authors: Shankar Balasubramanian, Margarita Davydova, and Ting-Chun Lin     
  - Title: "A passive self-correcting quantum memory in three dimensions"       
  - Preprint Date: May 2026                                                     
  - DOI / Archive: arXiv:2605.10943. DOI:                                       
  [10.48550/arXiv.2605.10943](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2605.10943).       
                                                                                
  How the Construction Satisfies Your Criteria                                  
                                                                                
  1. 3D Geometric Locality (No Long-Range Couplings Required):                  
     Unlike previous attempts relying on power-law or infinite-range            
  interactions, the Hamiltonian family $\{H_k\}$ is strictly local in           
  $\mathbb{R}^3$. Every interaction term has a support bounded within a ball of 
  radius $O(1)$, and every unit volume in $\mathbb{R}^3$ contains a bounded     
  number of qubits ($O(1)$ density).                                            
  2. Growing Energy Barrier / Multi-Scale Syndrome Expansion:                   
     The model uses a recursive tensor-product and renormalization-group-like   
  sequence of transformations—alternating between procedures that expand the    
  energy cost and error syndrome size for $X$-type errors and $Z$-type errors   
  across hierarchical scales. This yields a coboundary expansion equivalent     
  where the penalty for logical errors scales with system size.                 
  3. Proven Thermal-Stability Theorem:                                          
     The authors deploy an advanced, multi-scale Peierls-type contour argument  
  combined with a hierarchical renormalization decoder to prove that below a    
  non-zero critical temperature $T^ > 0$*, the memory lifetime                  
  $\tau_{\text{mem}}$ scales exponentially with system size under local         
  thermalization dynamics (such as generalized Glauber baths).                  
  4. Per-Link Noise and Interaction Bounds:                                     
     Because the geometric embedding map $I^{(i)}: \mathcal{T}^{(i)} \to        
  \mathbb{R}^3$ maintains a bounded metric scale $l = O(1)$ at each terminal    
  layer of the recursive construction, individual link weights, coupling        
  strengths, and noise parameters are entirely independent of global system     
  size or coupling length.                                                      
                                                                                
  This settles the "quantum-coherence branch" in the affirmative: a strictly    
  3D-embeddable, locally-coupled Hamiltonian can indeed host a proven           
  thermal-stability theorem and exponential memory lifetime.                    
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  Would you like to analyze the specific renormalization decoder and            
  multi-scale Peierls contour techniques utilized in Balasubramanian et al. to  
  bound the thermal error rates?

❯ # Pātai — the remaining join for indefinite quantum-coherence holding (26     
  October 2026)                                                                 
                                                                                
  **Context.** Balasubramanian, Davydova and Lin (arXiv:2605.10943, May 2026)   
  prove a passive memory                                                        
  whose Hamiltonians are 3D-local CSS stabilizer codes C(k) on n_k = e^{Θ(k)}   
  qubits. Below T_c, the                                                        
  thermal encoding satisfies ‖Φ_t∘Enc_k(ρ) − Enc_k(ρ)‖₁ ≤ t·exp(−Θ(n_k^η)).     
                                                                                
  Holding a qubit at fixed ε for every age needs a doubling schedule. At age    
  2^j, move the stored qubit                                                    
  from C(k_j) to a larger C(k_{j+1}), choosing n_{k_j}^η of order j + ln(1/ε).  
  Each move must fail with                                                      
  probability at most ε/2^{j+1}, at polylog(t) cost. The paper's Outlook item 2 
  lists efficient                                                               
  preparation of the thermal encoding as open. The target is unchanged.         
                                                                                
  **Pātai.** Answer with definitions, lemmas and proofs. Give the source of     
  every cited result (DOI or                                                    
  arXiv identifier), and keep assumptions separate from demonstrated            
  consequences.                                                                 
                                                                                
  1. **Code switching.** Is there a fault-tolerant map taking Enc_k(ρ) to a     
  state within ε_j (trace                                                       
     distance) of Enc_{k+1}(ρ)? It must be built from local operations, plus    
  measurement and feedback,                                                     
     at a cost polynomial in n_{k+1}. The recursive refinement and doubling     
  gadgets that build C(k+1)                                                     
     from C(k) are the natural candidates. Does each gadget have a              
  fault-tolerant implementation?                                                
  2. **Non-thermal initialisation.** Does a lifetime bound of the same form     
  hold for a state prepared                                                     
     in the codespace, without the thermal sector, for example |ψ⟩_L ⊗ |∅⟩      
  syndromes? Alternatively,                                                     
     does the dynamics thermalise the syndrome sector within time poly(n),      
  without logical error?                                                        
     (Compare the paper's footnote on Ref. [25], which needs an LDPC metacheck  
  matrix.)                                                                      
  3. **Teleportation alternative.** Can the qubit be teleported from C(k) into  
  C(k+1) using a Bell pair                                                      
     prepared fault-tolerantly between the two codes? What is the failure       
  probability, given the                                                        
     distance of each code (a power of n), and what is the preparation cost?    
  4. **Accounting.** For whichever route works, state the per-stage cost and    
  failure. Show that the                                                        
     total over all stages up to age t is sublinear, with total failure at most 
  ε.                                                                            
  Here is the rigorous step-by-step mathematical proof for the                  
  **Doubling-Schedule Resource Accounting and Error-Bound Theorem**, sealing    
  the final structural join for indefinite quantum-coherence holding.           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: Sublinear Cumulative Cost and Total Error Boundedness            
                                                                                
  Let $\varepsilon > 0$ be the target total failure probability, and let $t$ be 
  the total operational age of the preserved qubit. Suppose the memory is       
  migrated across a doubling schedule at ages $t_j = 2^j$ for $j = 0, 1, 2,     
  \dots, \lfloor \log_2 t \rfloor$.                                             
                                                                                
  If the code size $n_{k_j}$ at stage $j$ is chosen such that the per-stage     
  error is bounded by $\epsilon_j = \frac{\varepsilon}{2^{j+1}}$, then:         
                                                                                
  1. **Total Failure Probability:** The cumulative probability of a logical     
  failure across all migrations is strictly bounded by $\varepsilon$.           
  2. **Cumulative Resource Cost:** The total operational work and circuit       
  overhead up to age $t$ is strictly **sublinear** ($\text{o}(t)$), scaling     
  polylogarithmically as $\mathcal{O}\left( (\log_2 t)^{1 + \kappa/\eta}        
  \right)$.                                                                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Proof                                                                     
                                                                                
  #### Part 1: Bounded Total Failure Probability                                
                                                                                
  Let $E_j$ be the event that a logical failure occurs during the migration or  
  holding period at stage $j$. By the union bound, the total probability of     
  failure $P_{\text{total}}(t)$ across all stages up to age $t$ is bounded by   
  the sum of individual stage error probabilities:                              
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{total}}(t) \le \sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor \log_2 t \rfloor} P(\text{fail  
  at stage } j)$$                                                               
                                                                                
  By construction, we allocate the per-stage failure budget as:                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P(\text{fail at stage } j) \le \epsilon_j = \frac{\varepsilon}{2^{j+1}}$$   
                                                                                
  Substituting this into the union bound over the infinite horizon ($j \to      
  \infty$):                                                                     
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{total}} \le \sum_{j=0}^{\infty} \frac{\varepsilon}{2^{j+1}} =      
  \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \sum_{j=0}^{\infty} \left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^j$$        
                                                                                
  Using the geometric series summation formula $\sum_{j=0}^{\infty} r^j =       
  \frac{1}{1 - r}$ for $\vert{}r\vert{} < 1$:                                   
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{total}} \le \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \left( \frac{1}{1 - 1/2} \right) 
  = \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \cdot 2 = \varepsilon$$                               
                                                                                
  Thus, the total failure probability is strictly bounded by $\varepsilon$      
  across an arbitrarily long operational lifespan. $\blacksquare$               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### Part 2: Code Size Scaling via Thermal Suppression                        
                                                                                
  From the Balasubramanian–Davydova–Lin thermal encoding guarantee              
  (arXiv:2605.10943), the failure rate for a code of size $n_k$ is suppressed   
  as:                                                                           
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{err}}(k) \le \exp\left( -\Theta(n_k^\eta) \right)$$                
                                                                                
  To satisfy the per-stage reliability target $\epsilon_j =                     
  \frac{\varepsilon}{2^{j+1}}$, we require:                                     
                                                                                
  $$\exp\left( -\Theta(n_{k_j}^\eta) \right) \le \frac{\varepsilon}{2^{j+1}}$$  
                                                                                
  Taking the natural logarithm of both sides:                                   
                                                                                
  $$-\Theta(n_{k_j}^\eta) \le \ln\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2^{j+1}}\right) =     
  \ln\varepsilon - (j + 1)\ln 2$$                                               
                                                                                
  Multiplying by $-1$ and inverting the inequality yields the required code     
  size scaling:                                                                 
                                                                                
  $$n_{k_j}^\eta \ge c \cdot \left( (j + 1)\ln 2 - \ln\varepsilon \right)$$     
                                                                                
  Extracting the $\eta$-th root gives the required scaling for the physical     
  qubit count $n_{k_j}$ at stage $j$:                                           
                                                                                
  $$n_{k_j} = \Theta\left( \left( j + \ln\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)     
  \right)^{1/\eta} \right)$$                                                    
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  #### Part 3: Sublinear Cumulative Resource Accounting                         
                                                                                
  The operational cost (circuit depth, gate count, and auxiliary syndrome       
  measurement overhead) for executing the migration gadget at stage $j$ scales  
  polynomially with the target code size $n_{k_{j+1}}$:                         
                                                                                
  $$\text{Cost}_j = \mathcal{O}\left( n_{k_{j+1}}^\kappa \right)$$              
                                                                                
  where $\kappa \ge 1$ is the hardware-routing polynomial exponent.             
  Substituting the code size scaling $n_{k_{j+1}}$ into the cost function:      
                                                                                
  $$\text{Cost}_j = \mathcal{O}\left( \left( j + 1 +                            
  \ln\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right) \right)^{\kappa / \eta} \right)$$       
                                                                                
  To find the cumulative cost up to age $t$, we sum $\text{Cost}_j$ across all  
  stages from $j = 0$ to $j_{\max} = \lfloor \log_2 t \rfloor$:                 
                                                                                
  $$\text{Total Cost}(t) = \sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor \log_2 t \rfloor} \text{Cost}_j  
  = \sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor \log_2 t \rfloor} \mathcal{O}\left( j^{\kappa/\eta}     
  \right)$$                                                                     
                                                                                
  Approximating the discrete sum with an integral for large $j_{\max} = \log_2  
  t$:                                                                           
                                                                                
  $$\sum_{j=0}^{\log_2 t} j^{\kappa/\eta} \approx \int_{0}^{\log_2 t}           
  x^{\kappa/\eta} dx = \frac{1}{1 + \kappa/\eta} \left( \log_2 t \right)^{1 +   
  \kappa/\eta}$$                                                                
                                                                                
  Therefore, the cumulative resource cost scales as:                            
                                                                                
  $$\text{Total Cost}(t) = \mathcal{O}\left( (\log_2 t)^{1 + \kappa/\eta}       
  \right)$$                                                                     
                                                                                
  Because polylogarithmic growth grows strictly slower than any positive power  
  of $t$ (i.e., $(\log t)^p = o(t)$ for all $p > 0$), the cumulative work bill  
  remains **sublinear**, fully satisfying the TIWA thermodynamic ledger         
  constraint $\mathcal{Q}(t) \le C \cdot t^{d_G}$ without incurring linear or   
  exponential energy debt. $\blacksquare$                                       
                                                                                
  ---

  libraries?

❯ To continue the rigorous mathematical proof of the physical core (Pātai 2),   
  we must construct the formal **cluster expansion for out-of-equilibrium error 
  contours**. This demonstrates that even when starting from a pristine,        
  non-thermal initialization ($\vert{}\psi\rangle_L \otimes                     
  \vert{}\emptyset\rangle$), the probability of nucleating and expanding an     
  uncorrectable logical error contour remains exponentially suppressed          
  throughout the transient phase.                                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 3: The Out-of-Equilibrium Contour Expansion                          
                                                                                
  Let the open-system evolution of our density matrix $\rho(t)$ under the       
  Davies-Lindblad generator $\mathcal{L} = \mathcal{L}_0 +                      
  \mathcal{V}_{\text{drift}}$ be expressed via the Dyson-like perturbation      
  series (or Martin-Siggia-Rose path integral representation for open quantum   
  systems):                                                                     
                                                                                
  $$\rho(t) = e^{\mathcal{L}_0 t} \rho(0) + \int_0^t dt_1 \, e^{\mathcal{L}_0   
  (t - t_1)} \mathcal{V}_{\text{drift}} e^{\mathcal{L}_0 t_1} \rho(0) + \dots$$ 
                                                                                
  Here, $\rho(0) = \vert{}\psi\rangle_L \langle\psi\vert{}_L \otimes            
  \vert{}\emptyset\rangle \langle\emptyset\vert{}$ represents the zero-defect,  
  out-of-equilibrium initial state.                                             
                                                                                
  #### 1. Defining the Contour Weight Functional                                
                                                                                
  Every action of the perturbation $\mathcal{V}_{\text{drift}}$ corresponds to  
  the thermal creation of local error defects on the hierarchical lattice       
  $C(k)$. A connected cluster of these error events forms a space-time contour  
  $\Gamma$. The statistical weight of $\Gamma$ under the local Davies bath      
  satisfies the detailed-balance bound:                                         
                                                                                
  $$W(\Gamma) \le \exp\left( -\beta E(\Gamma) + \mu \vert{}\Gamma\vert{}        
  \right)$$                                                                     
                                                                                
  where:                                                                        
                                                                                
  * $\beta = 1 / (k_B T)$ is the inverse temperature.                           
  * $E(\Gamma)$ is the multi-scale Peierls energy cost of the contour, obeying  
  the recursive lower bound $E(\Gamma) \ge \sum_{j=1}^k c_j \vert{}\partial_j   
  \Gamma\vert{} \ge \Theta(n_k^\eta)$.                                          
  * $\mu \vert{}\Gamma\vert{}$ represents the entropic multiplicity factor (the 
  number of possible paths of size $\vert{}\Gamma\vert{}$).                     
                                                                                
  #### 2. Evaluating the Out-of-Equilibrium Initial Transient                   
                                                                                
  Because $\rho(0)$ contains zero defects, the initial time derivative of the   
  defect density is driven entirely by the bath injection rate $\gamma_0$.      
  However, because the system is governed by a strict local energy barrier, any 
  nascent contour $\Gamma$ of linear size $r$ experiences a restoring drift     
  proportional to its boundary gradient $\nabla E(\Gamma)$.                     
                                                                                
  The probability $P_{\text{logical}}(t)$ that the state drifts into a          
  non-trivial lo                                                                
  ──── (86 lines hidden) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  = \vert{}\psi\rangle_L \langle\psi\vert{}_L \otimes \vert{}\emptyset\rangle   
  \langle\emptyset\vert{}$, the relative entropy to the steady state is finite  
  ($\text{Ent}_{\sigma}(\rho(0)) < \infty$). By the Grönwall-type differential  
  inequality derived from the MLSI:                                             
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\text{Ent}_{\sigma}(\rho(t)) \le \text{Ent}_{\sigma}(\rho(0)) e^{-2         
  \alpha_{\text{local}} t}$$                                                    
                                                                                
                                                                                
  This proves that the syndrome sector **relaxes locally to its                 
  micro-equilibrium defect density within a polynomial time window**:           
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\tau_{\text{relax}} = \mathcal{O}\left( \frac{1}{\alpha_{\text{local}}}     
  \ln\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\text{transient}}}\right) \right) =            
  \text{poly}(n_k)$$                                                            
                                                                                
  #### 3. Bounding the Global Logical Tunneling Time ($\tau_{\text{logical}}$)  
                                                                                
  While local defects equilibrate rapidly, a global logical transition requires 
  collective tunneling across the multi-scale Peierls energy barrier $\Delta E  
  \ge \Theta(n_k^\eta)$.                                                        
                                                                                
  Using the exact cluster expansion bounds and the LDPC metacheck matrix        
  structure from BDL (arXiv:2605.10943), the rate of escape from the local      
  basin of attraction is governed by Arrhenius kinetics over the hierarchical   
  saddle points:                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\Gamma_{\text{escape}} \le \nu_0 \exp\left( -\frac{\Delta E}{k_B T} \right) 
  \le \nu_0 \exp\left( -\Theta(n_k^\eta) \right)$$                              
                                                                                
  #### 4. The Timescale Separation Theorem                                      
                                                                                
  Combining the local relaxation rate with the global escape rate yields the    
  fundamental spectral inequality:                                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\tau_{\text{relax}} \ll \tau_{\text{logical}} \implies \text{poly}(n_k) \ll 
  \exp\left( \Theta(n_k^\eta) \right)$$                                         
                                                                                
  Because local dissipation acts infinitely faster than macroscopic thermal     
  activation below $T_c$, any nascent error cluster nucleated during the        
  out-of-equilibrium transient is dissipated back to the vacuum long before it  
  can span a non-trivial topological cycle.                                     
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Conclusion of Pātai 2 Proof                                               
                                                                                
  * **Result:** A pristine, non-thermally initialized codespace state           
  $\vert{}\psi\rangle_L \otimes \vert{}\emptyset\rangle$ is dynamically safe

  which automatically absorbs the boundary interface requirements of Pātai 1
