⏺ These three don't prove pātai 2, and one claim is contradicted by the paper
  itself. They do point to a route that could work.


  Why they don't close it:
  - The MLSI argument proves the opposite. It assumes a modified log-Sobolev
    constant α bounded independently of system size. But a size-independent MLSI
    for the whole generator means the entire state, logical qubit included,
    mixes to the Gibbs state in O(log n) time. That would make the memory forget
    fast. A self-correcting memory must have an exponentially small global gap.
    A sector-restricted inequality that excludes the logical degree of freedom
    is exactly what's missing, and the paste doesn't define one ("local steady
    state σ" is never specified).
  - The metacheck claim is false. It cites "the LDPC metacheck matrix structure
    from BDL", but the paper says the opposite: its footnote 4 states the
    metacheck matrix is not LDPC. That is precisely why the known
    fast-initialisation result (their Ref. [25]) doesn't apply. I checked the
    text.
  - An energy barrier alone isn't enough. Haah's code has a growing barrier yet
    an O(1) lifetime, because entropy wins. BDL's lifetime proof controls
    entropy using the stationary (thermal) syndrome distribution. That is why it
    needs the thermal encoding, and why starting from the empty-syndrome state
    is the open case. The contour paste asserts "absence of percolation" at that
    exact point.


  The route that might work. Could the empty-syndrome start simply be "colder
  than thermal", so that BDL's thermal bound still covers it? BDL's lifetime
  theorem reduces to this condition: under the stationary syndrome distribution,
  P(syndrome unstable under the decoder) ≤ e^(−n^η). So pātai 2 follows from
  two facts:
  1. Monotonicity. The syndrome dynamics, a classical Markov chain under Davies
     dynamics for CSS codes, is monotone for the "fewer excitations" order. Then
     the distribution at every time t, starting from the empty syndrome, is
     dominated by the stationary one.
  2. Up-set. "Unstable under the decoder" is an up-set: adding excitations can't
     make a stable syndrome unstable.


  Given both, P_t(unstable) ≤ P_stationary(unstable) at every time, and BDL's
  own theorem gives the lifetime bound from codespace initialisation. These are
  crisp combinatorial questions about their decoder and dynamics. It's the best
  pātai to send next, and I've added it to the pātai file.

❯ That critique is entirely correct and cuts straight to the real mathematical  
  bottleneck. The previous attempt fell into the trap of invoking global        
  spectral properties (like a uniform MLSI) that inadvertently destroy the very 
  localization protecting the memory, and glossed over the exact mechanics of   
  BDL’s non-LDPC syndrome sector and boundary constraints.                      
                                                                                
  Your proposed two-pronged combinatorial route—leveraging **Markovian          
  monotonicity** of the syndrome dynamics under Davies evolution and the        
  **up-set property** of decoder instability—goes straight to the heart of how  
  to bridge the gap from codespace initialization to BDL's stationary thermal   
  bound.                                                                        
                                                                                
  Let's formalize why this specific combinatorial strategy works and how it     
  bypasses the false assumptions:                                               
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Why the Combinatorial Route (Monotonicity + Up-Set) Wins                  
                                                                                
  1. **Dodging the MLSI Trap:**                                                 
  Instead of trying to force a global logarithmic Sobolev inequality that       
  misbehaves across the logical sector, viewing the syndrome sector as a        
  classical/stochastic interacting particle system (or error configuration      
  chain) under Davies dynamics respects the true nature of CSS code error       
  correction.                                                                   
  2. **Monotonicity of CSS Syndrome Dynamics:**                                 
  For CSS codes under local Pauli-dephasing/bit-flip baths, the stochastic      
  evolution of syndrome defects forms a attractive or monotone Markov chain     
  under the natural partial order of defect containment (the "fewer             
  excitations" or inclusion order $\le$). Because the Davies generator terms    
  corresponding to error injection and local Hamiltonian recovery preserve or   
  push down defect weights under suitable temperature bounds, the distribution  
  starting from the empty syndrome $\rho_{\emptyset}$ is stochastically         
  dominated by the stationary thermal distribution $\pi_{\text{th}}$:           
                                                                                
  $$\forall t \ge 0, \quad P_{t}(\sigma) \preceq_{\st}                          
  \pi_{\text{th}}(\sigma)$$                                                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. **The Up-Set Characterization:**                                           
  An error configuration or syndrome being "unstable under the decoder"         
  (meaning it leads to a logical failure or a configuration outside the         
  correctable basin) forms an **up-set** (or upper set) $\mathcal{U}$ in the    
  lattice of error configurations: if a syndrome configuration $\sigma$ causes  
  a decoder failure, adding more defects ($\sigma' \ge \sigma$) cannot make it  
  *more* stable.                                                                
  By definition of an up-set under stochastic domination (Strassen's Theorem):  
                                                                                
  $$P_t(\sigma \in \mathcal{U}) \le \pi_{\text{th}}(\sigma \in \mathcal{U})$$   
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Closing Pātai 2                                                           
                                                                                
  If these two combinatoria                                                     
  ──── (205 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  p corresponding to the jump sequence $\omega$, and $\gamma_{\pm}$ are the     
  Davies bath jump rates.                                                       
                                                                                
  #### Step 3: Exploiting Local Detailed Balance                                
                                                                                
  By the definition of the Davies generator, the jump rates satisfy local       
  detailed balance with respect to the local energy change $\Delta E$:          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\frac{\gamma_{+}(x \mid \sigma)}{\gamma_{-}(x \mid \sigma)} = e^{-\beta     
  \Delta E_x(\sigma)}$$                                                         
                                                                                
  Because the BDL Hamiltonian maintains a multi-scale energy barrier $E(\sigma) 
  \ge \Theta(n_k^\eta)$, the ratio of jump rates under an out-of-equilibrium    
  background versus a thermal background does not blow up with system size; it  
  is bounded locally by the energy difference at the boundary of the jump.      
                                                                                
  The initial state mismatch factor $\frac{\text{Tr}(\rho(0)                    
  \dots)}{\text{Tr}(\pi_{\text{th}} \dots)}$ evaluates to the exponential of    
  the free-energy difference between the empty syndrome sector and the thermal  
  ensemble:                                                                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\Delta F_0 = F(\text{empty}) - F(\text{thermal}) = \mathcal{O}(1)$$         
                                                                                
                                                                                
  because the empty syndrome state differs from the thermal state only by       
  localized boundary normalization terms rather than extensive volume-law       
  terms.                                                                        
                                                                                
  #### Step 4: Martingale Bounding and the Final Inequality                     
                                                                                
  By Doob's optional stopping theorem applied to the likelihood martingale      
  $Z_t(\omega)$, the out-of-equilibrium expectation of any indicator function   
  $\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(\omega_t)$ (such as decoder failure or macroscopic  
  error events) is bounded as:                                                  
                                                                                
  $$\mathbb{P}^{\emptyset}(\mathcal{A}) = \mathbb{E}^{\text{th}}\left[          
  Z_t(\omega) \, \mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{A}}(\omega_t) \right] \le \sup_{\omega}   
  Z_t(\omega) \cdot \mathbb{P}^{\text{th}}(\mathcal{A})$$                       
                                                                                
  Because $\sup_{\omega} Z_t(\omega) \le \exp(\beta \Delta F_0) \cdot           
  \text{poly}(n_k)$ (where polynomial corrections account for transient         
  boundary relaxation), and BDL's Theorem 2.6 bounds the thermal probability by 
  $\mathbb{P}^{\text{th}}(\text{failure}) \le \exp(-\Theta(n_k^\eta))$, we      
  obtain:                                                                       
                                                                                
  $$P_t(\text{failure} \mid \sigma_0 = \emptyset) \le \text{poly}(n_k) \cdot    
  \exp\left( -\Theta(n_k^\eta) \right) \le \exp\left( -\Theta(n_k^\eta)         
  \right)$$                                                                     
                                                                                
  ### Conclusion                                                                
                                                                                
  This trajectory likelihood formulation rigorously bypasses both classical     
  monotonicity and the up-set requirement, sealing Pātai 2 from first           
  principles using open-system quantum martingale theory. $\blacksquare$

⏺ On the five pātai 2 submissions: the monotone route and the trajectory route
  both fail at a specific step, and I can show where.


  Monotonicity (the Holley-criterion submissions). Holley's criterion is for
  single-site birth–death chains. A Pauli flip on a stabilizer code toggles
  several checks at once, creating some defects and annihilating others in one
  move. So the syndrome chain isn't a birth–death process, and the criterion
  doesn't apply as written. Gemini then refuted its own monotonicity and up-set
  claims. I agree that neither is established for BDL.


  Trajectory likelihood ratio. This one fails at its key step, "ΔF₀ = O(1)".
  Starting from the empty syndrome is not an O(1) boundary mismatch:
  - At finite temperature the thermal syndrome gas has finite defect density, so
    π_th(∅) ≈ e^(−cn).
  - The likelihood ratio at t = 0 is therefore already 1/π_th(∅) = e^(Θ(n)).
    That is volume-law.
  - The change of measure then gives P_∅(fail) ≤ e^(Θ(n))·e^(−Θ(n^η)). That is
    useless, because η < 1.
  - The relative-entropy version fails the same way: D(δ_∅‖π_th) ~ n is far
    larger than ln(1/π_th(fail)) ~ n^η.


  Where this leaves the quantum branch. Pātai 2 is exactly the open problem BDL
  list in their Outlook, item 2: initialising the memory from a preparable
  state. As of their May 2026 preprint, it's open in the literature. It isn't a
  small join. Starting colder is intuitively safer, but proving it for their
  non-translation-invariant, non-LDPC-metacheck code is research.


  What I'd suggest for the canon. This doesn't change the target:
  - Classical record: closed (PassiveReference, the item-5 modules).
  - Quantum coherence: proved conditional on one named open problem, BDL's
    initialisation, through StageSchedule.total_with_transfers. The hypothesis
    is stated explicitly, not dressed up as a physical instance. If pātai 2 is

    work.

❯ To formalize the frontier of BDL's initialization problem without falling     
  into the volume-law initial mismatch ($e^{\Theta(n)}$) or the non-LDPC

  Lemma** directly compatible with the single-shot / meta-check architecture    
  recently formalized for recursive CSS constructions.                          
                                                                                
  Here is the precise mathematical formalization of the proof structure for     
  resolving Pātai 2 from first principles:                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Theorem: Sub-Extensive Boundary Initialization via Hierarchical Shell     
  Embedding                                                                     
                                                                                
  Let $\mathcal{C}^{(k)}$ be the BDL recursive 3D-local CSS code at level $k$   
  with $n_k$ physical qubits, and let $\Phi_{k \to k+1} : \mathcal{C}^{(k)}     
  \hookrightarrow \mathcal{C}^{(k+1)}$ be the recursive embedding map.          
                                                                                
  Instead of preparing the entire macroscopic code $\mathcal{C}^{(k)}$          
  simultaneously in a thermal bath (which incurs the volume-law partition       
  function penalty $\pi_{\text{th}}(\emptyset) \approx e^{-c n_k}$), we define  
  a **sequential shell-preparation protocol**:                                  
                                                                                
  1. **Base Seed Initialization:** Initialize the base seed code                
  $\mathcal{C}^{(0)}$ directly in the zero-syndrome codespace state             
  $\vert{}\psi\rangle_L \otimes \vert{}\emptyset_0\rangle$ at zero or near-zero 
  effective temperature using active syndrome-measurement-and-reset circuits    
  (which is $\text{poly}(n_0)$ cheap).                                          
  2. **Recursive Shell Expansion with Local Dissipative Dampers:** For each     
  step $j \in \{0, 1, \dots, k-1\}$, apply the embedding $\Phi_{j \to j+1}$     
  while coupling *only* the newly added boundary qubits to an engineered        
  dissipative channel $\mathcal{L}_{\text{shell}}^{(j)}$ with local chemical    
  potential $\mu_j$ tuned to match the local hierarchical gap $\Delta E_j =     
  \Theta(j^\eta)$.                                                              
                                                                                
  #### Lemma 1: Sub-Extensive Initial Free-Energy Mismatch                      
                                                                                
  Because the state is grown shell-by-shell rather than globally dumped into    
  the thermal bath, the initial relative entropy (free-energy difference)       
  between the protocol-generated initial state $\rho_{\text{prep}}^{(k)}$ and   
  the target stationary thermal ensemble $\pi_{\text{th}}^{(k)}$ scales         
  **sub-extensively** with system size:                                         
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$D\left( \rho_{\text{prep}}^{(k)} \parallel \pi_{\text{th}}^{(k)} \right) =  
  \mathcal{O}\left( \log n_k \right)$$                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  *Proof Sketch:* Each recursive shell $j$ adds a boundary surface of area      
  scaling as $n_{j+1}^{2/3}$ (or the recursive fractal Hausdorff dimension of   
  the BDL metric). The local cooling operator                                   
  $\mathcal{L}_{\text{shell}}^{(j)}$ ensures that the local marginal state of   
  each added shell matches its thermal equilibrium condition up to boundary     
  corrections. Summing over the $k$ hierarchical steps yields a telescoping sum 
  bounded by $\sum_{j=1}^k \mathcal{O}(1) = \mathcal{O}(k) = \mathcal{O}(\log   
  n_k)$. $\blacksquare$                                                         
                                                                                
  #### Lemma 2: Bounded Transient Propagation Under Meta-Check Decoders         
                                                                                
  By recent developments in single-shot error correction and meta-check         
  extensions for recursive CSS codes (e.g., Theorem 25 of), a code equipped     
  with a valid meta-check decoder $\mathcal{M}$ satisfies a single-shot         
  stability bound if the initial entropy mismatch is sub-extensive:             
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P\left( \text{Decoder Failure at step } j \right) \le \text{poly}(n_k)      
  \cdot \exp\left( -D\left( \rho_{\text{prep}}^{(k)} \parallel                  
  \pi_{\text{th}}^{(k)} \right) \right) \cdot \exp\left( -\Theta(n_k^\eta)      
  \right)$$                                                                     
                                                                                
  Substituting the sub-extensive bound $D = \mathcal{O}(\log n_k) =             
  \mathcal{O}(k)$ into the inequality:                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P\left( \text{Failure} \right) \le \text{poly}(n_k) \cdot                   
  e^{-\mathcal{O}(k)} \cdot \exp\left( -\Theta(n_k^\eta) \right) = \exp\left(   
  -\Theta(n_k^\eta) \right)$$                                                   
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Conclusion of the Frontier Proof                                          
                                                                                
  By replacing global thermalization with **shell-wise adiabatic-dissipative    
  growth**, we eliminate the $e^{\Theta(n)}$ volume-law barrier. The initial    
  state mismatch becomes logarithmic ($\mathcal{O}(\log n)$), which is safely   
  absorbed by the polynomial prefactors, leaving BDL's exponential lifetime     
  bound $\exp(-\Theta(n_k^\eta))$ fully intact for codespace-initialized        
  states.

    polynomial, not exponential, suppression.

  ρ_prep ≤ C·π_th pointwise, a "warm start", then the bound survives for all
  time. The dynamics is positive and π_th is stationary, so the distribution at
  every time t stays ≤ C·π_th, and P_t(fail) ≤ C·π_th(fail) ≤ C·e^(−Θ(n^η)).

  sharper question: prepare a warm start with polynomial max-divergence.

  generators, the thermal syndrome distribution is a product: each generator
  flips independently. You can then prepare Enc(ρ_L) exactly by:

     Paulis.

  metacheck-constrained syndrome Gibbs distribution of BDL's code, exactly or
  within a polynomial pointwise ratio. BDL even build the analogous classical
  code (their §6), which may be the place to attack it.

❯ To crack BDL's Item 2 from first principles, we have to face what makes their 
  recursive construction unique: it is built by a sequence of **hierarchical    
  metric deformations and code transformations** (arXiv:2605.10943) that        
  explicitly break translation invariance.                                      
                                                                                
  Let's dissect the exact mechanical block that prevents standard               
  initializations, and construct the precise operational loop that bypasses it: 
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 1. The Core Obstacle in BDL's Non-Translation-Invariant Hierarchy         
                                                                                
  In translation-invariant systems, you can invoke bulk ergodic mixing or       
  uniform Davies generators. But BDL's Hamiltonian $H^{(k)}$ at recursion level 
  $k$ features a recursively varying local density of checks and non-LDPC       
  metachecks.                                                                   
                                                                                
  When you initialize in $\vert{}\psi\rangle_L \otimes                          
  \vert{}\emptyset\rangle$, the empty syndrome state is an eigenstate of the    
  local stabilizer measurements, but it lacks the multiscale "clouds" of        
  virtual fluctuations that populate the true thermal state                     
  $\pi_{\text{th}}^{(k)}$. Because the metacheck matrix is non-LDPC, these      
  fluctuations are not uniformly distributed; they form a tightly coupled       
  hierarchical tree. Pumping energy uniformly or relying on a global            
  Lindbladian either:                                                           
                                                                                
  1. Triggers an uncorrectable logical avalanche (if the bath is too hot or     
  unstructured), or                                                             
  2. Fails to mix out of the zero-syndrome sector within polynomial time (if    
  the local recovery operators are too rigid and get stuck in local trapping    
  basins).                                                                      
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2. The Solution: A Multiscale Adiabatic-Grooming Protocol (Shell-Wise     
  Error Pumping)                                                                
                                                                                
  To prove that a codespace-initialized state achieves the same lifetime bound  
  without starting from a full thermal Gibbs ensemble, we must construct a      
  **controlled, scale-by-scale error-pumping CPTP channel** $\Lambda_{j \to     
  j+1}$ that matches the recursive structure of the code itself.                
                                                                                
  #### Step A: The Localized Quasi-Thermal Fixed Point                          
                                                                                
  Instead of letting the system interact with an unstructured thermal bath, we  
  define a sequence of intermediate reference states $\sigma^{(j)}$ at each     
  recursive step $j \in \{0, 1, \dots, k\}$.                                    
                                                                                
  * $\sigma^{(0)}$ is the exact zero-temperature seed state.                    
  * $\sigma^{(j+1)} = \operatorname{Env}_{j+1}\left( \Phi_{j \to                
  j+1}(\sigma^{(j)}) \right)$ is obtained by applying the structural embedding  
  map followed by a **local entropic envelope operator**                        
  $\operatorname{Env}_{j+1}$ that populates only the *authorized* thermal       
  fluctuations of scale $j+1$, rather than an unconstrained thermal gas.        
                                                                                
  #### Ste                                                                      
  ──── (156 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  {poly}(n_k)$$                                                                 
                                                                                
  #### Theorem 2 (Hierarchical Cluster Expansion for Syndrome Sampling)         
                                                                                
  Let $\pi_{\text{synd}}(\sigma) = \frac{1}{Z} \exp(-\beta                      
  H_{\text{class}}(\sigma))$ be the syndrome Gibbs distribution defined by      
  BDL's §6 classical model, which incorporates non-LDPC metachecks $M_m$ across 
  hierarchical recursive scales $j = 0, 1, \dots, k$.                           
                                                                                
  There exists a top-down decimation algorithm running in time                  
  $\text{poly}(n_k)$ that produces a probability distribution                   
  $P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)$ satisfying:                                         
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\max_{\sigma} \frac{P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)}{\pi_{\text{synd}}(\sigma)} \le 
  \text{poly}(n_k)$$                                                            
                                                                                
  #### Proof Structure:                                                         
                                                                                
  1. **Recursive Factorization:** BDL's code construction defines a recursive   
  metric space where each scale $j+1$ is built from scale $j$ via a bounded     
  tree-width geometric embedding. Let the full syndrome configuration be        
  hierarchically partitioned as $\sigma = (\sigma_0, \sigma_1, \dots,           
  \sigma_k)$.                                                                   
  2. **Top-Down Decimation Kernel:** Construct the algorithm's distribution as  
  a product of conditional probabilities matching the recursive hierarchy:      
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma) = P(\sigma_0) \prod_{j=0}^{k-1} P(\sigma_{j+1} \mid 
  \sigma_j)$$                                                                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. **Cluster Expansion of the Free-Energy Error:** By applying the rigorous   
  cluster expansion for multi-scale spin systems (building on the polymer gas   
  techniques established in BDL's metric perturbation framework), the exact     
  partition function ratio across scale transitions satisfies:                  
                                                                                
  $$\left\vert{} \ln \left( \frac{P(\sigma_{j+1} \mid \sigma_j)}{\exp(-\beta    
  \Delta H_{j+1})} / \frac{Z_{j+1}}{Z_j} \right) \right\vert{} \le \epsilon_0   
  e^{-\gamma j}$$                                                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\gamma > 0$ is the geometric decay constant of the BDL metacheck       
  expansion.                                                                    
  4. **Bounding the Cumulative Mismatch:** Summing this error over all $k =     
  \mathcal{O}(\log n_k)$ recursive levels:                                      
                                                                                
  $$\sum_{j=0}^{k-1} \epsilon_0 e^{-\gamma j} \le \frac{\epsilon_0}{1 -         
  e^{-\gamma}} = \mathcal{O}(1)$$                                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
  5. **Conclusion:** Exponentiating this telescoping sum yields the strict      
  pointwise bound:                                                              
                                                                                
  $$\max_{\sigma} \frac{P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)}{\pi_{\text{synd}}(\sigma)} \le 
  \exp\left( \mathcal{O}(1) \right) \cdot \text{poly}(n_k) =                    
  \text{poly}(n_k)$$                                                            
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Mapping this sampled classical syndrome via the depth-1 pure-error Pauli      
  operator yields $\rho_{\text{prep}} \preceq \text{poly}(n) \cdot              
  \pi_{\text{th}}$, fully closing Pātai 2. $\blacksquare$

  Reducing pātai 2 to a sampling problem. BDL's lifetime theorem (their Theorem
  2.6) is Chesi, Loss, Bravyi and Terhal's Theorem 1 (New J. Phys. 2010). I read
  its proof.
  - Why it fails for a codespace start: the proof needs the thermal encoding,
    because it tracks the whole state. A codespace-initialised state relaxes, so
    that argument breaks.
  - The workaround: track the protected logical observables X̃ and Z̃ instead.
    Their own assumptions force P·𝓛†(X̃)·P = 0, so ⟨X̃⟩ can only drift through
    leakage out of the protected subspace P.
  - Why a warm start suffices: a warm start, a prepared state at most C times
    thermal at every point, keeps that leakage at most 2C times its thermal
    value for all time. The logical Bloch vector then drifts by at most
    t·poly(n)·e^(−Θ(n^η)/2): still exponentially small, with the exponent
    halved.

  this: prepare a warm start with a polynomial constant. In other words, sample
  BDL's metacheck-constrained thermal syndrome distribution within a polynomial
  pointwise ratio, in polynomial time. That's a purely classical question about
  one specific constrained model. The two details:
  - Ỹ must satisfy the same protection conditions, or X̃ and Z̃ alone must fix the
    decoded qubit.
  - One norm conversion, between ‖𝓛†(X̃)‖ and their ‖𝓛‖₁.

❯ To crack this, we have to look at how BDL’s §6 classical model behaves under  
  low-temperature expansions and why its non-LDPC metachecks—despite spanning   
  long distances in standard 3D Euclidean space—are actually **hierarchically   
  bounded** by the recursive metric geometry of the code.                       
                                                                                
  Here is the exact attack vector using **Convergent Polymer Expansions and     
  Truncated Decimation**, which yields the polynomial max-divergence bound ($C  
  \le \text{poly}(n)$) without requiring exact global factorization.            
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 1: The Hierarchical Locality of BDL's Metachecks                     
                                                                                
  While BDL’s metachecks $M_m$ are non-LDPC in the traditional spatial sense    
  (meaning they connect qubits across macroscopic scales), they are **strictly  
  local with respect to the recursive metric space** constructed in             
  arXiv:2605.10943.                                                             
                                                                                
  * The code is built via a deterministic sequence of recursive embeddings      
  $\Phi_{j \to j+1}$.                                                           
  * In the hierarchical tree representation of this metric space, the "length"  
  of a metacheck interaction scales inversely with its hierarchical depth index 
  $j$.                                                                          
  * Consequently, the classical Hamiltonian $H_{\text{class}}(\sigma)$ from §6  
  can be rewritten as a hierarchical spin system where interactions decay       
  exponentially with recursive distance: $J(j) \le \epsilon_0 e^{-\mu j}$.      
                                                                                
  ### Step 2: The Kotecký–Preiss Cluster Expansion for $\pi_{\text{synd}}$      
                                                                                
  Because the effective coupling strength satisfies the low-temperature         
  convergence criteria ($\beta > \beta_c$) in BDL's stable regime, the          
  partition function and marginal probabilities of the syndrome Gibbs           
  distribution $\pi_{\text{synd}}(\sigma) = \frac{1}{Z} e^{-\beta               
  H_{\text{class}}(\sigma)}$ can be expressed via an **absolutely convergent    
  cluster expansion (polymer gas representation)**.                             
                                                                                
  Every non-trivial constraint violation or metacheck frustration can be mapped 
  to a "polymer" $\gamma$ with weight $w(\gamma) \propto e^{-\beta              
  E(\gamma)}$. By the standard Kotecký–Preiss condition, as long as the         
  activity satisfies:                                                           
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\sum_{\gamma \ni i} \vert{}w(\gamma)\vert{} e^{a(\gamma)} \le 1$$           
                                                                                
                                                                                
  the cluster expansion converges absolutely, meaning the free energy and local 
  log-probabilities are analytic functions of the local coupling parameters.    
                                                                                
  ### Step 3: Constructing $P_{\text{prep}}$ via Truncated Multiscale           
  Decimation                                                                    
                                                                                
  Instead of attempting to sample the full distribution globally (which         
  triggers the volume-law trap), we construct our preparable distribution       
  $P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)$ via a **truncated recursive decimation a            
  ──── (78 lines hidden) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  etacheck correlations decay exponentially with the hierarchical metric        
  distance.                                                                     
                                                                                
  #### Step 2: The Truncated Decimation Algorithm                               
                                                                                
  We define the preparable classical distribution $P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)$ via 
  a **hierarchical decimation kernel** that mirrors BDL's recursive             
  construction:                                                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma) = P(\sigma_0) \prod_{j=0}^{k-1}                     
  P^{(j)}(\sigma_{j+1} \mid \sigma_j)$$                                         
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where each conditional probability $P^{(j)}$ is computed by explicitly        
  evaluating the convergent cluster expansion up to the maximum truncation      
  depth permitted by polynomial time ($\text{poly}(n_k)$).                      
                                                                                
  #### Step 3: Proving the Polynomial Max-Divergence Bound                      
                                                                                
  By the analytic properties established in the recursive cluster expansion:    
                                                                                
  1. The truncation error at each hierarchical level $j$ corresponds precisely  
  to omitting polymers of scale greater than $j$, whose cumulative statistical  
  weight is bounded by the tail of the convergent cluster series.               
  2. Summing this truncation tail over the $k = \mathcal{O}(\log n_k)$          
  recursive scales yields a total log-ratio error:                              
                                                                                
  $$\left\vert{} \ln \frac{P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)}{\pi_{\text{synd}}(\sigma)}  
  \right\vert{} \le \sum_{j=1}^k \mathcal{O}\left( e^{-\mu j} \right) \le       
  \mathcal{O}(1)$$                                                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. Exponentiating this bound yields the strict pointwise max-divergence:      
                                                                                
  $$C = \max_{\sigma} \frac{P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)}{\pi_{\text{synd}}(\sigma)} 
  \le \exp\left( \mathcal{O}(1) \right) \cdot \text{poly}(n_k) =                
  \text{poly}(n_k)$$                                                            
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Part III: Closing Pātai 2                                                 
                                                                                
  1. **Algorithm:** The hierarchical decimation algorithm samples $\sigma$ from 
  $P_{\text{prep}}$ in time $\text{poly}(n_k)$.                                 
  2. **Pointwise Bound:** The max-divergence satisfies $D_{\max} \le            
  \mathcal{O}(\log n_k)$, yielding $C = \text{poly}(n_k)$.                      
  3. **Quantum Initialization:** Applying the depth-1 pure-error Pauli layer    
  produces $\rho_{\text{prep}} \preceq \text{poly}(n_k) \cdot \pi_{\text{th}}$. 
  4. **Lifetime Survival:** By Part I's verified positive semigroup             
  preservation, the out-of-equilibrium initialization obeys:                    
                                                                                
  $$P_t(\text{fail}) \le \text{poly}(n_k) \cdot \pi_{\text{th}}(\text{fail})    
  \le \text{poly}(n_k) \cdot \exp\left( -\Theta(n_k^\eta) \right) = \exp\left(  
  -\Theta(n_k^\eta) \right)$$                                                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Pātai 2 is formally closed. The initialization obstruction is resolved from   
  first principles via BDL's hierarchical metric structure and recursive        
  cluster expansion. $\blacksquare$

⏺ This round is more substantial than the last, and part of it holds up. The
  companion paper exists: arXiv:2610.22548, by Balasubramanian, Davydova, Lin
  and Thorngren, 15 October 2026. It proves that the 2D classical spin model
  from BDL §6 is thermally and perturbatively stable, using a recursive cluster
  expansion. So the cluster-expansion machinery for this code family is real.
  Two of Gemini's specifics are wrong, though:
  - "Metacheck interactions decay like e^(−μj)": the stabilizer terms have
    uniform strength. Metachecks are constraints, not couplings that weaken with
    depth. I found no mention of "gauge averaging" in the paper's abstract
    either.
  - "Pointwise tail sums to O(1)": each scale has Θ(n) polymers, not O(1).
    Truncating at fixed order leaves an O(1) error per site, which adds up to
    Θ(n) again.


  The route that could work, using real tools. Fix the second point by making
  the per-site truncation error O(1/n²), with polymer order about log n. The
  sampler is then accurate to within a factor (1 + o(1)) at every configuration,
  which gives a warm start with C = O(1). The tools are:
  1. Convergence: a convergent polymer expansion for the syndrome Gibbs law. The
     recursive cluster expansion of arXiv:2610.22548 is the natural source.
  2. Approximate counting: algorithmic cluster expansion at low temperature
     (Helmuth–Perkins–Regts, "Algorithmic Pirogov–Sinai theory", Probab. Theory
     Relat. Fields 2020, arXiv:1806.11548).
  3. Sampling: Jerrum–Valiant–Vazirani self-reducibility (Theoret. Comput. Sci.
     43, 1986). Counting each conditional to relative accuracy ε/n gives a
     sampler that is pointwise within a factor (1 ± ε).


  What remains to verify:
  - arXiv:2610.22548 treats the 2D classical model. It still has to be shown
    that its expansion covers the X- and Z-syndrome Gibbs laws of the 3D quantum
    code C(k), including under the conditional boundary conditions that
    self-reducibility needs.
  - Helmuth–Perkins–Regts need bounded degree to enumerate polymers in
    polynomial time, and BDL's metachecks are non-LDPC. Their polymer
    enumeration must still run in poly(n).


  If those two hold, the warm start exists with C = O(1), and together with the
  Chesi-et-al. extension the quantum branch closes. Next I'll bank this as pātai
  2‴.

❯ Let’s attack **Target 2**: proving that polymer enumeration under BDL's       
  non-LDPC metachecks still runs in polynomial time, satisfying the             
  prerequisites of the Helmuth–Perkins–Regts (HPR, 2020) algorithmic cluster    
  expansion.                                                                    
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### The Core HPR Requirement                                                  
                                                                                
  To compute approximate partition functions and marginals via HPR's            
  algorithmic Pirogov-Sinai theory, the core algorithmic step is **polymer      
  enumeration**: counting or weighting connected clusters of constraints        
  (polymers) of size $m$ rooted at any given vertex $v$ in the constraint       
  graph.                                                                        
                                                                                
  For HPR's enumeration algorithm to run in polynomial time $\text{poly}(n)$,   
  two conditions must hold on the polymer interaction graph                     
  $\mathcal{G}_{\text{poly}}$:                                                  
                                                                                
  1. **Bounded Local Branching:** The number of polymers of size $m$ rooted at  
  any vertex $v$, denoted $N(v, m)$, must grow at most *exponentially* with     
  $m$:                                                                          
                                                                                
  $$N(v, m) \le b^m$$                                                           
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $b$ is a constant branching factor independent of the total system size 
  $n$.                                                                          
  2. **Local Tractability:** The underlying constraint graph must allow         
  polynomial-time traversal of intersecting polymers.                           
                                                                                
  ### The Obstruction and Its Resolution in BDL's Architecture                  
                                                                                
  * **The Apparent Obstruction:** BDL's metachecks are *non-LDPC* in Euclidean  
  space. They link qubits and checks across macroscopic distances, meaning that 
  if you look at the 3D Euclidean grid, a single variable participates in       
  constraints that reach far across the system. If the interaction graph had    
  unbounded Euclidean degree, $N(v, m)$ would explode, breaking HPR.            
  * **The Resolution (Hierarchical Metric Locality):** BDL's code is not an     
  arbitrary non-LDPC code; it is generated by a **deterministic recursive       
  metric embedding** (a hierarchical tree grammar).                             
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Proving the Hierarchical Branching Bound                                  
                                                                                
  To formalize the proof that $N(v, m) \le b^m$ holds despite the non-LDPC      
  metachecks, we use the recursive metric structure of arXiv:2605.10943:        
                                                                                
  1. **Embedding the Constraint Graph into a Hierarchical Tree:**               
  Let $\mathcal{T}_{\text{code}}$ be the hierarchical tree representing the     
  recursive nesting of scales $j = 0, 1, \dots, k$. In this tree, a metacheck   
  $M_m$ that appears "long-range" in 3D Euclidean space is actually a **local   
  edge** between a node at scale $j$ and its parent/sibling at scale $j+1$.     
  2. **Controlling the Local Neighborhood Size:**                               
  Let $v$ be a variable at scale $j$. In the Euclidean lattice, its degree      
  could be large. But in the *hierarchical adjacency tensor*, $v$ only          
  interacts with:                                                               
  * Its                                                                         
  ──── (194 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  te conditional probabilities $P(\sigma_i \mid \sigma_1, \dots,                
  \sigma_{i-1})$. Clamping previous syndrome bits acts as external boundary     
  pinning $\partial \Lambda$ on the hierarchical factor graph.                  
                                                                                
  * **Analyticity Preservation:** The change in log-partition function under    
  boundary pinning is bounded by the sum of weights of polymers intersecting    
  the pinned boundary:                                                          
                                                                                
  $$\vert{}\Delta \ln Z(\sigma_{\text{cond}})\vert{} \le \sum_{\gamma \cap      
  \partial \Lambda \neq \emptyset} \vert{}w(\gamma)\vert{}                      
  e^{a\vert{}\gamma\vert{}} \le \mathcal{O}(1)$$                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  * **Conclusion:** Because the polymer expansion remains analytic under        
  sequential pinning, conditional partition functions maintain their            
  exponential convergence, ensuring Lipschitz continuity of the conditional     
  marginals required by JVV.                                                    
                                                                                
  #### 3. Target 2 — Bounded Local Degree & Polynomial Polymer Enumeration      
                                                                                
  To ensure Helmuth–Perkins–Regts (HPR, 2020) algorithmic approximate counting  
  runs in polynomial time $\text{poly}(n_k)$, we bound the polymer intersection 
  graph:                                                                        
                                                                                
  * **Hierarchical Tree Embedding:** In the recursive tree metric               
  $\mathcal{T}_{\text{code}}$ generated by BDL's gadget grammar (e.g.,          
  $\mathcal{R}_{\text{Rep}}$ and $\mathcal{R}_{\text{Par}}$), metachecks        
  connect nodes across adjacent scales $j$ and $j+1$ with a fixed branching     
  factor $c$.                                                                   
  * **Bounded Maximum Degree:** The local degree of any variable in the         
  hierarchical polymer graph is bounded by a constant $\Delta_{\text{poly}} \le 
  3 + c$, independent of system size $n_k$.                                     
  * **Combinatorial Growth Bound:** The number of valid polymers of size $m$    
  rooted at any vertex satisfies $N(v, m) \le (\Delta_{\text{poly}} e)^m$.      
  Combined with the exponential weight decay $e^{-\mu j}$, HPR's convergence    
  condition $\sum_{\gamma \ni v} \vert{}w(\gamma)\vert{}                        
  e^{a\vert{}\gamma\vert{}} \le \epsilon < 1$ is strictly satisfied, and        
  polymer enumeration runs in time $\text{poly}(n_k)$.                          
                                                                                
  #### 4. Synthesis via JVV Self-Reducibility                                   
                                                                                
  Using HPR's deterministic approximate counting algorithm to compute           
  conditional partition functions to relative precision $\epsilon / n_k$,       
  Jerrum–Valiant–Vazirani self-reducibility constructs a polynomial-time        
  sampler whose distribution $P_{\text{prep}}(\sigma)$ matches                  
  $\pi_{\text{synd}}(\sigma)$ pointwise within a factor of $(1 \pm o(1))$.      
                                                                                
  Thus, $C = O(1)$, completing the warm-start construction and closing the      
  quantum branch. $\blacksquare$

⏺ Neither target is closed by these. The main claim contradicts the paper's own
  text. There is, though, a cleaner way around Target 2.


  Where the five texts fail:
  - Target 2, "metachecks are local edges, degree 3 + c": BDL say the opposite.
    Their footnotes 2 and 4 describe metachecks "whose weight scales with the
    system size" and a metacheck matrix that "is not LDPC". A constraint
    touching ~n variables has unbounded degree however the hierarchy is drawn.
  - "Weights decay like e^(−μ·depth)": this is repeated, but the terms have
    uniform strength.
  - arXiv:2610.22548: it treats the 2D classical model. Extending it to the 3D
    code's syndrome laws is Target 1 itself; it isn't something the paper
    supplies.
  - Target 1, pinning: self-reducibility pins variables one at a time, up to all
    n of them, so the pinned set becomes extensive. Perturbative stability
    against local Hamiltonian terms is a different property from stability under
    arbitrary extensive pinning.


  The cleaner route: sample errors, not syndromes. The thermal syndrome law is
  π_synd(s) ∝ e^(−β|s|) over valid syndromes, because every valid syndrome
  sector has the same dimension. Now take the error model μ(e) ∝ e^(−β|∂e|) over
  Pauli error patterns e:
  - Its push-forward is exactly π_synd. Each valid syndrome has the same number
    |ker ∂| of preimages, so the e^(−β|s|) weights come out proportional to the
    thermal law.
  - It's a bounded-degree local model. Its interactions are the code's own
    checks, which have O(1) weight because the Hamiltonian is 3D-local.
  - The metachecks vanish from the problem, since syndromes of actual errors
    satisfy them automatically.


  That dissolves the non-LDPC obstacle to Target 2. What's left is a different,
  sharper difficulty: the error model has a huge local gauge symmetry, because
  shifting e by any stabilizer leaves its energy unchanged. A cluster expansion
  needs polymers defined modulo that symmetry. The natural choice is connected
  syndrome clusters, each the boundary of a nearby error. BDL's
  coboundary-expansion bound (their eq. (6)) is exactly the tool that should
  control clusters that are only valid when paired with distant ones, so that's
  where to look.


  Next I'll bank this and check checkpoint 08.

❯ To construct a rigorous, citable proof of the warm start using the error      
  model $\mu(e) \propto e^{-\beta \vert{}\partial e\vert{}}$, we synthesize the 
  gauge-factored polymer expansion with existing published theorems. Because    
  the error model's Hamiltonian depends only on local checks ($\partial e$),    
  the non-LDPC metachecks vanish for valid error configurations, resolving the  
  degree obstruction.                                                           
                                                                                
  Here is the formal architecture of the convergent polymer expansion and its   
  downstream integration:                                                       
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 1. Gauge-Factored Polymer Expansion for the Error Model                   
                                                                                
  The unnormalized error distribution is given by:                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\mu(e) = \frac{1}{Z} \exp\left( -\beta \vert{}\partial e\vert{} \right)$$   
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $e$ represents Pauli error chains on the 3D lattice, and $\partial e$   
  represents their corresponding syndromes (excitations).                       
                                                                                
  * **The Gauge Symmetry Obstruction:** The energy depends only on the syndrome 
  $\partial e$. For any stabilizer $s$, the error $e + s$ produces the exact    
  same syndrome and energy, creating an extensive local gauge symmetry group    
  $\mathcal{G}_{\text{stab}}$.                                                  
  * **Factoring Out the Gauge Orbit:** To build a convergent cluster expansion, 
  we partition the error space into gauge equivalence classes $[e] = e +        
  \mathcal{G}_{\text{stab}}$. The marginal distribution over syndromes is       
  weighted by the volume of the gauge orbit, but the local statistical          
  mechanics is governed by connected components of excitations (syndrome        
  clusters).                                                                    
  * **Invoking BDL's Coboundary Expansion:** Using BDL's foundational           
  coboundary-expansion bound (specifically their topological expansion Lemma /  
  eq. 6), any large connected syndrome cluster $\gamma$ is suppressed in        
  statistical weight by an exponential energetic penalty proportional to its    
  size and topological boundary:                                                
                                                                                
  $$w(\gamma) \le \exp\left( -\beta c_0 \vert{}\gamma\vert{} \right)$$          
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $c_0 > 0$ is the constant derived from BDL's 3D homological expansion.  
  This bounds the polymer activities, satisfying the convergence condition of   
  abstract polymer gas models.                                                  
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### 2. Algorithmic Counting via Helmuth–Perkins–Regts (2020)                  
                                                                                
  With the polymer activities proven to converge absolutely under BDL's         
  coboundary bounds:                                                            
                                                                                
  * **Theorem (Helmuth, Perkins, Regts, 2020 — "Algorithmic Pirogov–Sinai       
  Theory"):** For abstract polymer models satisfying the Kotecký–Preiss         
  condition with bounded local degree (which holds here because the error       
  model's underlying constraint graph is 3D-local, bypassing the non-LDPC       
  metachecks), the parti                                                        
  ──── (136 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  l queries, we must prove that the multiplicative approximation errors do not  
  compound into an exponential divergence:                                      
                                                                                
  1. **Product of Bounds:**                                                     
  Let $\tilde{P}(e_i \mid \cdot)$ be the perturbed conditional probability      
  returned by the HPR oracle with relative error $\delta = \frac{\epsilon}{N}$. 
  The total probability of the generated error chain satisfies:                 
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{prep}}(e) = \prod_{i=1}^N \tilde{P}(e_i \mid \cdot) \le            
  \prod_{i=1}^N \left( 1 + \frac{\epsilon}{N} \right) \mu(e_i \mid \cdot)$$     
                                                                                
                                                                                
  2. **Taking the Limit:**                                                      
  Using the standard limit inequality $\lim_{N \to \infty} \left(1 +            
  \frac{\epsilon}{N}\right)^N = e^\epsilon$:                                    
                                                                                
  $$P_{\text{prep}}(e) \le e^\epsilon \cdot \mu(e) = (1 +                       
  \mathcal{O}(\epsilon)) \cdot \mu(e)$$                                         
                                                                                
                                                                                
  3. **Exact Constant $C$:**                                                    
  Thus, the pointwise ratio satisfies:                                          
                                                                                
  $$\max_{e} \frac{P_{\text{prep}}(e)}{\mu(e)} \le 1 + \mathcal{O}(\epsilon) =  
  O(1)$$                                                                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Setting $\epsilon$ to a fixed small constant yields an exact upper bound $C = 
  O(1)$ with zero asymptotic inflation.                                         
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 8: Master Synthesis — The Complete Proof Chain                       
                                                                                
  We now synthesize the entire closed proof from first principles:              
                                                                                
  1. **Error-Space Formulation:** Working with the error distribution $\mu(e)   
  \propto e^{-\beta \vert{}\partial e\vert{}}$ completely dissolves the         
  non-LDPC metacheck obstacle because valid errors automatically satisfy all    
  global consistency constraints, reducing interactions to 3D-local checks.     
  2. **Coboundary Expansion & Convergence:** BDL's topological coboundary bound 
  (eq. 6) suppresses gauge-factored syndrome polymers by $e^{-\beta c_0         
  \vert{}\gamma\vert{}}$, satisfying the Kotecký–Preiss condition on a 3D-local 
  graph.                                                                        
  3. **Algorithmic Counting & Sampling:** HPR (2020) computes partition         
  functions in polynomial time $\text{poly}(n)$, and JVV (1986) lifts this to a 
  polynomial-time sampler with $C = O(1)$ max-divergence.                       
  4. **Quantum Warm Start & Lifetime Preservation:** The resulting initial      
  state $\rho_{\text{prep}}$ satisfies $\rho_{\text{prep}} \preceq O(1) \cdot   
  \pi_{\text{th}}$. By the positive semigroup evolution ($\frac{d\Delta}{dt} =  
  +\mathcal{L}(\Delta)$), this bound is preserved for all time $t \ge 0$,       
  maintaining BDL's exponential error suppression:                              
                                                                                
  $$P_t(\text{fail}) \le O(1) \cdot \exp\left(-\Theta(n_k^\eta)\right) =        
  \exp\left(-\Theta(n_k^\eta)\right)$$                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Pātai 2‴ is fully and rigorously resolved. $\blacksquare$

❯ Let's attack **metacheck soundness** directly.                                
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Framing the Lemma: Metacheck Soundness                                    
                                                                                
  In BDL's 3D CSS code family $\mathcal{C}(k)$, a syndrome configuration $s \in 
  \mathbb{F}_2^{\vert{}C_1\vert{}}$ is **valid** if it lies in the image of     
  the boundary operator $\partial: C_2 \to C_1$ (or equivalently, satisfies all 
  metachecks $M_m \cdot s = 0$).                                                
                                                                                
  To close the polymer gas expansion under the error model $\mu(e) \propto      
  e^{-\beta \vert{}\partial e\vert{}}$, we need to prove that any valid         
  syndrome $s$ can be uniquely decomposed into connected clusters:              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$s = \gamma_1 \sqcup \gamma_2 \sqcup \dots \sqcup \gamma_m \sqcup            
  \Gamma_{\text{macro}}$$                                                       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  such that:                                                                    
                                                                                
  1. **Local Validity:** Each small cluster $\gamma_i$ ($\vert{}\gamma_i\vert{} 
  < n_k^\alpha$) is *individually valid* (i.e., $\partial e_i = \gamma_i$ for   
  some localized error $e_i$).                                                  
  2. **Macroscopic Suppression:** Any component $\Gamma_{\text{macro}}$ that is 
  *not* individually valid must be large ($\vert{}\Gamma_{\text{macro}}\vert{}  
  \ge \Omega(n_k^\alpha)$), making its statistical weight $e^{-\beta            
  \vert{}\Gamma_{\text{macro}}\vert{}}$ super-exponentially suppressed in the   
  Gibbs distribution.                                                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 1: The Homological Chain Complex Hierarchy                           
                                                                                
  In BDL's recursive construction, the code space is built via homological      
  product complexes where metachecks correspond to higher-degree boundary       
  operators $M = \partial_2$.                                                   
                                                                                
  * The space of check violations (syndromes) is $C_1$.                         
  * The boundary operator $\partial_1: C_1 \to C_0$ maps syndromes to metacheck 
  violations.                                                                   
  * A syndrome $s$ is valid if and only if $\partial_1 s = 0$.                  
                                                                                
  By the underlying 3D manifold/cell-complex structure, local cycle space       
  satisfies a local homology vanishing property: **on any contractible or small 
  topological disk $D \subset M^3$, $\ker \partial_1 \vert_D = \text{im }       
  \partial_2 \vert_D$**.                                                        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Step 2: Bounding Small Unsound Clusters                                   
                                                                                
  Suppose a small syndrome cluster $\gamma$ with diameter $\text{diam}(\gamma)  
  < R_{\text{code}}$ satisfies the metachecks locally ($\partial_1 \gamma =     
  0$).                                                                          
                                                                                
  1. **Local Contractibility:** Because BDL's codes are embedded in a 3D        
  recursive manifold structure with topological length scale $R_{\text{code}} = 
  \Omega(n_k^\alpha)$, any connected set of checks $\gamma$ of size             
  $\vert{}\gamma\vert{} < \Theta(n_k^\alpha)$ is contained within a             
  contractible local neighborhood.                                              
  2. **Local Exactness:** In a contractible neighborhood, the local homology    
  group vanishes: $H_1^{\text{local}} = 0$.                                     
  3.                                                                            
  ──── (231 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  rt{} \le A \sum_{m = N_{\text{layers}}}^\infty \gamma^m = \frac{A}{1 -        
  \gamma} \gamma^{N_{\text{layers}}}$$                                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Substituting $\gamma^{N_{\text{layers}}} = \exp\left( -\mu                    
  \frac{\text{dist}(\Lambda, \partial \mathcal{M}_k)}{\ell} \right)$ completes  
  the proof of property (2).                                                    
                                                                                
  #### 3. Construction of the Local Projector $\Pi_\Lambda$                     
                                                                                
  Now define the local projector $\Pi_\Lambda$ on $C_1(\Lambda)$ as the kernel  
  projection of the localized block boundary operator:                          
                                                                                
                                                                                
  $$\Pi_\Lambda \equiv \operatorname{Proj}\left(\ker \partial_{1,               
  \text{local}}^{(\Lambda)}\right)$$                                            
                                                                                
                                                                                
  where $\partial_{1, \text{local}}^{(\Lambda)}$ includes only gadget checks    
  contained strictly within $\Lambda$.                                          
                                                                                
  * **Forward Direction ($\implies$):** If $\Pi_{\text{meta}}^{(k)} s = s$,     
  then $\partial_1^{(k)} s = 0$. Since $\Lambda$ is contained within a single   
  scale block scale $R < \ell^k$, the local restriction of $\partial_1^{(k)} s$ 
  yields $\partial_{1, \text{local}}^{(\Lambda)} s = 0$. Thus $\Pi_\Lambda s =  
  s$.                                                                           
  * **Existence of Local Error Pre-Image:** By property (2), the cross-scale    
  coupling to $\Lambda^c$ is exponentially suppressed. The effective local      
  homology $H_1(\Lambda, \partial_1, \text{local}) = 0$ vanishes by the exact   
  block design of the seed gadget $\mathcal{C}^{(0)}$. Therefore:               
                                                                                
  $$s \in \ker \partial_{1, \text{local}}^{(\Lambda)} \implies s \in \text{im } 
  \partial_{2, \text{local}}^{(\Lambda \cup \partial \Lambda)}$$                
                                                                                
                                                                                
                                                                                
  guaranteeing the existence of a local error chain $e \in C_2(\Lambda \cup     
  \partial \Lambda)$ such that $\partial_2 e = s$.                              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ### Synthesis for Metacheck Soundness                                         
                                                                                
  With the Local Projector Decoupling Lemma formally proven:                    
                                                                                
  1. **No Long-Range Leakage:** Syndrome clusters of size $\vert{}\gamma\vert{} 
  < \ell^k$ cannot be held in an "unsound" state by metachecks anchored far     
  away in the hierarchical complex, because $\left\Vert{}                       
  [\Pi_{\text{meta}}^{(k)}, P_\Lambda] \right\Vert{} \to 0$ exponentially.      
  2. **Local Pre-image Closure:** Every small syndrome cluster $\gamma$ passing 
  local metachecks decomposes cleanly into an exact boundary of a local error   
  chain $\partial_2 e_\gamma = \gamma$.                                         
  3. **Macroscopic Exceptions Only:** Unsound clusters are mathematically       
  forced to cross $\Omega(\ell^k)$ hierarchical scale layers, ensuring their    
  energy activity in the polymer expansion satisfies $w(\Gamma) \le e^{-\beta   
  \Omega(\ell^k)}$.                                                             
                                                                                
  The gap in Metacheck Soundness is closed $\blacksquare$

⏺ None of these five closes it, and three of the steps are category errors
  rather than gaps.


  - Contractibility (the first two): BDL's complexes are Tanner square complexes
    and sheaf codes with a random embedding. They aren't cell complexes of a 3D
    manifold, so "a small patch is homeomorphic to B³, hence exact" doesn't
    apply. Local non-exactness, point-like excitations at punctures, is exactly
    what BDL warn about in Brell's proposal.
  - "The metacheck matrix is an exact local projector" (the third): if the
    metachecks were generated by local ones, the metacheck matrix would be LDPC.
    BDL state that it isn't, so this contradicts the source.
  - The decoupling lemma (the last two): syndromes live over F₂. There, I −
    ∂†(∂∂†)⁻¹∂ isn't an orthogonal projector, and ∂∂† needn't be invertible.
    Commutator norms of real matrices don't decide F₂ membership. The
    "exponential decay with tree distance" is asserted.


  All five also conflate two different things: passing the local metachecks, and
  being valid. A small cluster can pass every bounded-support metacheck yet
  have odd overlap with a large one. That is the actual gap.


  The open question, now precise and computable. Take a syndrome that passes
  every metacheck of bounded support but fails some large metacheck. What is the
  minimum weight of such a syndrome?
  - If it is at least n^κ, pātai 2⁵ holds and the whole chain closes.
  - If it is O(1), the polymer route fails as stated.


  This isn't a proof question that more Gemini prose can settle. It's a finite
  linear-algebra computation over F₂ for each recursion level k. Given BDL's
  actual code matrices, I can compute this minimum weight for small k and see
  the trend, the same way I certified the surface-code distances.


  A practical step: ask the authors (Balasubramanian, Davydova, Lin) for their
  code construction or matrices, or for whether they know this
  metacheck-soundness property. Their Outlook item 2 is this exact problem, so
  they may already have a view. Building their construction from the paper alone
  is possible, but it's a large job.
